## Global UI priority — MANDATORY RULE

Any work that creates, modifies, fixes, reviews, or tests UI must be executed strictly in the following priority order:

1. **Mobile/phone — first priority; iPhone is the primary reference and first acceptance target.** Mobile compositions, safe areas/GuiInset, notch/Dynamic Island, short landscape height, touch-only interaction, readability, and accessible touch targets are first designed. Android phones are included in the same first class and must not receive desktop hover assumptions. For the current regression baseline, the iPhone 13 is first verified; the standard game orientation remains landscape, and portrait is checked as a defensive fallback where supported.
2. **Tablet — second in line.** After a stable phone UI, a standalone tablet composition is defined with its own dimensions, density, and safe area; tablet should not be considered either a "larger phone" or a shrunk desktop.
3. **Desktop/PC — only third in line.** Desktop receives a full adaptation only after mobile and tablet. Hover, mouse precision, wide canvas, and additional density are allowed only in the desktop branch and cannot dictate the base geometry of phone or tablet.

Consequences of the rule:

- desktop-first approach "first build a large PC panel, then just scale it down" is prohibited;
- the overall state/data contract must be shared, but layout functions must explicitly and idempotently set properties for each device profile, without inheriting stale geometry after resize/rotation;
- plan, implementation review, screenshots, and acceptance matrix for UI are always listed and executed in the order **iPhone/mobile → tablet → desktop**;
- if conflicts arise between profiles and the user does not provide a more narrow surface-specific instruction, mobile/iPhone usability wins; desktop convenience is not a reason to allow mobile overlap, clipped text, or inaccessible controls;
- a UI task is not considered complete based on a single desktop screenshot. Minimal acceptance begins with the target iPhone viewport and touch path, then proceeds to tablet, and only after that to PC.

This rule has higher priority than previous desktop-first dimensions and assumptions in historical change-set summaries. Exceptions are possible only with direct user instruction for a specific surface; such exceptions do not negate the global rule for other UI components.

**One-pass deploy candidate / 2× SOON 3.56 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** the welcome-back modal no longer promotes a non-existent purchase for <code>49 R$</code>. The secondary button displays only <code>2× · SOON</code> on phone and <code>2× EARNINGS · SOON</code> on tablet/desktop, has <code>Active=false</code>, <code>Selectable=false</code>, does not bind purchase/click callback and does not contain <code>PromptProductPurchase</code>. A studio-wide scan confirms the absence of <code>PromptProductPurchase</code> and <code>ProcessReceipt</code>; the only monetization asset in this version is the permanent Game Pass <code>Custom FX Pass</code>, ID <code>1969981456</code>, live Marketplace state <code>IsForSale=true</code>, <code>PriceInRobux=129</code>. A synthetic iPhone welcome-back runtime showed disabled 140×56 <code>2× · SOON</code>; the modified LocalScript compiles and Play did not report new errors. The known warning <code>TycoonMandatoryProgression_v1 98 vs TycoonConfig 99 pads</code> remains an analytics debt, and the physical iPhone/manual 10-cycle soak from the release checklist is still uncompleted; thus, the build is a technical deploy candidate but not formally passed release acceptance. Device Simulator is reset to default, Studio remains in Edit mode; publish was not performed.

**2× Offline Earnings transparent icon variants 3.55 — ASSET-ONLY / SELECTION PENDING:** three independent square PNG compositions are preserved as <code>artifacts/monetization/2x-offline-earnings-transparent-v1.png</code>, <code>...-v2.png</code>, and <code>...-v3.png</code>. All have a real 32-bit alpha channel with fully transparent corners, a safe margin, a single readable <code>2X</code>, and maintain the Sell Cars language: cream lettering, neon-green cash, gold time symbol, heavy black outline, and restrained cyan/magenta accents. V1 — moon-clock + cash base; V2 — circular-arrow clock + flying notes; V3 — the cleanest symmetric moon-clock + double cash base. The old black-background master is not replaced until the owner selects a variant; Creator Dashboard upload was not performed.

**Passenger seating / exit + negotiation touch recovery 3.54 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** passenger prompt is no longer limited to immediate <code>Seat:Sit()</code>. The server resets velocity, positions the avatar to authored <code>PassengerSeat</code>, waits for Heartbeat, and confirms the double invariant <code>Seat.Occupant == Humanoid</code> and <code>Humanoid.SeatPart == PassengerSeat</code> up to three times; if safe exit fails, it safely returns the prompt. A new global <code>PersonalPassengerClient</code> recognizes only <code>PassengerSeat</code> within <code>PersonalVehicle</code>, moves the default camera at least 7 studs away, displays a safe-area 60×60 Exit on touch, and provides <code>E</code>/<code>ButtonB</code> on desktop/gamepad. The exit uses the existing collision-safe <code>CarExitHandler</code> and restores original zoom limits; race exit remains prohibited. The Synthetic iPhone passenger seat confirmed the actual <code>SeatPart</code>, visible Exit, and camera distance; the keyboard <code>E</code> removed the SeatPart and deleted the passenger UI. In nearby buyer dialogue, the transparent fullscreen <code>InputBlocker</code> is no longer <code>Modal</code>/<code>Active</code>; Close and all decision buttons have <code>Activated</code>, <code>MouseButton1Click</code>, direct <code>InputBegan</code> and a general touch hit-test with 150 ms deduping. A separate 36–40 px <code>×</code> can always synchronously send refuse/cancel and restore controls, <code>CameraType=Custom</code>, <code>AutoRotate=true</code> and UI mode <code>World</code>. The Synthetic world-buyer lifecycle confirmed this recovery; Play loaded all modified server/client scripts without new errors. Publish was not performed.

**Customize auto-return 3.53 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** activation of the physical <code>CUSTOMIZE</code> terminal now server-authoritatively verifies the owner, tutorial, distance, and client readiness; after that, if the active personal car is outside the garage, it triggers the canonical <code>spawnActiveCar</code> and respawns it in the authored garage bay. A one-time Customize ticket is issued only after confirmed <code>activeCarIsParked</code>; entry is blocked and preview is opened; auto-return is disabled during <code>RacingActive</code>. If the car is already parked, an extra respawn does not occur. This supersedes the old requirement "first return/park yourself."

**Racing lock-state refresh on Phone open 3.48 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** stale eligibility screen in the app <code>RACING</code> has been fixed. Previously <code>RacingClient</code> stored the startup response <code>COMPLETE THE TUTORIAL TO UNLOCK RACING.</code>, and the action <code>open_phone</code> returned only a session token and did not recalculate the authoritative profile; thus, players who had completed the tutorial continued to see the old reason until rejoin or final <code>RacingUnlocked</code>. Now every <code>open_phone</code> sequentially returns a token and immediately calls <code>requestState</code>/<code>eligibility</code>, so the message in the same Phone-open follows the canonical chain <code>tutorial → starter personal car → continuous 5-second first drive → unlocked</code>. Studio <code>loadstring</code> for <code>RacingService</code> has been passed; the live sentinel <code>STALE LOCK COPY</code> was replaced with a server response on each open. Acceptance was performed in order: iPhone 17 Pro landscape (<code>750×362</code>) → iPad Pro M5 13-inch landscape (<code>1376×1032</code>) → desktop (<code>1570×772</code>); the panel did not receive new clipping/overlap, no new client/server script errors. Device Simulator was reset to <code>default</code>, Studio is returned to Edit; publish was not performed.

**Mobile radio NO RADIO cycle 3.45 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** the mobile yellow-black radio icon no longer triggers a general infinite <code>ChangeStation</code>; instead, it sends a separate server-authoritative action <code>CycleMobileStation</code>. Forward order is <code>DISTRICT BLOOM FM → RATTLESNAKE → NIGHTTRIP → GOLDEN MILE → NO RADIO → DISTRICT BLOOM FM</code>. <code>NO RADIO</code> is a power-off pseudo-state, not the fifth streaming timeline: the server retains the last station ID, sets <code>RadioEnabled=false</code> and <code>RadioAutoStopped=false</code>, so cabin audio fades in properly 0; the next mobile tap turns on the first station and updates the session station. Off-card displays <code>NO RADIO</code> and on touch — <code>TAP RADIO TO START AGAIN</code>; desktop retains <code>PRESS R TO TURN IT ON</code>, keyboard/mouse keys <code>R / [ / ]</code> and four-station wrap without mandatory off-step. Relevant client/server errors <code>0/0</code>, Device Simulator reset to <code>default</code>, Studio was left in Edit mode; publish was not performed.

**Race seat-lock / seated-start handoff / countdown anchor anti-exploit 3.44 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** During countdown and active race, a participant no longer has voluntary on-foot state: before boarding, the server forcibly removes the old <code>SeatWeld</code> personal vehicle, transitions the Humanoid through <code>GettingUp</code>, and then in two Heartbeat steps locks the race seat, moves the character, and triggers <code>VehicleSeat:Sit()</code>. This sequence closes the Roblox frame in which <code>Humanoid.SeatPart</code> is still stale, but <code>VehicleSeat.Occupant</code> is already nil, and immediate <code>Sit()</code> is ignored. <code>RacingService</code> holds the seat lock and continuously maintains physical details anchored with zero velocities throughout the entire <code>10…1</code>; generic <code>RuntimePackage.Server.Occupant</code> no longer unanchors <code>RacingVehicle</code> upon reboarding. The race-car <code>DrivePrompt</code> is disabled; touch, keyboard/mouse, and gamepad clients hide Exit and do not bind exit action, while the general <code>CarExitHandler</code> separately rejects exit during <code>RacingVehicle</code> or <code>RacingActive</code>. If the seat is lost due to external interference, the server immediately returns the live racer to their DriverSeat; death/reset remain terminal DNF and standard release after finish is not blocked. Real iPhone 13 landscape play from an occupied <code>Personal_Bavora.DriverSeat</code> confirmed transition directly into <code>ActiveRace…Racer_01…DriverSeat</code>, empty personal seat, countdown <code>10</code>, <code>39/39</code> anchored parts before and after forced exit attempt, restored race seat, disabled DrivePrompt, and hidden touch Exit. During racing, a retry exit attempt also restored boarding; the physical car was unanchored, except for the permissible service proxy. Relevant client errors <code>0</code>, server errors <code>0</code>; Device Simulator reset to <code>default</code>, Studio was left in Edit mode; publish was not performed.

**Personal Garage layout recovery / sale-only metadata / front-garage teleport 3.43 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** <code>pressable</code> never overwrites layout properties that belong to the player <code>AnchorPoint</code> and <code>Position</code>; only four phone home tiles are explicitly centered and receive <code>UIScale</code>; all other controls use safe color/stroke feedback. This rule supersedes the blanket anchor conversion from V3.42. The phone panel is calculated from <code>Shade.AbsoluteSize</code> and in layer <code>IgnoreGuiInset=true</code> does not add <code>GuiInset</code> a second time — it is precisely the double inset that clipped the bottom border and footer. On iPhone 13 landscape, the panel is accepted as <code>494×290</code>, slightly lowered relative to the geometric center, maintains <code>6.4 px</code> between ACCELERATION and actions, has the same height <code>32 px</code> for single and split action buttons, and has a consistent <code>10 px</code> bottom margin for the footer; the footer buttons themselves did not shrink. The yellow owned-card line <code>IN GARAGE • BAY … • UPGRADES …</code> is hidden: garage bay, car value, upgrades included, total payout/charge, and buyback are shown only within server-quoted sale/replacement confirmation; the phone label is shortened to <code>SELL</code>. <code>TO GARAGE</code> exists only in Personal Garage, async response is bound to the original <code>viewMode</code>, on success the modal closes and stale status does not appear in Personal Car Market. The server places the player on the ground in <code>17 studs</code> before garage spawn and faces them toward the garage. Acceptance was completed strictly on iPhone 13 (<code>749×368</code>) → iPad Pro M5 13-inch (<code>1375×1031</code>) → Average Laptop desktop (<code>1365×768</code>); on all profiles, the panel and footer are fully within viewport, card metadata is hidden, and the market does not contain garage actions. Runtime teleport confirmed offset/facing, sale review — garage bay/value/upgrades/total/buyback; the simulator profile was discarded, no new PersonalGarage errors occurred, Studio was completed in Edit; publish was not performed.

**Race timing / medals / phone interactions / rich results 3.42 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** the server records personal finish time relative to real <code>startedAt</code> and passes <code>finishTimeSeconds</code> into the final package; the client displays it as <code>MM:SS.mmm</code>. Tapping the top money panel or its arrow opens a compact <code>STATS &amp; MEDALS</code>: current money, number of actually purchased non-starter enabled pads, number of wins, and accumulated gold/silver/bronze results. The source is the updated server attributes <code>PurchasedPads</code>, <code>RaceWins</code>, <code>RaceFirstPlaces</code>, <code>RaceSecondPlaces</code>, <code>RaceThirdPlaces</code>, and <code>RaceFinishes</code>; a separate save schema was not added. Medals are built natively with no bitmap background or gradients: a circle with a thick black outline, digit <code>1/2/3</code>, filled with gold/silver/bronze. The final race list became a scrollable row layout: positions 1–3 use these medals, starting from 4-th, a standard ordinal is shown, DNF is highlighted in red, nicknames receive calm, distinguishable colors, and time is moved to the right in a separate blue color. Garage, Buy Cars, Racing, Custom FX, and actions within corresponding phone pages use the same hover/press via <code>UIScale</code>; the historical blanket anchor conversion from this increment is canceled in V3.43, as layout-owned anchors/positions cannot be changed via a general interaction helper. Runtime acceptance confirmed the unchanged center of Garage/Racing/Custom FX during hover <code>1.04</code>, symmetric phone desktop grid, updated stats attributes, and full synthetic server→client results payload with gold/silver/bronze, <code>4TH</code>, DNF, and various times. Panel was checked in compact phone-like and desktop sizes; publish was not performed.

**Race recovery / traffic / route preview / cabin radio 3.41 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** an active racer no longer receives local ambient traffic: <code>TrafficClient</code> tracks only the player's own attribute <code>RacingActive</code>, immediately removes their local traffic cars, and upon exiting the race re-populates the roads; thus, non-participating players continue to see normal traffic. The route simultaneously displays up to three upcoming arches: the current cyan/neon one with a label, two subsequent gray, semi-transparent, and labelless arches; the set only updates when the next checkpoint changes, not on every position packet. Exiting the race car and exiting the route corridor replace place/lap/checkpoint within the compact race HUD with <code>RETURN TO YOUR CAR</code> / <code>RETURN TO THE TRACK OR YOUR RACE WILL END</code>. Recovery received a clear <code>recoveringUntil</code>, preventing re-reset until the first boarding completes; the vehicle ground-aligns to the last safe checkpoint, the player immediately re-boards, and after a five-second penalty, all physical parts are forcibly unanchored (a service <code>RoadHeadlightProxy</code> may remain anchored during runtime). The Outer Ring removed the redundant former checkpoint 17, which was only 90.5 studs away from the next one; the old marker is preserved recoverably in <code>ServerStorage.RacingCheckpointBackups.Outer_17_Extra_V341</code>, and the active sequence is renumbered in <code>1…26</code>. <code>MusicController</code> checks every 0.25 s against the real <code>Humanoid.SeatPart</code>, so a missed boarding event no longer leaves the race car in the outer muffled channel or with a false <code>Radio Off</code>. Admin solo Outer Play confirmed <code>1/26</code>, three arches (cyan + 2 gray), <code>RaceSuppressed=true / 0</code> traffic cars, cabin volume <code>0.45</code>, HUD warnings, re-boarding, ground-level car, and absence of anchored physical parts after penalty; after DNF, the personal <code>Personal_Porschka911</code> is restored, the HUD is hidden, traffic returns (<code>14</code> cars). Phone-first static runtime profile <code>874×402 touch</code> resolved as <code>PhoneLandscape</code> with HUD <code>250×42</code>, then real desktop <code>1570×772</code> went through the same lifecycle. No new Racing/Music/Traffic errors, Studio completed in Edit; publish not performed.

**Race countdown/HUD regression fix 3.40 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** after moving the voluntary exit from race HUD to a dynamic line in Settings, the client-side <code>RacingClient.render</code> retained two references to a now-deleted variable <code>leaveRaceButton</code>. On the first <code>countdown</code> packet, <code>hud.Visible</code> would activate, then a nil-index exception would interrupt render before assignment of <code>hudText</code> and <code>countdown.Text</code>; resulting in a visible black frame with no digits or status. Both stale references have been removed, and the separate HUD button is no longer returned. Simultaneously, actions <code>join/decline</code> from the upper YES/NO invite now pass the current race ID and go through the server stale-ID guard. Real admin solo Main Track Play first on iPhone 17 Pro landscape (<code>749×361</code>), then on Average Laptop/PC confirmed simultaneously visible <code>9</code>, <code>1/1 PLACE • LAP 1/1</code> and <code>CHECKPOINT 1/24</code>; after <code>GO!</code> countdown, the HUD properly hides, and the filled HUD remains. <code>RacingClient</code> compile OK, stale <code>leaveRaceButton</code> references <code>0</code>, Device Simulator reset to <code>default</code>, Studio completed in Edit; publish not performed.

**Race safety lifecycle 3.39 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** A standard race requires a minimum of two participants; only <code>AdminAccess.isDebugAuthorized</code> may initiate solo-debug, and such a run does not generate money, medals, or race-stat rewards. The Grid is shuffled server-side before each start, the vehicle remains anchored throughout the entire count <code>10…1</code>, and the exact position is determined by server time at the arch crossing without shared ties. For Drag, <code>15 s</code> applies without progress / <code>45 s</code> hard limit / <code>30 s</code> finish grace; for Main — <code>45 s</code> without progress, <code>180/300/420 s</code> on <code>1/2/3</code> lap and <code>60 s</code> grace; for Outer — <code>60 s</code>, <code>240/420/600 s</code> and <code>90 s</code>. The first no-progress timeout returns the vehicle to the last safe checkpoint and physically locks the car at <code>5 s</code>; the second timeout results in DNF. Exiting the route corridor first shows a warning, then after <code>4 s</code> it returns to the last checkpoint; after two route/flip recoveries, the next violation results in DNF. Death/reset or leaving the server results in DNF without resume/rejoin; voluntary exit from the vehicle, described in this historical increment, is superseded by V3.44. The historical Settings row <code>HOLD TO LEAVE</code> is superseded by V3.44: during countdown and racing, voluntary exit from the race car or the race itself is unavailable; a separate HUD button is also prohibited. The finisher immediately receives a terminal result, and their race car is locked; after <code>DONE</code> or DNF, the player is moved left off the racing line, the temporary vehicle is deleted, and the active Personal Garage car is restored nearby and takes the player into the DriverSeat. RaceVehicles are ghosted relative to each other and to spectators, while terminal racers remain ghosted until the end of the match. If no opponent has passed the checkpoint 1, the result becomes <code>NO CONTEST</code> without rewards; otherwise, the last remaining participant must finish by the grace deadline. Reward abuse is limited to a two-minute per-player paid-race cooldown and decaying composition multipliers <code>1 / 0.5 / 0.25 / 0</code> within a ten-minute window; hard/finish timers and reward multipliers depend on the track and laps. The server additionally rejects stale race/lobby IDs, impossible teleportations, unready/dead players, and spawn failures; three consecutive update exceptions result in an emergency match termination with cleanup. MCP Play confirmed admin solo eligibility without rewards, countdown <code>10/9/8</code>, anchored race car, route reset to a safe checkpoint, five-second lock, DNF/cleanup on <code>leave_race</code>, removal of the race car, and restoration of <code>Personal_Porschka911</code> nearby to the player. The Settings row passed acceptance in the order iPhone 17 Pro landscape (<code>749×361</code>) → iPad Pro M5 13-inch (<code>1375×1031</code>) → desktop (<code>1570×772</code>), without HUD overlap. Four affected scripts compile; Device Simulator is returned to <code>default</code>, Studio is returned to Edit; publish was not performed.

**Custom FX acquisition surfaces / entitlement lifetime 3.38 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; GAME PASS ACTIVE:** <code>Custom FX Pass</code> remains a permanent account-wide Game Pass, not a per-vehicle license. Selling, swapping, or removing a personal car deletes only its per-car customization state and never removes the entitlement: on any subsequent personal car, all six premium FX are again available for <code>$0</code>. The appearance of the old car is intentionally not automatically carried over — the new car appears in stock state, after which the owner can freely apply the desired FX themselves. In the authored phone desktop, a fourth panel <code>CUSTOM FX</code> is added in the existing visual language (thick black stroke, rounded tile, Gotham Black, rainbow <code>FX</code> glyph) and a separate mobile-first package page with six benefits, lifetime copy, and a single CTA. On each player-owned <code>JunkSpot</code> a separate rainbow non-progression pad <code>CustomFxOfferPad</code> is created: it does not appear in <code>TycoonConfig.PADS</code>, does not modify chapter/pad counts, and for a regular player is visible only to the owner of a completed tutorial tycoon who has not yet acquired the pass. After receiving the entitlement, the pad, glow, and Billboard are fully hidden locally, and the server touch-handler additionally prevents re-opening the offer. A narrow superseding QA exception V3.49 keeps its pad visible to the user from <code>AdminAccess.isAuthorized</code> even during entitlement bypass or an incomplete tutorial and allows opening the owned package page without re-purchasing. For non-owners, the Billboard is limited to <code>MaxDistance=58</code> and size <code>210×88</code>; upon touch, it opens the same phone package page but does not show a native purchase prompt. The pad and phone page display the price with a flat official Robux glyph <code>rbxasset://textures/ui/RobuxIcon.png</code> next to the number <code>129</code>; the glyph is not a standalone action, and the CTA is explicitly labeled <code>UNLOCK</code>. The Gold Robux glyph is prohibited for product-price surfaces. Attribution uses the same server-side funnel events with feature dimensions <code>bundle_phone</code> and <code>bundle_pad</code>. Acceptance order remains mandatory: iPhone/mobile → tablet → desktop. Production ID is set: <code>CUSTOM_FX_GAMEPASS_ID=1969981456</code>; until the current Studio change set is published, production servers still use the previously published build.

**Custom FX Pass monetization MVP 3.37 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; GAME PASS ACTIVE:** The first paid customization MVP is consolidated into a single account-wide permanent Game Pass <code>Custom FX Pass</code> with a target price <code>129 Robux</code>. **Ten-second countdown / synchronous phone-blur cleanup / route-tangent checkpoint arches 3.34 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** The race start countdown has been changed from <code>3…1</code> to <code>10…1</code>. **Phone-only race creation guard 3.32 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** A new race can be created only from the app <code>RACING</code> inside the phone — regardless of which of the eight tycoons the player is on. **Seated personal-car prompt suppression / no hot-swap 3.30 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES V3.29 ACTION LABEL:** Server V3.29 already rejected passenger entry when a <code>Humanoid.SeatPart</code> was occupied, but the client guard only hid the owner <code>DrivePrompt</code> when specifically in a <code>VehicleSeat</code>. Therefore, the driver saw a shared passenger prompt overlaying <code>Exit</code>, while the owner in <code>PassengerSeat</code> could see a free <code>DrivePrompt</code>. <code>PersonalVehicleOccupancyClient</code> now on each client determines any current Seat within <code>PersonalVehicle</code>, locally hiding both <code>DrivePrompt</code> and <code>PassengerPrompt</code> only for the seated player and safely restoring each prompt after actual exit with rechecked ownership/runtime/empty-seat/modal gates. **Overturned vehicle recovery 3.28 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; PRESERVES ZENTAURI ZERO ANTI-ROLL:** inability to recover a flipped <code>ZentauriR</code> was not a direct result of zero anti-roll stiffness, but a race between two shared central-runtime systems. After loss of occupant, the empty slow vehicle anchored itself approximately <code>1.2 s</code> after <code>Parking</code>, while <code>Redresser</code> only enabled <code>AlignOrientation</code> after <code>2 s</code>; the constraint became enabled, but could not rotate the already anchored assembly. <code>Parking</code> no longer anchors chassis with <code>UpVector·Y&lt;0.7</code>, and <code>Redresser</code> defensively removes Anchored from all parts during recovery. Flipped threshold expanded from <code>−0.5</code> to <code>0.25</code>, to lift a vehicle resting on its side, and a <code>6 s</code> timeout was added instead of infinite waiting for a failed recovery. Exact central-runtime Play on an empty Zentauri confirmed automatic recovery for original roll <code>90°/100°/110°/120°/130°/140°/150°/180°</code> within <code>2.45–3.62 s</code> with subsequent standard parking; upright control remains anchored behind <code>1.27 s</code>. Zentauri anti-roll remains <code>0/0</code>; a separate high-speed test on <code>186.5 mph</code> produced a symmetric <code>+6.21°/−6.39° yaw</code>, maximum <code>2.78° roll</code>. QA removed, filtered runtime warnings/errors absent, cash/purchases/saves/DataStore/publish unchanged, Studio returned to Edit.

**Fierro Rossa steering geometry 3.27 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES FIERRO ACCEPTANCE IN 3.16:** previous high-speed result <code>0.24° yaw</code> at <code>0.35 s</code> was not a sign of stability, but confirmation of nearly absent steering. On <code>ServerStorage.CarModels.FierroRossa</code> both steering links had <code>Length=2.14884</code> at actual attachment distance <code>1.82100</code>; excessive length bent the front knuckles in opposite directions by approximately <code>±9.09°</code> at zero input, and the wheels mutually canceled lateral forces. Disabling the single body collider and increasing steering rack force/responsiveness did not resolve the defect, and the rack itself precisely reached the target, thus eliminating the invisible-collider and weak-rack hypotheses. Lengths of both <code>RodConstraint</code> have been corrected to canonical attachment distance <code>1.82100</code>; the template is marked <code>SteeringGeometryVersion=FierroToeFix_2026_09_03</code>. Exact saved-template MCP Play resulted in zero toe <code>0.00°/0.00°</code>, on <code>173.5 mph</code> with symmetric <code>0.35 s</code> pulse <code>+11.52°/−11.57° yaw</code> versus <code>10.93°</code> on Porschka 911, maximum roll <code>3.85°</code> and no roll-over. Test objects removed, filtered vehicle/runtime warnings/errors absent, cash/purchases/saves/DataStore/publish unchanged, Studio returned to Edit.

**Repair-bay late-observer initial yaw 3.26 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** The client-only first frame where a parked service vehicle could appear across the Repair Bay has been removed. For late joins, returns of visual interest, or reactivation, the state was already not <code>Approaching</code>, so the inbound route was immediately considered complete; the zero-tangent route landed in <code>CFrame.new(initialPosition)</code> and set the world LookVector <code>-Z</code> before the first Heartbeat. On Tycoon3/4/7/8 this resulted in a yaw <code>90°</code> error, on Tycoon1/2 — <code>180°</code>, and on Tycoon5/6 the appearance was correct only by coincidence. <code>TycoonServiceClient</code> now sets the stationary repairing car's exact <code>geo.forward</code> position of the current plot/slot before parenting the proxy; the standard heading logic in Heartbeat remains unchanged. Edit audit of all <code>8 plots × RepairBay1/2</code> confirmed the fix of the old error <code>0°/90°/180° → 0°</code>. MCP Play on rotated Tycoon3 RepairBay1 and Tycoon8 RepairBay2 showed perfect alignment of the proxy LookVector with the local axis (<code>dot=1</code>) and correct lift center positioning; no new script errors exist. QA attributes and owner overrides have been removed, cash/purchases/saves/DataStore/publish did not mutate; Studio has been restored to Edit mode.

**Obstacle-aware purchase-pad route 3.25 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** The world navigation arrow is no longer a single straight <code>Beam</code> from player to <code>ObjectiveWorldTarget</code>. Such a ray previously selected the exact <code>TA_Ceiling</code>, but physically passed through walls like Car Wash/Atelier and others. <code>PadPathGuideClient</code> now locally constructs a walkable route through <code>PathfindingService</code>, simplifies only nearly collinear points, and draws the previous white arrow texture as separate ground segments between saved angles; the route is recalculated when the player moves by 6 studs, when the target shifts by 3 studs, or when the objective changes. The destination remains exclusively owned by the authoritative <code>ObjectiveWorldTarget</code>; <code>NextPadId</code> is no longer used as a fallback. When no nav path can be computed, a safe direct fallback is preserved, and previous modal/vehicle/outside-plot/distance visibility gates remain unchanged. Edit audit confirmed <code>TA_Ceiling</code> on Tycoon1–8, pathfinding <code>Success</code> on all eight plots, and obstacle intersection of the old straight line on each plot; this rules out the hypothesis of a broken copy only in Tycoon2–8. MCP Play acceptance passed on iPhone 17 Pro → iPad Pro M5 → Average Laptop: route status <code>success</code>, active segmented Beams, and absence of intersections with collidable geometry; only transparent <code>CanCollide=false</code> vehicle hitboxes are allowed. Script compile OK, no new runtime errors; the only warning is the existing analytics count 98/99. Cash, purchases, saves, DataStore, and publish did not mutate; Device Simulator has been restored to <code>default</code>, Studio — to Edit.

**Server Restart migration lifecycle 3.15 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** The standard Creator Hub <code>Restart Servers</code> team itself terminates matchmaking on the outdated servers, maintains the selected bleed-off delay, and then automatically transfers all players from one old server to the replacement server with the last published version; the game does not manually duplicate this transport <code>TeleportAsync</code>. <code>RestartMigrationService</code> is signed up to <code>game.ServerRestartScheduled</code> and uses only the passed Roblox <code>restartTime</code>, thus supporting any actual delay without a hardcoded 10-minute assumption and independently of UI/documentation limits. <code>ServerRestartState</code> replicates state and serves late joiners. The client-side <code>RestartNotice</code> is a non-blocking, mobile-first string directly under CashPanel: white <code>GothamBold</code> with a black border, without a card and without input/focus lock. The notification appears immediately, then at each 10-minute milestone, at the 5/4/3/2/1 minute, disappearing after 4 seconds; the last 10 seconds remain visible and update every second. On T−5 the server concurrently performs a two-pass <code>DataService.flush</code>, and the existing <code>BindToClose</code> remains the last save fallback. iPhone landscape <code>749×368</code> screenshot-pass confirmed centering of the money line and absence of overlap with touch controls; runtime cadence confirmed transitions 11→10 and 6→5 minutes, exact 5/4/3/2/1-minute and 15/10/1-second copy; end-to-end Studio hook provided <code>saved=1 / failed=0</code> and returned state to Idle. The full transport is not launched in Studio due to platform limitations and requires a controlled published Restart Servers smoke-test; publish and production restarts were not performed, Studio is returned to Edit.

**High-speed handling profiles 3.16 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** The last four cars in the catalog — <code>FierroRossa</code>, <code>Porschka911</code>, <code>Mercurion190r</code> and <code>ZentauriR</code> — received an opt-in high-speed chassis profile, not affecting slow cars. <code>RuntimePackage.Controller</code> supports individual quadratic steering reduction, symmetrically uses forward/backward absolute speed, and provides a soft straight assist that only activates when the steering wheel is released, remembers the driver's chosen heading, and disables when intentional A/D/input is detected. Peak motor torque is reduced for Fierro/Porschka/Zentauri without reducing their maximum speed; the steering rack is enhanced and accelerated for Mercurion; suspension damping for Porschka is raised to a verified safe <code>1800/2100</code>. The standard zero anti-roll Zentauri is preserved: artificial addition of an anti-roll stabilizer in telemetry sweep degraded vehicle behavior. On the overall 2048-stud Asphalt track, high-frequency Play telemetry before steering pulse produced lateral offset after 4.15 s: Fierro <code>0.10</code>, Porschka <code>3.66</code>, Mercurion <code>0.40</code>, Zentauri <code>0.12</code> stud at <code>188.9/179.9/75.9/200.8 mph</code>; full input on <code>0.35 s</code> produced controlled yaw <code>0.24°/11.33°/14.39°/7.56°</code>, without roll-over or skid. Central runtime attach confirmed all four templates and exact application suspension attributes; Controller errors <code>0</code>. DataStore, purchases and publish did not change; Studio is returned to Edit.

**Personal Garage post-Return DrivePrompt recovery 3.21 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** client-only deadlock was eliminated after the sequence <code>drive → exit → phone/world suppression → Return → close phone</code>. In the fixed live state, the server-side <code>Personal_Wronkler.DrivePrompt.Enabled</code> was <code>true</code>, empty <code>VehicleSeat</code> had <code>Disabled=false</code>, but the same prompt remained permanently on the client <code>false</code>; manual local enable did not cancel, thus excluding active server lock. Cause: <code>WorldHoverClient</code> restored owner-only Repair/Seller/Customize prompts, but did not consider <code>DrivePrompt</code> self-healing surface, thus race could preserve a temporary false phone snapshot. <code>DrivePrompt</code> is now added to general owner recovery only under full invariant: personal car belongs to LocalPlayer, <code>VehicleRuntimeActive=true</code>, <code>DealerDriveLocked~=true</code>, seat exists, is not disabled and empty, starter is selected, Customize/Offline/world suppression are closed. This preserves blocking of foreign vehicles, occupied seats, and real lock states. End-to-end MCP Play re-entered via real keyboard <code>E</code>, exit, character removal from bay, phone suppression, successful <code>Return</code>, phone closure, and re-entry: new runtime prompt reappeared after <code>0.1 s</code>, seat remained <code>Disabled=false</code>, subsequent <code>E</code> provided server/client occupant owner and thawed chassis. Relevant errors <code>0</code>; purchases, cash, DataStore schema and publish were not changed; Device Simulator <code>default</code>, Studio returned to Edit.

### Visual acceptance and unified UI geometry — REQUIRED

- Each significant UI state after change is individually verified via screenshot in MCP Play on iPhone landscape. The actual composition together with Roblox Core UI is checked: safe area, top inset, cropping, text wrapping, touch target accessibility, scroll behavior, and result of each action. Then tablet and desktop passes are executed; Studio is returned to Edit after verification.
- Windows and cards of the same type use one geometry contract: identical outer/inner width, CornerRadius, padding, header/action/footer heights, and vertical spacing. The yellow top accent line is positioned within the rounded border. A one-action window ends immediately after the last button or text, without hints or empty bottom space.
- In palette windows (Paint/Neon/Tint), only the list of options is scrolled; the header and CTA remain fixed. <code>OptionsPanel.OptionTitle</code> is the only header of the selected Customize section on phone/tablet/desktop; card-local title labels are always hidden. The selection stroke is drawn as an internal line and does not clip the CornerRadius. Repeated names, current/selected status, or obvious hints are prohibited.
- One typographic family/weight is used across a single surface. All filled yellow and green CTA buttons use black text; disabled/locked CTA buttons have a gray background, white text, and disabled interactivity. The font for individual buttons cannot be changed due to monetary text. Non-interactive states cannot be disguised as buttons: performance <code>MAX</code> remains in its original location as yellow text without a background, border, or hit target.
- Customize uses <code>CoreUISafeInsets</code>, keeps standard Cash/Settings at the top, hides unnecessary HUD/Run/CarIndex/Garage layers, and displays a compact radio under TopHUD. Close must fully restore camera, controls, prompts, seat entry, and hidden HUD elements; reopening after Close and re-entering the vehicle are considered part of acceptance testing.
- Customize is a two-way confirmed session, not a single server flag. The server has no right to hide walls, disable terminals, Drive prompts, or VehicleSeats until the LocalScript registers readiness. Each opening uses a one-time session token and client acknowledgment; the absence of acknowledgment automatically and atomically removes all locks no later than 3 seconds. Any Return, Summon, vehicle replacement/respawn, Close, character removal, or player exit first ends the session and restores camera/controls/HUD, walls, terminals, Drive prompts, and seats. Phone Return/Summon are recovery paths and cannot leave a partial Customize state.
- Window Tint displays levels strictly <code>0% → 50% → 100%</code>; level and color are purchased in a single action. At <code>100%</code> the color is forcibly optical black with no light reflection, so only the black swatch remains in the palette: red/yellow/cyan/magenta are hidden and non-interactive. Upon returning to <code>0%</code> or <code>50%</code> all colored swatches become visible and accessible.

**Personal Garage Customize split-navigation 3.14 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** <code>PersonalGarageCustomizeClient</code> no longer uses a single right desktop panel with horizontal <code>ComponentNav</code> and a separate lower car selector. Customize is now a standalone exploration mode with a state model <code>ROOT → CATEGORY → OPTIONS</code>: on the left are root <code>BODY / ENGINE</code>, Back/Close, a vertical list of sections, and an embedded vehicle selector; on the right appears only the selected option-card. BODY retains Paint/Neon/Tint/Lights/Stance/Plates, ENGINE — Performance/Nitro; RemoteFunction actions, server authority, prices, and DataStore schema remain unchanged. During the mode, local and with exact restoration, <code>TopHUD</code>, <code>LeftRail</code>, Sprint/Debug/Phone/CarIndex/Objective layers and all physical personal cars are hidden; the selected vehicle is shown as a separate sanitized preview, and camera target/radius/horizontal offset are calculated based on the bounding box and free rectangle between panels. Drag/pinch are accepted only from the free viewport area. Phone landscape uses left <code>20%</code> clamp <code>148…172 px</code>, right <code>260 px</code>, real <code>GuiService:GetGuiInset()</code> + 8 px; tablet — <code>210/330</code>, desktop — <code>240/380</code>; portrait has a sequential bottom fallback. **Personal Garage Customize fail-safe lifecycle 3.14 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** the generalized partial-open state has been eliminated, where the server would set <code>PersonalGarageCustomizing=true</code> and block the garage before the client could connect <code>OnClientEvent</code>; when an event loss occurs, walls disappear, the character retains free movement, and Customize/Drive/VehicleSeat remain inaccessible. **Owner-only world UI / Personal Garage driveway clearance — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** the client must strictly hide all <code>BillboardGui</code> and <code>ProximityPrompt</code>, belonging to another player: owner is determined by explicit <code>PrivateOwnerUserId</code>, owner attributes of world anchor/ancestor or <code>TycoonN.OwnerUserId</code>. ## Roblox Studio hang/crash — REQUIRED LOG-FIRST PROTOCOL

If Roblox Studio freezes, stops responding, closes, crashes during Play, or endlessly loads before a new Play, **process logs must be read upon restarting Play, closing the current session, reinstalling Studio, and reading one Output at a time**. A standard Output shows only part of the events; process logs contain engine hang detector, Play boundary, Lua stack, OOM/fatal, and main-thread telemetry.

Official Roblox directories:

- **Windows (Win32 Studio):** <code>%LOCALAPPDATA%\Roblox\logs</code>, typically <code>C:\Users\&lt;user&gt;\AppData\Local\Roblox\logs</code>.
- **macOS:** <code>~/Library/Logs/Roblox</code> (Finder → Go → Go to Folder…).
- When Studio responds, for example via the built-in path **Help → Open Logs Folder**; logs can be read without closing the current Studio session.

Source paths: [Roblox Creator Hub — Debugging / Log files](https://create.roblox.com/docs/studio/debugging#log-files).

Required investigation order:

1. Record the local time of the incident, the exact place and Studio process, and do not launch a new repro over the first one.
2. In the logs folder, sort by Last Modified and select the files closest in time to the incident with <code>_Studio_</code>; the timestamp in the filename and identical session suffix link entries to the same session. For cross-session failures, also read the previous Studio log.
3. Do not load large multi-megabyte files entirely. First filter by <code>LuaHangedScript</code>, <code>Hang stack trace</code>, <code>StudioHangMonitor</code>, <code>MainThreadHangs</code>, <code>PlaySoloStartTotalTime</code>, <code>PlaySoloSuccess</code>, <code>CreatorOutput</code>, <code>OutOfMemory</code>, <code>bad allocation</code>, <code>fatal</code>, <code>crash</code> and the suspicious script name; then read the narrow context around the match.
4. Classify the failure type: did runtime reach <code>PlaySoloSuccess</code>; did the hang detector identify the exact Lua script/stack; is there an OOM/fatal; or did the failure occur before project runtime. Metadata/network warnings are not root causes if a later stack points to a game script.
5. When a freeze occurs, allow Studio time to record hang telemetry and copy a fresh log **before** forced termination. If the process remains alive, do not close it solely for reading logs.
6. If the text log does not show a stack: on Windows, create a dump of the exact <code>RobloxStudio</code> process via Task Manager → Create dump file; on macOS, select the exact process in Activity Monitor → Sample Process. Do not terminate all Studio processes by name: other windows may contain unsaved work.
7. Logs and dumps may contain local paths, project names, and session data. Do not publish them in full; only minimal diagnostic strings without credentials/PII should be transferred to the Bible/KB.

Only after log-first triage are targeted code fixes and a bounded repro permitted. Each output must separate proven stack/root cause from working hypotheses.

**Supersession Rule V3.05:** the current Personal Garage sells cars only through <code>PrepareSell → ConfirmSell</code>; a free starter car may be released for $0 only if another owned personal car exists, and repeated starter selection is prohibited. Compact geometry of the Garage and screen orientation are defined by section 25: previous LIVE summaries 2.68/2.90/2.99 are preserved as history of implemented change sets, but their prior direct-Sell/action names, sole-starter release, and phone sizes are no longer part of the current contract.

**Personal Garage capacity reconciliation 3.09 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDED FOR EXACT-FULL PURCHASES BY 3.12:** the number of owned personal cars is strictly limited by the number of purchased physical garage bays: one bay allows one car, two bays allow two. Purchasing in a free bay works normally; exact-full state without overflow uses an authenticated atomic replacement from V3.12, while legacy/future overflow still blocks normal actions until manual invariant restoration. Overflow is not fixed by hidden deletion and is not a one-time migration: the server outputs <code>overflow=max(0, ownedCount-garageCapacity)</code> on each state/action and requires the user to sequentially select excess vehicles; therefore, recovery automatically reverts upon future desync. A mandatory phone-first modal appears after closing Offline Earnings, does not close on Shade/X, displays all owned cars, and for each car shows a single <code>PrepareCapacitySell → ConfirmCapacitySell</code> with a one-time 15-second token. Any car can be sold, including the original free Rusty/Wronkler, but the server stops providing sale quotes immediately upon reaching capacity, so at least one car remains. Capacity refund equals catalog acquisition paid + the cost of the current saved configuration (current paint/tint/headlight finish, installed Neon/Nitro/Stance, and current performance levels) + 10% goodwill. History of repeated previous repaints is intentionally not summed; only the latest/current configuration is considered. For V3.09 <code>acquisitionPaid/customizationPaid</code> are included in sanitized per-car save metadata, and the current customization component is recalculated from the current configuration and is not inflated by past color experiments. Settings build label — <code>V3.09</code>. MCP Play on the actual legacy state confirmed <code>owned=4 / capacity=1 / overflow=3</code>, mandatory <code>SELL 3 OF 4 CARS • KEEP 1</code>, four selectable refund cards, and hidden Close; a read-only Wronkler quote was composed <code>$0 car + $325,000 current configuration + $32,500 goodwill = $357,500</code>, Return was blocked, owned/overflow remained <code>4/3</code>. A destructive Confirm on the real profile was not executed; Studio was returned to Edit.

**Personal Garage join runtime reconciliation 3.00 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES 2.98 SINGLE-DEFER CLAIM ASSUMPTION:** the live session showed a correctly loaded owner save <code>expanded=true / active=Porschka911 / secondary=Wronkler</code>, but <code>Tycoon1.Buildings.PersonalGarage.RuntimeCars</code> remained empty: both cards were saved as box assignments, not as physical models somewhere in the world. The cause — an existing-player claim could pass on the bootstrap boundary: <code>PersonalGarageService</code> sometimes still saw <code>record.owner=nil</code> during its one-time audit and did not receive the already passed <code>tycoonClaimed</code>. Restoration now coalesces per-player via <code>queueRuntimeSync</code> and is triggered from three consistent sources: <code>tycoonClaimed</code>, the bootstrap audit of an existing record, and a stable change/initial read <code>TycoonFolder.OwnerUserId</code>. Repeated signals from the same frame result in one restore; arbitrary <code>task.wait</code> / <code>task.delay</code> are no longer used. Init no longer performs unconditional <code>destroyGarageCars</code> on the claimed record: cleanup is applied only to actually free tycoons, so late bootstrap cannot erase vehicles just restored. Two consecutive fresh Plays on the actual save confirmed immediate <code>RuntimeCars=2</code>: <code>Personal_Porschka911</code> in the primary slot and <code>PersonalSecond_Wronkler</code> in the secondary slot. Ownership, active/secondary IDs, cash, and DataStore did not mutate; publish was not performed.

**Personal Garage expansion transaction recovery 2.99 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES 2.98 PROFILE OBSERVATION AND 2.94 AUTO-FILL:** live LogService identified the exact cause of persistent <code>Garage is busy</code>: purchasing a second box already made a critical commit <code>expanded=true / secondaryCarId=Wronkler</code>, then <code>GameplayEvents.emit("PersonalGarageExpanded")</code> crashed from <code>Unknown gameplay event</code> to line <code>mutating[player]=nil</code>. Purchasing capacity no longer automatically selects a vehicle: the new second bay is preserved empty (<code>secondaryCarId=nil</code>) until a deliberate collection action occurs. Previously saved Wronkler is not a new issuance: it is the retained original free starter in the owner's collection, which the previous 2.94 code automatically selected as the first owned vehicle, different from Porschka. Ownership was not erased; the player can explicitly <code>MAKE CURRENT</code> or <code>RELEASE FREE CAR • $0</code>. The current owner-save after actual user purchase has <code>expanded=true / active=Porschka911 / secondary=Wronkler</code>. **Personal Garage Customize categories / Pink / performance 2.95 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES 2.89 SIX-UNIVERSAL-COLOR CATALOG:** universal palette for all vehicles that have an authored factory palette is expanded from six to seven colors: adds <code>Pink RGB(238,72,148) / #EE4894</code> for $25,000 per vehicle. Customize is divided into <code>BODY</code> and <code>ENGINE</code>. BODY retains Paint, Neon, Tint, Headlights, Stance and Plates; ENGINE contains Nitro and three real performance branches <code>Acceleration / Top Speed / Handling</code>. Each branch stores a separate per-car level 0…3; factory rating is taken from catalog acceleration/handling, and top speed is normalized as <code>topSpeedKph / 36</code>. The total gain across three levels is capped at 10/10: +1.80 acceleration, +1.65 top speed, +1.65 handling; the branch with factory 10/10 receives max level 0. Level prices equal 2.5% / 4% / 5.5% of the personal market price, rounded to $5,000 and have a minimum of $25,000. Acceleration hardware scales drive torque/acceleration by <code>1 + ratingGain×0.12</code>; Top Speed scales forward/nitro speed by <code>1 + ratingGain×0.08</code>, and reverse receives only 35% of this increase; Handling reduces steering reduction, increases rack responsiveness, and moderately raises tyre friction/slip threshold. Original rig attributes are cached in <code>_PersonalBase_*</code>, so repeated apply is idempotent and levels do not multiply. Hardware is still considered in permanent salvage value according to the previous 35% rule. UI shows three gradient-filled rating bars, level dots, and authoritative next-price/max state. On compact touch, service selection comes first, then its options/install are shown; on desktop, the overall category layout is preserved. The lower gradient selector with two arrows switches all physically present personal cars in the two boxes without clone/spawn or primary car change; the server accepts customization only for the actual vehicle currently standing in the garage. Old personal copies <code>PHONE STORAGE / PARK THIS CAR / ADD SPOT</code> are deleted; actions are named <code>PERSONAL COLLECTION / MAKE CURRENT / SEND TO GARAGE</code>. Synthetic runtime confirmed Rusty level 3 ratings <code>4.20/4.57/4.85</code>, speed <code>43.016</code>, acceleration <code>24.320</code>, steeringReduction <code>0.251</code>; Zentauri preserved factory 10/10 for acceleration/top speed with max level 0. UI runtime confirmed Pink, BODY/ENGINE, ComponentNav, CarSelector, PerformanceCard and non-zero bar fills; the final fresh Play resulted in 0 client/server errors. Production upgrade/purchase and publish were not performed; Studio is reverted to Edit.

**Personal Garage visual-only Stance Kit 2.91 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; UNINSTALLED PREVIEW DEFAULT SUPERSEDED BY 2.98:** Customize received a persistent per-car service <code>STANCE KIT</code>. A one-time installation on a vehicle costs $50,000, after purchase negative toe can be toggled on and off for free; server requires built <code>TA_BodyKitStation</code>, saves <code>stanceInstalled/stanceEnabled</code> and publishes only presentation attributes <code>PersonalStanceInstalled/PersonalStanceEnabled</code>. The hotfix now explicitly removes <code>WeldConstraint</code>, <code>JointInstance</code>, <code>Constraint</code> and <code>Attachment</code> before parenting/anchoring; OFF additionally restores stale hidden originals even after reload/interrupted renderer. ON→OFF→ON resulted in a physical-wheel max position delta of <code>0.000000</code>, OFF yielded visual folders 0 / hidden originals 0, re-ON resulted in clones 4 / clone welds 0; client errors 0. The saved user stance remains enabled for re-testing; the current false-default normalization for un-purchased vehicles is defined 2.98. Studio is returned to Edit.

**Vehicle identity / glossy finishes / repair feedback 2.86 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES COLOR-REFRESH AND CAR-FIXED SCALE BEHAVIOR:** the exact factory variant is now part of the vehicle's identity: <code>FactoryPaintId</code>, selected on the first incoming proxy, is saved as <code>ActiveCarSave.factoryPaintId</code>, exposed to owner service through <code>ServiceSavedFactoryPaintId</code>, and restored on W/service/P proxies; legacy processing records without this field use a stable fallback based on <code>tradeId</code>. <code>CarVisuals.applyCondition</code> captures the saved <code>PaintHue</code> before the factory-base refresh and reapplies it last, so Listed→Offer→Sold transitions and helper pickup no longer change a blue or otherwise repainted car back to factory yellow; the buyer departs in the same display instance. **OWNER DEBUG in-car access / last-driver plates 2.84 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES 2.65 SEAT HIDING:** the compact <code>DebugButton</code> remains visible while <code>VehicleSeat</code> is occupied.
