**ChevroluxC10 suspension stance 2.85 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES 2.84 NOT-FIXED STATUS:** the cause of the lift-like stance is resolved minimally in the canonical full rig and staged Workspace preview. Of the four cylindrical suspension motors <code>WheelFL/FR/RL/RRMotor</code>, the limits <code>LimitsEnabled=true</code>, <code>UpperLimit=1</code>, spring stiffness/damping/free lengths, chassis, body, and visuals are preserved; only <code>LowerLimit −1→0</code> is changed. Prior to the fix, the occupied runtime was located approximately at <code>−1.053</code>; after the fix, actual motor positions match <code>−0.062…−0.078</code>, meaning the wheels have risen approximately 0.99 stud and no longer dangle from the old lower stop. Occupied Play provided spring lengths <code>3.105…3.132</code>, roll <code>−0.008°</code>, and pitch <code>−0.101°</code>; the rendered side capture confirmed standard clearance and nearly level body. Three straight wheel-ground rays provided penetration/clearance within <code>−0.034…−0.050 stud</code>; the left rear ray intersected authored <code>WheelStopper.Stopper</code> and was excluded from ground metric. Canonical roots are marked <code>ChevroluxSuspensionPreset=NoDroop_0_1_2026_09_01</code> and <code>ChevroluxSuspensionFixVersion=occupied_v1</code>. Prior to the change, <code>ServerStorage.VehicleSourceImports.ChevroluxC10_PreSuspensionFix_2026_09_01.Full</code> and the available Workspace preview were preserved. Relevant runtime errors 0; Play paused, Studio Edit.

**ChevroluxC10 first runtime stance diagnosis 2.84 — DIAGNOSED / NOT FIXED:** the first occupied Play after a deliberately static-only import confirmed an incorrect lift-like stance. The new body is not raised relative to the authored wheels: the old source visual and the new canonical maintain the same body↔wheel-center delta approximately <code>2.333 stud</code>. At runtime, all four <code>WheelFL/FR/RL/RRMotor</code> have limits <code>−1…1</code> and actually reach approximately <code>−1.053</code>, lowering the physical wheel assemblies an additional <code>≈1.05 stud</code>; the four suspension springs have <code>FreeLength=3.8017</code> and are occupied <code>CurrentLength≈4.083…4.090</code>. Therefore, visually it appears the body is raised, although in reality the wheels have dropped down to the lower stop. The likely fix — a similar transition to the lower limit <code>−1→0</code>, as in the accepted Nissho droop fix, with mandatory occupied A/B; according to the current request, the suspension has not changed.

**Personal Garage Customize shell 2.72 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** while the owner opened Customize, only their client fully hides <code>Building</code> shell, roof and <code>GarageDoor</code> via <code>LocalTransparencyModifier=1</code>. <code>Foundation</code>, <code>FrontThreshold</code>, driveway, terminal, runtime car preview, and other players are unaffected. A single <code>PersonalVehicleOccupancyClient</code> owns both transparency modes: Customize uses 1.0, exiting a personal car from the garage uses 0.62; switching modes and closing the modal exactly restores previously saved modifiers without conflict between two client scripts.

**Repair world card 2.73 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** exactly one detail-layer exists above the owner's dealer car: <code>RepairProgressPresenter</code> no longer creates its own <code>BillboardGui</code>, but instead supplements the existing authoritative <code>DealerValueBillboard</code>. Within one card, sequentially appear <code>FIX &lt;PART&gt; • complete/total → CAR NAME → $VALUE → progress → +$GAIN FROM REPAIRS</code>; the tutorial helper preserves the Highlight/SelectionBox for the required detail, but its separate text billboard is disabled. <code>WorldHoverClient</code> dynamically accepts a late-added <code>RepairProgress</code>: collapsed 24×24 hides all details, expanded size is taken from attributes and equals 310×146. Play on Rusty Repairing confirmed one enabled billboard, <code>FIX ENGINE • 2/3</code>, correct value/gain, and zero relevant errors.

**Fancy world surfaces 2.74 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** a single <code>DealerValueBillboard</code> received a visual language for negotiation UI: a dark semi-transparent rounded capsule, a vertical yellow→orange→green accent, a thin warm stroke/gloss, Gotham-family for identity/action, and standard money typography for amounts; car name/value/gain and repair progress each have their own gradients. The geometry of the expanded repair-card remains 310×146 and has non-overlapping intervals <code>0–27 / 27–54 / 54–89 / 94–110 / 116–142</code>. <code>PadHologramClient</code> no longer creates a separate dark <code>PadWorldCard_*</code>: the nearest active pad temporarily expands its authored billboard 150×56 → 190×96 and adds lines for category/effect + <code>STEP ON PAD TO BUY</code> or <code>SHORT $…</code>. Title/price/status use one Gotham-family; upon leaving authored size/geometry, they are restored, and new lines are hidden. Play on Car Jack confirmed four non-overlapping lines, <code>PadWorldCard duplicates=0</code> and accurate restore.

**World-surface refinement 2.75 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES 2.74 BACKING/CATEGORY COPY:** <code>DealerValueBillboard</code> has no dark capsule base, frame, accent, or gloss: the vehicle remains visible behind the floating caption/name/value/gain; only text gradients/strokes and gradient progress track are preserved. Pad benefit is never replaced by category: <code>PadHologramClient</code> triggers the authoritative <code>TycoonConfig.padEffectText(definition)</code>, so Car Jack displays a specific <code>+25% REPAIR SPEED</code>, and the action remains a separate line <code>STEP ON PAD TO BUY</code>/<code>SHORT $…</code>. Play confirmed <code>DealerValuePanel=false</code>, <code>PadWorldCard=0</code> and exact Car Jack lines.

**Compact objective/pad copy 2.76 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES 2.75 PAD ACTION COPY AND THE OLD OBJECTIVE LAYOUT:** <code>MVPObjectiveGui.ObjectiveCard</code> is pinned in the top-right corner on desktop/tablet/phone, showing one current action in a single line, with authored size 320×46 px on desktop and does not create <code>ProgressBack/Progress/Fill</code>. Optional reward and session-local <code>−/+</code> remain in the same thin line; Cash continues to reside above center. <code>PadHologramClient</code> preserves authored bold fonts <code>GothamBlack</code> title and <code>FredokaOne</code> price; the nearest pad expands 150×56 → 190×82 and displays only a large yellow authoritative economic effect <code>+25% REPAIR SPEED</code>. <code>STEP ON PAD TO BUY</code>, <code>SHORT $…</code>, and category-row are not part of the presentation. Play on Air Compressor confirmed card 190×82, correct fonts/effect, hidden status, <code>PadWorldCard=0</code>; Objective was 320×46 on the right, without progress descendants and without overlapping with Cash.

**Post-purchase effect typography 2.77 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** a single <code>BuildAnimationClient.playPurchaseEffectPopup</code> that receives authoritative <code>effectText</code> after successfully stepping on any pad repeats the visual contract of the existing <code>CashPopup</code>: <code>FredokaOne</code>, saturated green <code>RGB(120,255,150)</code>, black text-stroke with transparency 0.15 and a fully transparent background. The dark capsule, <code>UICorner</code> and separate green <code>UIStroke</code> are removed; rise/fade timing 5 studs / 1.1 s is preserved. The contract is shared across repair speed, sales speed, and all other pad characteristics.

**Bavora ambient traffic stance/colors 2.78 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** lightweight <code>ReplicatedStorage.TrafficCars.Bavora</code> has authored <code>TrafficHeightOffset=0.30</code>, compensating for the changed pivot of the new geometry. On standard Asphalt this leaves the lower tire point approximately <code>0.045 stud</code> above the surface instead of sinking into <code>0.255 stud</code>. The optimized <code>TrafficClient</code> now applies a global <code>VehiclePaint.applyFactory</code> on each spawn and uses a cyclic variant key, so ambient Bavora sequentially uses Main Slate Blue, Deep Burgundy, and Muted Emerald; all other traffic templates without factory palette remain unaltered.

**Bavora front stance / mesh plate typography 2.79 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** for full drivable Bavora <code>frontSuspensionLength</code> is raised from <code>4.166976</code> to <code>4.286976</code>, rear remains <code>4.166976</code>. On flat Asphalt with an occupied DriverSeat this reduced the nose-down pitch from approximately <code>−0.99°</code> to <code>−0.31°</code>, without creating a raised nose; the front/rear wheel-center delta became nearly zero. The general default plate renderer no longer covers authored <code>*LicensePlate*</code> MeshPart with a white GUI panel: the frame is fully transparent, and only uppercase <code>GothamBold</code> text remains on the plate mesh with a soft light stroke. The text area uses <code>X=0.20…0.96</code>, reserving the left 20%-section for a future Roblox logo; vertical inset equals <code>Y=0.10…0.90</code>.

**Plate logo reserve refinement 2.80 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; SUPERSEDES 2.79 TEXT-AREA X:** after visual review, the left edge of the plate text is shifted another 3% to the right: <code>Position.X=0.23</code>, <code>Size.X=0.73</code>, right edge is preserved <code>X=0.96</code>. The left 23%-section is fully reserved for a future Roblox logo; font, vertical inset, transparent background, and authored plate mesh remain unchanged. Runtime front/back Bavora confirmed exact bounds and text <code>DNLVSTUDIO</code> without errors.

**ChevroluxC10 visual/damage 2.81 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; STATIC QA ONLY:** the selected new import ChevroluxC10 has been moved onto the previous proven full rig: chassis, suspension, wheel physics, DriverSeat calibration, interior, rear lights, front turn signals, plate meshes/runtime, and helper/collision contracts are preserved; the body, glass, exhaust, individual <code>L_Headlight</code>/<code>R_Headlight</code>, and four wheel visuals are replaced with the new FBX geometry. Canonical copies are synchronized in <code>ServerStorage.CarModels.ChevroluxC10</code>, <code>ReplicatedStorage.TrafficCars.ChevroluxC10</code> and staged <code>Workspace.ChevroluxC10</code>; the original import is retained in <code>ServerStorage.VehicleSourceImports.ChevroluxC10_ImportSource_2026_09_01</code>. The white Base Map of the body remains universal; the only explicit paint target is the new <code>Body</code>. Factory/personal palette: Main Brick Red <code>#90413E</code> by default, Graphite Gray <code>#60646B</code>, Desert Ochre <code>#90713E</code>. From <code>ChevroluxC10.fbx</code> an authored blend shape <code>Broken</code> is extracted: <code>ChevroluxDamageProfile</code> contains 480 affected control points and 960 packed delta values; body damage is connected to the general EditableMesh pipeline. In broken state <code>L_Headlight</code> is hidden and restored after Body repair. Static Edit QA confirmed mesh/material hierarchy, wheel transforms/welds, front/back plates, one paint target, all three factory colors, and personal default. Play, morph-ready/runtime vertex mapping, physics, and traffic motion were intentionally not tested at the owner's request and remain mandatory acceptance prior to publish.

**FierroRossa selected fit / non-wheel textures 2.83 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** the manually selected and tuned <code>Workspace.FierroRossa</code> has become the authoritative visual source for 12 precise transform deltas: <code>FrontLights</code>, <code>LF_TurnSignal</code>, <code>RF_TurnSignal</code>, rear <code>Back_LicensePlate</code> and eight parts of the full rear light cluster. These deltas are transferred to <code>ServerStorage.CarModels.FierroRossa</code> and are available in the corresponding identical parts <code>ReplicatedStorage.TrafficCars.FierroRossa</code>; full now has 0 transform differences with the Workspace source, and relative to the pre-change backup differs by exactly 12 parts. Body SurfaceAppearance is updated only with the maps <code>M_FierroRosso_*</code> from the specified texture folder: Color <code>122257192899641</code>, Normal OpenGL <code>78660320544631</code>, Roughness <code>97663132242125</code>, Metallic <code>79071305687267</code>. All <code>M_FierroRossoW_*</code> are excluded; a static audit confirmed 0 wheel SurfaceAppearance changes. Canonical roots are marked <code>FierroVisualVersion=2026_09_01_v2</code>, <code>FierroTextureSet=M_FierroRosso_nonwheel_v1</code> and <code>FierroLightFitVersion=selected_workspace_v4</code>. Pre-change full/traffic is saved in <code>ServerStorage.VehicleSourceImports.FierroRossa_PreManualTexture_2026_09_01</code>. Rendered Play front/rear captures confirmed the new optics, rotated rear cluster/number and white tint-compatible Base Map; runtime preserved one paint target and an invisible VehicleSeat.

**FierroRossa visual/damage/debug colors 2.82 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** the selected imported model asset <code>105910747050643</code> and original <code>FierroRosso.fbx</code> are moved to the previous proven <code>FierroRossa</code> full rig. Chassis, occupied suspension preset <code>FierroBalanced_3p98_3p72</code>, wheel physics/welds, eight wheel↔visual/body <code>NoCollisionConstraint</code>, DriverSeat calibration, interior, exhaust, plates and runtime helpers are preserved; body, glass and four wheel visuals are replaced with new geometry. Canonical copies are located in <code>ServerStorage.CarModels.FierroRossa</code>, <code>ReplicatedStorage.TrafficCars.FierroRossa</code> and <code>Workspace.FierroRossa</code>; raw import and previous canonical rigs are saved in <code>ServerStorage.VehicleSourceImports</code>. The new <code>Body</code> is the only explicit paint target. Factory palette: Rosso <code>#E34342</code> default, Giallo <code>#E3B942</code>, Blu Corsa <code>#426FE3</code>. Authored FBX shape <code>Body1</code> is converted to compact <code>FierroRossaDamageProfile</code>: 451 affected control point / 902 packed values; Play matched 1203 runtime EditableMesh vertices, showed <code>BrokenGlassOverlay</code> with cracked appearance at <code>BrokenAmount=1</code> and accurate morph/glass restore during repair. Old front lights/turn signals and full rear amber/red/white cluster are fitted to the new body surface via <code>FierroLightFitVersion=new_body_v3</code>; Play captures confirmed both views. All canonical <code>VehicleSeat</code> are hidden, and dealer/personal/debug spawn forcibly reassigns <code>Transparency=1</code>/<code>CastShadow=false</code>, so the service driver block does not participate in the visual of any vehicle. OWNER DEBUG reveals factory swatches only for vehicles with palette; server stores the selected debug instance and <code>SetDebugCarColor</code> changes its color without clone/spawn/PivotTo. Rosso→Giallo→Blu Corsa→Rosso preserved one <code>DebugInstanceId</code>, one name and count 1.

**Bavora visual/damage 2.71 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** the new imported Bavora serves as the visual source for the full dealer/personal rig and lightweight traffic template, while physics, suspension, seat calibration, rear lights, number plates and runtime contracts are preserved from the previous working model. The white Base Map of the body remains universal; factory tint is applied via <code>SurfaceAppearance.Color</code>. Dealer arrivals and ambient traffic deterministically receive Main Slate Blue <code>#4B6387</code>, Deep Burgundy <code>#874B5B</code> or Muted Emerald <code>#4B8765</code>; a permanent personal Bavora without selected paint always receives Main Slate Blue. Only the new MeshPart <code>Body</code> is an explicit paint target. The lightweight traffic template additionally uses <code>TrafficHeightOffset=0.30</code> for correct tyre clearance. The authored FBX blend shape <code>Broken</code> is converted to <code>BavoraDamageProfile</code>: 435 control points, 1088 matching runtime EditableMesh vertices; <code>BrokenAmount</code> controls the body and damage overlays. In broken state <code>L_Headlight</code> and <code>LF_TurnSignal</code> are hidden, and Body repair restores both parts and the original lighting. After successful selection of starter client on 10 seconds, the selection closes, the physical personal car is highlighted, and a pointer with text <code>THIS IS YOUR NEW PERSONAL CAR</code> appears above it.

**Current canonical 2.86 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** a fresh profile starts with **$3,300** and completes **one** tutorial vehicle — Rusty. The FTUE order is: <code>Cash Register $100 → Rusty $25 → Engine $6 → Wheels $4 → FirstSalesman $1,500 → Manny паркует Rusty W→P → игрок вручную закрывает первую продажу за fixed $1,000</code>. After mandatory purchases, the player has $1,665, after sales — $2,665. The body of Rusty is already fixed; Rex uses 9 tutorial cues. Old deal 2/3 migrates to standard post-tutorial stock and is no longer part of the new tutorial.

**Current gameplay/UI contract:** entire dealer stock is staff-only; players do not sit in business vehicles. FirstSalesman automates incoming purchases, W→P listings, and buyer acceptance after the first manual transaction; a free ServiceHelper can pick up the W→P listing fallback. <code>Quick Fix</code> was removed from the live presentation and replaced with <code>PARTS SALES LAPTOP → SELL CAR PARTS</code> with a curve <code>28 + 16 × (level − 1)</code>, maximum 20. Tutorial guidance is always enabled and toggled only via a compact button <code>?</code>; the legacy large yellow arrow <code>HUD.Powers.Toggle</code> is hidden and non-interactive. Objective does not display the word <code>CHAPTER</code>; it has session-local <code>−/+</code> and preserves collapsed state upon objective/modal suppression. In all layout profiles, this is a thin top-right row without a progress bar; on phone it scales to 0.5 and dynamically remains below live <code>SellCarsHUD.CashPanel</code>. Optional reward does not create a second layer. The visual button 22×22 px with glyph 14 px has a separate touch-target 44×44 px. <code>−</code> hides the entire panel — background, stroke/accent, action, and reward — and leaves only a compact <code>+</code>; guidance remains unchanged. Tablet maintains scale 0.7 and top-right placement. Sale Spot — a circular green-yellow marker without a vehicle silhouette. Purchase guidance uses canonical <code>PadId</code> and has been verified across all Car Wash zones/plots; customer service cars retain a compact blue locator.

**Personal Garage/UI 2.68 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED; STARTER RELEASE SUPERSEDED BY 2.90:** after the tutorial, the player receives a separate owner-only driving path, not linked to dealer stock. A legacy profile with a completed tutorial and no Personal Garage save receives an update offer: first, <code>OfflineEarningsUI</code> is completely closed, then a clean pause of 2 seconds is held, and then a choice between two starter cars is shown. Only the first selected car from Rusty/Wronkler is free; its identity is saved as <code>freeStarterCarId</code>, so it is never converted into a revenue source. The team <code>RELEASE FREE CAR • $0</code> removes this car and its customization for $0, but does not reset the permanent <code>starterChosen</code>, does not close the Garage/Market, and does not open a new free distribution. Until the first <code>starterChosen=true</code> the physical door of the Personal Garage remains visible, collisions and queries are enabled, and the application <code>GARAGE</code> in Phone is hidden; after a confirmed critical save, the door opens and the active vehicle is created inside. There is no separate Garage slot on the left rail: after the tutorial, only <code>CAR INDEX</code> and immediately below it <code>PHONE</code> remain. In the authored Phone desktop <code>GARAGE</code> is the first application and uses the approved Workshop garage asset <code>77153690525766</code>, while <code>BUY CARS</code> is the second; before the starter Buy Cars selection, it temporarily occupies the first position. The Property guard maintains both states and prevents late build-state restore from either prematurely opening a closed garage or closing an already open one. All other permanent personal cars are purchased through the application <code>BUY CARS</code> within the standard Phone visual interface and are unlocked through the same progression gates as Car Index. The active vehicle is physically restored in <code>Buildings.PersonalGarage</code>; a separate button <code>RETURN TO GARAGE</code> parks it back, and <code>SPAWN CAR</code> moves the same runtime instance to the player without recreating it. The vehicle is customized at the internal terminal Body Paint/Window Tint/Neon/Nitro/Plate Delete and is saved separately for each model; a standard paid vehicle is sold with 35% salvage only for hardware. Body Paint and Window Tint use the current superseding contracts 2.87–2.89. While the player is sitting in owned <code>PersonalVehicle</code>, the client-private dealer <code>SALE SPOT / PARK HERE</code> guide is forcibly cleared and does not reappear in the polling cycle; after exiting, it becomes available again for real dealer tasks. The Phone-only compact branch (<code>TouchEnabled</code>, compact viewport, not tablet) reduces Garage/Market to 69% width with clamp 460…520 px, height 218…220 px and a safe top position; the car row has 116…118 px, and the main/sell/footer actions have 44 px. Runtime: iPhone 17 — 516×220; iPhone 7 — 460×220; Galaxy A06 — 486×218, the footer remains fully inside the panel. Customize on phone is limited to 276…292 px in width and 252…276 px in height, uses a fixed header and vertical scroll; Plate Delete is added as a separate compact card with 44 px action. Tablet/desktop authored geometry is restored separately and does not inherit the phone rewrite. Actual Garage/Customize captures were obtained on iPhone 17, iPhone 7 and Galaxy A06; the native transition <code>BUY CARS</code> did not work through MCP pointer injector, so the Market route remains BLOCKED and is not replaced with a mislabeled Garage screenshot. Customize-preview has a constant 360° drag-orbit via mouse/touch, pinch/scroll zoom; UI amounts are taken from server state. While a modal is open, <code>CustomizePrompt</code> and <code>DrivePrompt</code> are hidden/disabled and are restored only upon closing. Dealer cars remain staff-only.

**MainTrack activity 2.65 — LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO / UNPUBLISHED:** when the owner sits in permanent <code>PersonalVehicle</code>, they receive a compact invitation to MainTrack. On the track, a client-private arrow points to the start/finish arch; the first lap determines the entry side and initiates one full circuit. The first of 24 route positions appears exactly 40 studs in front of the arch in the direction of movement, and simultaneously three owner-private cash pickups are visible and accessible; a rolling window shifts along the route. Revenue is credited server-side when the X/Z coordinates of the collected money fall within the oriented horizontal footprint of the vehicle, expanded by 7 studs; the Y height and dimensions of the specific vehicle model do not interfere with collection. After completing the route, a second pass through the arch ends the run if at least 12 points have been collected, even if other pickups are skipped; if fewer than 11 points are collected, the player continues to the next lap without a new start. Only actually collected points are awarded. Each active collected point has a green vertical beacon and a readable <code>+$value</code>. After a successful finish, a persisted cooldown 10 minutes is applied. On the desktop HUD, it appears in the top-right corner and does not overlap the central balance; the touch variant 220×42 px and passive ready/guide/cooldown notices disappear after 3–4 seconds. On a tablet, it is positioned below the Objective with a vertical gap, and in a vehicle, on any touch device it is moved to the central free lane <code>X=0.5, Y=76</code>, thus not blocking <code>Exit/Nitro</code>. OWNER DEBUG has received server-authorized <code>RESET TRACK COOLDOWN</code>.

**Validity boundary:** change sets 2.49–2.99 implemented in open Studio, but **not published**. Change set 2.99 passed live root-cause/transaction recovery: PersonalGarageExpanded was registered, exception-safe lock cleanup, and successful Return of two saved vehicles without MessageError; active/ownership remained unchanged. Change set 2.98 passed fresh Play acceptance of a standard circular second-bay pad, authoritative $2.5M price, saved <code>expanded=false</code>, absence of legacy parking guide, and stance-state audit; purchase of a second box was not executed. Change set 2.97 passed fresh Play on synthetic clones of the entire canonical/traffic set: optical-black OFF, personal ON/OFF, dealer authored restore, Wronkler 2/2 lenses and Chevrolux 3 appearance lenses / 2 emitters confirmed during 0 client/server errors. Change set 2.96 passed isolated Customize radio lifecycle, real modal load, camera pitch-range, and fresh Play acceptance without client/server errors; pointer-driven full drag capture was not executed due to 1×1 suspended MCP viewport, but the same mouse/touch <code>InputChanged</code> consumer and spherical camera producer loaded successfully without errors. Change set 2.95 passed config/physics/UI acceptance and fresh Play without client/server errors; real paid purchase of performance level was not executed. Change set 2.94 passed two-slot runtime/geometry/swap acceptance with subsequent rollback of the test expanded-save; the final profile remains single-bay. Change set 2.93 passed fresh Play on synthetic canonical Porschka911: exact ArcticSilver/GuardsRed/MiamiBlue changed only <code>Body.Chassis.SurfaceAppearance.Color</code>, Base Color atlas and all other details remained unchanged, personal default and all three traffic variants confirmed, server/client errors 0. Change set 2.92 passed fresh Play on real Personal_Bavora and real Customize UI: five lens/beam colors, standard/super brightness, OFF restore, collision-free emitters, Rainbow/Flashing/Cop phases, admin-free CTA and animated preview; three non-admin Robux paths remain disabled until published Game Pass IDs are installed instead of 0. Change set 2.91 passed physical visual-only stance acceptance without changing colliders/hangars. Change set 2.90 passed clean personal-car damage/glass and reversible Plate Delete acceptance; its non-admin Game Pass also awaits published ID. Change set 2.89 passed model-specific palette/NARDO/grid acceptance; its previous Porschka-unavailable and six-universal claims are superseded 2.93/2.95. Change sets 2.87–2.88 confirmed color continuity, optical-black tint, and color-only repaint. Change sets 2.82–2.86 cover Fierro import/optics/color, debug plates, Chevrolux suspension and repair feedback; individual full traffic soak, second cross-player, and full buyer-departure runs were not executed. Exact native Objective-minus and Buy Cars pointer activation remain <code>BLOCKED</code> MCP input restrictions. Phone Garage/Customize visually accepted on iPhone 17, iPhone 7 and Galaxy A06; unowned Tycoon2–8 P1/CUSTOMIZE surfaces are hidden 7/7. Production performance purchases, DataStore namespace, and publish-state remained unchanged. PlaceVersion <code>1630</code> was audited. Historical contracts 2.34–2.38 covered three tutorial vehicles, rhythm 3→2→1, early Zentauri, player-driven W→P and Quick Fix remain only dated history.

## 0. How to use this Bible

This document answers four questions:

1. What is this game about, and why and how do players play it.
2. What actually works in the current Studio build.
3. Where the code, configuration, UI, world, data, and web-hub are located.
4. How to safely make changes without breaking the economy, save data, or server authority.

### Truthfulness Tags

- **[LIVE]** — confirmed by code and DataModel of the open Studio build.
- **[TARGET]** — explicitly confirmed by the owner as the target solution, not yet necessarily implemented.
- **[CONCEPT SNAPSHOT]** — a published idea from a specific hub version; its status as a future target still needs confirmation from the owner.
- **[LEGACY]** — an old contract, archive, compatibility layer, or disabled branch.
- **[RISK]** — a known issue or area where it's easy to damage the project.
- **[UNKNOWN]** — solution not fixed; product decision required before implementation.

### Source of truth hierarchy

If documents contradict each other, use this order:

1. **Actual behavior of the open Studio build.**
2. **Executable Studio code and its actual consumers**, not comments or unused fields.
3. **Runtime configs** <code>TycoonConfig</code>, <code>DealerCarConfig</code>, <code>ServiceConfig</code>, <code>TownsfolkConfig</code> together with server modules that read these fields.
4. **This file** as a map of verified state on a specified date.
5. **<code>sell_cars_source_recovered</code>** as the canonical published implementation of the web-hub, API/deploy policy, and published asset set. Its product copy is a separate snapshot, not an unconditional target.
6. **Bloom Brain** — a dated internal product context. Always consider the status of a specific entry: materials used in cross-audit 18 August have <code>needs-review</code>, thus explaining the design intent, but do not create [TARGET] or override live runtime.
7. **Named dated documents and datasets in <code>sell_weird_pizzas_hub</code>** can be canonical only for their own artifact/date and always yield to current Studio. The entire dirty folder is not canonical.
8. Screenshots, mirrors, generated assets, teardowns, and backup folders — only as evidence/reference.

> Critical rule: files <code>app.js</code>, path cards <code>Workspace...</code> and concept images are not Roblox place sources. Changing web-hub does not change the game.

---

## 1. Briefly: what is this game

**Sell Cars** — a Roblox tycoon game about transforming a cheap roadside plot into an automotive business. The player starts with a foldable desk and a plot full of ruins, buys problematic cars from locals, repairs them, lists them for sale, accepts buyer offers, and reinvests profits into staff and new service areas.

### Player's fantasy

I find undervalued cars, restore their value, sell them profitably, and in real time transform a barren junk lot into a living automotive empire.

### Core product pillars

1. **Clear causality:** bought an object — the world physically changed; repaired a part — the price increased; hired an employee — manual routine disappeared.
2. **Cars and NPCs live in the world:** a dealer arrives, the car physically stands on the workbench, the hired employee drives it to the lot, a buyer comes and the car leaves. The player manages the deal and the business, but does not drive dealer stock.
3. **Manual work explains automation:** Junk, Repair and Tuning first give the player manual actions, then employee actions. Repair Bay Checkout Clerk completes the manual checkout and acts as a strict gate for Car Wash. In Tuning, a specialist automates work in their queue, while a late Service Advisor handles checkout for all three queues.
4. **One clear next step:** HUD and world guide show the nearest meaningful goal.
5. **The server owns money and state:** the client sends intent, not the price, payout, or confirmation that a purchase occurred.
6. **Visible business growth:** progression is not just the amount of Cash, but also built buildings, equipment, staff, and operational queues.

### Current product limitations

In runtime there is:

- rebirth, prestige or business reset;
- daily streak, daily quests or battle pass;
- a second currency;
- a global item inventory, full auction, gifting, or containers; a separate permanent collection of personal cars exists;
- pets;
- Robux gamepasses, developer products or receipt handling;
- implemented Main Money Track economy;
- implemented Premium Showroom, Tokyo, and Alien chapters.

Offline return reward is implemented as a basic server-authoritative Cash only after automation begins: the profile must have a completed tutorial, purchased <code>FirstMechanic</code> and previously persisted <code>lastSeenAt</code>. Until the first mechanic is hired, offline earnings are zero and <code>YOUR BUSINESS KEPT WORKING</code> does not appear, even if a Cash Register has already been purchased. After unlock, the absence of service for less than 90 seconds is compensated for by built functional business pads and is capped at eight hours. The first login and legacy profile without an anchor only start the countdown. The popup shows the already credited amount. Buttons are clearly raised above the modal background through a shared ZIndex contract; <code>2× · 49 R$</code> remains a signed placeholder without MarketplaceService, receipt, and additional payout.

The concept-hub also directly avoids expansion into auctions, containers, rarity mutations, pets, rebirth, and multiple currencies. Target 2.48 uses the word auction only as a fictional interface for repeatable part sales: no individual item, bids, marketplace, or player-to-player economy appears. If this changes, an intentional product decision is required, not a local patch.

## 2. Main game loop [LIVE]

### Cycle of one dealer car

~~~mermaid
flowchart LR
    A["World seller: Incoming"] --> B["Offered: BUY / HAGGLE / REFUSE"]
    B -->|BUY| C["Junk: the car belongs to the player"]
    F1["Phone fence: list of offers"] --> F2["PICK / HAGGLE / BUY"]
    F2 --> F3["Paid Incoming: delivery"]
    F3 --> C
    C --> D["JunkSpot: Body + Wheels + Engine → roadworthy"]
    D --> E["Ready + persisted pipeline plan"]
    E -->|no facility or helper| F["Listing remains available; absence of service does not block sale"]
    E -->|free ServiceHelper + eligible owner stock| X["Critical W → processingCars; W becomes immediately free"]
    X --> R["Repair Bay: mandatory full service"]
    R --> O["All built: dirty Wash → Dyno → Body → Paint"]
    O --> P["Critical processingCars → exact reserved P"]
    P --> P2["Used Cars gate → center aisle → setup → reverse park"]
    P2 --> G
    F --> G["Listed: price is fixed"]
    G --> H["Buyer arrives"]
    H --> I["Offer: ACCEPT / ASK MORE / REFUSE"]
    I -->|ACCEPT| J["Sold: money is recorded, the car leaves"]
    I -->|REFUSE| G
~~~

The main world-seller branch still goes through states  
<code>Incoming → Offered → Junk → Repairing → Ready → Listed → Offer → Sold → removed</code>. Within <code>Ready</code> there exists an independent persisted owner pipeline: <code>idle → queued → servicing → complete</code>. Listing is prohibited only for truly launched/reserved service runs; absence of facility or helper does not permanently make the car unlistable. If the facility is not built, the corresponding stage does not enter the plan. If the helper is not purchased, the service plan does not start and the car remains available for regular W→P listing. If all helpers are occupied, the car waits for a limited time.

For <code>queued</code> a server watchdog is in place: after 60 seconds warnings are logged, <code>LastQueuedWarningAt</code> and analytics metric <code>queue_age</code> are recorded; after 180 seconds, an emergency exit from the queue is executed, freeing the car, NPC, and station/claim slot. Mechanic/service claims also have bounded timeouts and must release all associated resources. Manual emergency path <code>/releasecar &lt;username&gt; [tradeId|all]</code> is accessible only to authorized administrators. Seller patience/departure logic cannot take already purchased owner stock. Owner car transitions are logged through the funnel <code>acquired → repair → service → listing → sold</code>.

Incoming cars in the physical queue receive an invisible anchored <code>QueueCollisionHull</code> in collision group <code>Car</code>; this is a query-only target (<code>CanCollide=false</code>, <code>CanQuery=true</code>), independent of the decorative collision of the imported chassis. Any anchored queue presentation is moved to an authored CFrame and has no right to physically collide with Character; otherwise, the shell becomes a high-speed kinematic wall and throws the player. One <code>DealerProxyId</code> lives on the same Model from the first visible queue arrival via Q1 and promotion to W; ownership/state changes do not clone the chassis or reassemble unchanged condition-treatment. Parts, dirty, and oilLeak roll until the proxy appears and are transferred to the authoritative car save. Therefore, during the tutorial, Rusty's front left wheel is missing from the very first appearance, does not 'disappear upon purchase', and returns only after Wheels repair. The waiting driver uses R15 traffic-style drive pose; upon progression to Q1, it is replaced by the exact authored seller. Queue sanitize restores the authored controller/light state, but on gameplay boundary <code>CarVisuals.lockDealerDriving()</code> repeatedly and permanently blocks all <code>Seat/VehicleSeat</code>, <code>DrivePrompt</code>, and <code>GetInToListPrompt</code>. After being placed on W, the driver exits immediately. If FirstSalesman is engaged in another physical action, the world-seller prompt remains available to the player; manually closing the buy transaction in this window triggers a deal-cash popup and one of four reactions.

The client-only owner-service proxy also remains fully non-collidable, and four-wheel surface fitting must exclude all Player Characters from physical raycasts: an avatar inside the body is not a road surface and must not change the vehicle's pitch/roll. ServiceHelper seating stores the result of <code>NPCSeatFit.fit</code> in the exact queue-drive state and reuses it on every <code>PivotTo</code>; raw <code>SeatOffset</code> must not be substituted after fitting. An explicit <code>NPCSeatRoofY</code> is the authoritative per-seat ceiling and bypasses the general plausibility clamp: when a model pivot is low, the distance from <code>SeatOffset</code> to the actual roof can legitimately exceed the generic <code>CABIN_MAX_ROOF_RISE</code>. Per-car <code>NPCSeatCalibrationOffset</code> is applied after dynamic headroom fitting; in traffic/service RadoBoxlet, the vertical calibration component is zero because height is determined by the exact <code>NPCSeatRoofY=4.433049</code>, while the horizontal offset <code>(0.600000, 0, 0.499985)</code> only centers the driver in the seat. For Tow Truck, authored <code>RoofY</code> refers to the actual cab roof, not the upper bound of the single body mesh. TrafficCars face along the local <code>−Z</code> axis, so the tow-cargo mount uses positive X-pitch <code>+8°</code>: the front axle is raised toward the lift, while the rear stays closer to the road.

The authoritative source for manual NPC seating is the per-car <code>NPCSeatCalibrationOffset</code> on full dealer rigs in <code>ServerStorage.CarModels</code>. Before tycoon systems initialize, <code>TycoonBootstrap.syncNpcSeatCalibrations()</code> propagates it and the optional <code>NPCSeatRoofY</code> to every corresponding lightweight <code>ReplicatedStorage.TrafficCars</code> and existing <code>VehicleSourceModels/CarSources</code>; traffic, seller, and service proxies therefore use the same 11 manually accepted calibrations and cannot diverge after a template rebuild. Current early cars: Rusty <code>(0,+1.5,0)</code>, Wronkler <code>(0,+1.6,-0.3)</code>; Rado retains the superseding roof fit <code>NPCSeatRoofY=4.433049</code> and only the horizontal offset <code>(+0.6,0,+0.5)</code>.

The physical W → P transfer pre-reserves a specific display slot for exact <code>tradeId</code>. Dealer stock has only staff-controlled lane: FirstSalesman retrieves the ready vehicle from W, proceeds through the common centre-aisle approach, and physically places it in exact P; lease is removed only upon successful commit, cancellation, or full player teardown. Players cannot sit in a tycoon vehicle either before or after tutorial: <code>VehicleRuntimeActive=false</code>, <code>DealerDriveLocked=true</code>, seat disabled/non-touchable, both input prompts are disabled. The implemented Personal Garage uses a separate ownership/runtime path <code>PersonalVehicle=true</code> and does not reuse the dealer listing seat. The final owner-service handoff preserves <code>ROUTING_V2_USED_PARKING=true</code>: ServiceHelper follows the same general approach to setup at 14 studs before P, then performs a short physical reverse into the pre-reserved slot.

Before final W/P settle <code>DealerCar</code> projects the bounding box of the vehicle onto XZ and checks whether a live Player Character ends up inside the future footprint. For each character entering the footprint, the closest point outside is selected, confirmed by a ground raycast as collidable and free from other vehicles, obstacles, and characters; only after this is the HumanoidRootPart safely teleported. Guard works for animated arrival, direct/fallback settle, and several delayed retries. If no safe candidate is found, the system avoids a dangerous teleport and retries the check later. This prevents players from getting stuck inside a vehicle that appears on a repair/display pad.

The check <code>carReadyOnLot</code> distinguishes foreign occupation from own lease: if a regular free P is no longer available, the exact reserved destination of the same model and the same <code>tradeId</code> remains valid for final commit. Otherwise, the first tutorial transfer with a single P1 entered the target but incorrectly returned to recovery on W.

Each occupied display slot <code>P</code> has its own buyer lane: arrival timer, NPC, exact <code>tradeId</code>, offer, and sale lock are not shared with adjacent P. Therefore, four displayed vehicles can simultaneously receive four buyers, complete four independent transactions, and leave in parallel; the physical animation of one UsedCarsSalesman is not an authoritative mutex for sales. <code>acceptFallback</code>, <code>raiseFallback</code>, and <code>walkFallback</code> always allow exact ticket within per-player bucket. The phone serializes multiple concurrent buyer-threads: the current conversation is not overwritten by a foreign packet, and the next pending trade is shown after closing the current one.

Fence — a separate buy-flow: list and selection occur before the vehicle appears; after payment, a paid <code>Incoming</code> is created, and the delivered vehicle directly transitions into <code>Junk</code>, without going through a world-seller <code>Offered</code>.

Physical slots:

- <code>W1</code>, <code>W2</code> — working slots;
- <code>P1…P6</code> — display/sale slots;
- a fresh profile actually starts from W1 and P1;
- W2 can be opened;
- P2…P6 have separate sequential purchase pads and are again available to a new profile; old sparse purchases are normalized down without re-payment.

### What the player does

1. Buys a Cash Register, meets a physical seller, and completes the first purchase with Rusty; the FirstSalesman enters an obligatory tutorial before the first sale.
2. After the tutorial, for standard seller offers, the player selects to buy, negotiate once, or decline; the Rusty tutorial remains a mandatory first purchase.
3. In the tutorial, Rusty is manually repaired strictly <code>Engine → Wheels</code>; the Body is already fixed. The standard post-tutorial stock still requires Body, Wheels, and Engine.
4. Buys FirstSalesman, after which the employee physically transports Rusty from W to P. Late <code>ServiceHelper/2/3</code> services handle the owner-service pipeline, while First/Second Mechanic perform W1/W2 JunkSpot repairs; the player does not drive dealer stock in any of these branches.
5. Waits for a buyer.
6. Accepts the offer, requests a higher price once, or declines.
7. Receives payout and repeats the cycle or purchases the next building.

### Important differences

- Sold vehicles **disappear from stock and leave**. They do not remain as passive display units.
- Repair Bay, Car Wash, and Tuning serve two strictly separated sources: <code>Street/StationCash</code> and <code>Owner/CarValue</code>. Customer vehicles pay immediately for the job; dealer stock does not provide station payouts and accumulates multipliers until sold in Used Cars.
- JunkSpot repairs to Body/Wheels/Engine make a wreck roadworthy. Repair Bay in the owner pipeline provides a full-service or oil leak fix; this is not a repeat of the same operation.
- <code>ListCar</code> is a legacy remote that only updates prompts. A standard W-stock listing commit performs <code>ListingTransport</code> after physical inspection from W → P; the completed owner pipeline uses a separate critical server transfer to exact P, but after commit, the same authoritative vehicle physically traverses the final segment from the Used Cars gate and parks backwards.
- Phone serves two separated scenarios: legacy fence-chat before buying FirstSalesman and post-tutorial personal desktop with the application <code>BUY CARS</code>. The personal-car market does not duplicate the physical garage view.

### Meta-business cycle

~~~mermaid
flowchart TD
    A["1 Rusty flip + FirstSalesman"] --> B["Automated Junk Lot"]
    B --> C["Repair Bay: customer service"]
    C --> D["Car Wash lane 1"]
    D --> E["Car Wash lane 2"]
    E --> F["Tuning: Dyno"]
    F --> G["Tuning: Body"]
    G --> H["Tuning: Paint"]
    H --> I["TA_Compressor → BUSINESS COMPLETE"]
~~~

Old zones continue to generate revenue while players build new ones. FirstSalesman handles world seller, W→P listing, and buyer acceptance; the phone-fence is disabled after purchase, not automated. Whitelist <code>pick1…pick3</code> has been fixed and is no longer a current blocker. Live auto-buy triggers <code>salesmanCanBuy</code>: the ask ratio and reserve for the next mandatory pad are applied before purchase, so an unattended loop should not consume the progression reserve. The current formal end of progression is the purchase of <code>TA_Compressor</code> and the achievement of objective <code>YOUR BUSINESS IS RUNNING!</code>. No victory reset occurs after that.

## 3. First session and tutorial [LIVE]

### Start

A new blank profile has:

- Cash: **$3,300**;
- chapter 1;
- tutorial: <code>eligible=true</code>, <code>dealIndex=1</code>, <code>completed=false</code>;
- a saved blank profile starts with <code>pads={}</code>, then <code>JunkLotService.grantFreeStarterPads()</code> automatically marks <code>RepairPad</code> and <code>FirstWorkbench</code> as owned until the first <code>PadService.refresh</code>; their $0 purchase pads are hidden, the player does not click them, and <code>PadPurchased</code> is not sent;
- the first paid pad: <code>CashRegister</code> for **$100**.

Cash Register requires both automatically issued starter pads and is the player's first real purchase. Before its purchase, the seller is not reserved, and the objective guides the player through the sequence:

<code>BUY CASH REGISTER → SELLER IS PULLING IN → MEET THE SELLER → BUY RUSTY → REPAIR ENGINE → REPAIR WHEELS → HIRE A SALESMAN → SALESMAN IS PARKING RUSTY → BUYER IS WALKING OVER → TALK TO THE BUYER → SELL RUSTY</code>.

On first login, Rex directly offers to buy the Cash Register. <code>NextPadId=CashRegister</code> guides the existing ground beam to the exact purchase pad, and local <code>Highlight</code> and pulsing <code>PointLight</code> maintain strong highlighting even near the target. Only the purchase receipt triggers the next line about the approaching first seller.

### One deterministic tutorial transaction

| Vehicle | Starting parts Body/Wheels/Engine | Manual actions | Ask | Service cost | Buyer offer | Net profit |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Rusty | <code>true/false/false</code> | Engine, then Wheels | $25 | $6 + $4 | fixed $1,000 | $965 |

The starting economy is a rigid contract: <code>$3,300 − Cash Register $100 − Rusty $25 − Engine $6 − Wheels $4 − FirstSalesman $1,500 = $1,665</code>. The player has enough money from the very first second to cover the entire mandatory tutorial path without grinding on the Parts Sales Laptop or earning money from sales; the Salesman's price is not reduced for FTUE purposes. After selling Rusty, the balance becomes $2,665; subsequent CarJack $300, AirCompressor $600, and FirstMechanic $700 leave $1,065.

FirstSalesman is the last paid tutorial action. Its real <code>FirstSalesManBuyPad</code> is hidden until the tutorial Rusty reaches exact state <code>Ready</code>, then becomes available for $1,500. After purchase, the permanent FirstSalesman model appears on the plot, physically moves to exact Rusty, and performs the W→P route. The first buyer transaction remains manual: the tutorial ends only after a confirmed Rusty sale. After <code>tutorial.completed=true</code> FirstSalesman automatically handles world seller, W→P listing, and buyer acceptance.

For Rusty, at <code>Wheels=false</code> only the rendered front-left tire <code>WheelFL/LF_Wheel</code> is invisible; the physical wheel assembly is preserved for stable authored arrival and Salesman route. After wheels are completed, the original transparency is restored. The Rusty body is already fixed and does not generate a repair prompt.

All business vehicles are staff-only. On spawn, promotion, listing recovery, and completion of staff route, the server holds <code>DealerDriveLocked=true</code>, <code>VehicleRuntimeActive=false</code>, clears <code>owner</code>, disables seats and both input prompts. The code for creating a temporary player listing seat has been removed. Personal Garage is a separate implemented system: ownership control remains owner-only, one physical passenger seat is shared, and neither of these entry contracts extends to dealer stock.

Tutorial attention uses the same horizontal textured ground Beam and one persistent edge marker. <code>ObjectiveClient</code> publishes an exact world target to the local <code>ObjectiveWorldTarget</code>, and <code>PadPathGuideClient</code> guides the Beam to this detail. For seller and buyer targets, an exact <code>ownerUserId + tradeId/tutorialDealIndex</code> is selected, and <code>Highlight.Adornee</code> is assigned to a full NPC model — the NPC pad does not highlight. The target sequence is: Cash Register pad → arriving Rusty → exact seller NPC → Engine marker → Wheels/front-left wheel helper → Salesman pad → real Salesman → listed Rusty → exact buyer NPC.

Rolling migration does not delete previous unfinished deals 2/3: the tutorial normalizes to <code>completed=true, dealIndex=2</code>, and existing W/processing/P stock becomes standard post-tutorial stock with <code>tutorialDealIndex=0</code>. A new schema/DataStore season was not created.

### 9 short tutorial cues Rex

All tutorial <code>SoundId</code> are currently empty; these are subtitle cues. Canonical English copy:

- <code>TUTORIAL_00</code> — buy the Cash Register;
- <code>TUTORIAL_01</code> — Rusty approaches;
- <code>TUTORIAL_02</code> — talk to the seller;
- <code>TUTORIAL_03</code> — engine is dead, front-left wheel is missing; start with Engine;
- <code>TUTORIAL_04</code> — Rex says: a guy called to offer to work; buy the Salesman pad;
- <code>TUTORIAL_05</code> — Manny gets the keys and takes Rusty;
- <code>TUTORIAL_06</code> — Rusty is listed, buyer approaches;
- <code>TUTORIAL_07</code> — talk to the exact buyer and close the deal;
- <code>TUTORIAL_08</code> — first sale is complete, tutorial is finished.

### Historical: three deterministic tutorial deals [SUPERSEDED 2026-08-26]

The following block describes the previous contract and is preserved only as dated design history.

| Deal | Vehicle | Starting parts Body/Wheels/Engine | Manual actions | Repair objective | Sell objective | Value before/after | Ask | Buyer offer | Profit without seller haggle |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | Rusty | <code>false/false/false</code> | Body, Wheels, Engine | <code>RESTORE RUSTY 0/3 → 3/3</code> | <code>SELL RUSTY</code> | $90 → $220 | $25 | $210 | $185 |
| 2 | Nissho | <code>true/false/false</code> | Wheels, Engine | <code>RESTORE NISSHO 0/2 → 2/2</code> | <code>SELL NISSHO</code> | $640 → $730 | $35–44 | $693 | $649–658 |
| 3 | Zentauri R | <code>true/true/false</code> | Engine | <code>START THE ZENTAURI 0/1 → 1/1</code> | <code>SELL THE HYPERCAR</code> | $4,945 → $5,000 | $75 | fixed $5,000 | $4,925 |

Features:

- manual repair rhythm — **3 → 2 → 1**; server readiness still requires all real <code>Body/Wheels/Engine</code> to be <code>true</code>;
- Nissho arrives with a clean Body, but faulty Wheels/Engine and diagnosis <code>BODY IS CLEAN · WHEELS AND ENGINE NEED WORK</code>;
- Zentauri arrives with a clean Body/Wheels and a single faulty Engine; her authored tutorial value and buyer offer are strictly fixed at $5,000;
- tutorial seller cards split <code>CURRENT VALUE</code>, <code>VALUE AFTER REPAIRS</code>, and <code>ASKING PRICE</code>; pre-corrected details are not revealed for player manual upgrades;
- tutorial deals do not show numerical rarity probability; only deal 3 receives a genuine <code>WILDCARD DEAL</code>;
- while the tutorial vehicle is in <code>Incoming/Offered</code>, the owner client binds a large green arrow <code>▼</code> to its exact <code>tradeId</code> with <code>AlwaysOnTop=true</code> and <code>MaxDistance=2000</code>; upon purchase and transition to <code>Junk</code>, the marker is removed, and post-tutorial stock never receives it;
- <code>NO THANKS</code> is not shown when purchasing any of the three tutorial vehicles; the server rejects stale/forged <code>refuse</code>. A normal rejection is returned after the tutorial;
- <code>KEEP THE CAR</code> is not shown in any of the three tutorial sales; the server also rejects stale/forged <code>refuse</code>. The option to keep the car appears only for post-tutorial stock;
- Vehicle runtime hides the lower NOS/Nitro meter, desktop/gamepad <code>NitroFrame</code> and touch <code>NitroButton</code> on all regular vehicles and does not bind Nitro action. UI and Nitro control are only enabled if the root model has <code>DebugSpawned=true</code>; this replicated attribute sets only <code>DebugService.spawnDebugCar</code>. <code>TutorialDealIndex</code> no longer controls Nitro visibility;
- the final card shows authoritative <code>BOUGHT / SOLD / PROFIT</code>, and Zentauri additionally <code>WILDCARD FLIP</code>;
- the first seller does not expire;
- authored tutorial Nissho and Zentauri bypass their normal progression gates only during the corresponding tutorial deal;
- the tutorial ends after the third confirmed sale;
- after tutorial <code>dealIndex=4</code>;
- regular post-tutorial vehicles start with <code>false/false/false</code> and require all three repairs;
- no new save schema exists: <code>tutorialDealIndex</code> and existing <code>ActiveCar.parts</code> preserve exact progress; old profiles without tutorial schema are still considered completed tutorial. Rolling migration converts unfinished old deal 2 Wronkler into Nissho, and deal 3 Nissho into Zentauri, preserving identity and the number of already completed manual parts.

After Cash Register, a guaranteed path without haggle leaves Cash **$235** after Rusty, **$884–893** after Nissho and **$5,809–5,818** after Zentauri. A fully mandatory automation spine after the tutorial costs **$3,200**, so after that remains **$2,609–2,618**: the previous tutorial money softlock is not restored. For the third deal, <code>ASK MORE</code> does not change the fixed offer, but the buyer responds with a natural in-game phrase <code>No, $5,000 is all I've got.</code>, without revealing the technical tutorial contract.

Cue mapping: fresh session until Cash Register → <code>00</code>, receipt at Cash Register → <code>01</code>, <code>CarArrived → 02/08/14</code>, <code>CarBought → 03/09/15</code>, <code>RepairCompleted → 04/10/16</code>, first <code>CarListed → 05</code>, <code>OfferReceived → 06/11/17</code>, first <code>CarSold → 07</code>, second <code>CarSold → 12 затем 13</code>, third <code>CarSold → 18</code>. The service applies session dedupe, cue priority, and contextual rejoin to prevent repeated replication from breaking the current step.

### Historical runtime acceptance — 18 August 2026 [SUPERSEDED]

This dated run tested the previous authored contract <code>Rusty → Wronkler → Nissho</code>; its numbers below are preserved as historical evidence and do not describe the current tutorial. The then-unpublished Studio draft was tested on isolated one-time QA DataStore namespaces; the production namespace and existing dev-profile were not reset. A full state/economy run yielded:

- Rusty: <code>$90 → $130 → $165 → $220</code>, three <code>PartFixed</code>, sale of $210, profit of $185, Cash after deal of $235;
- Wronkler: <code>$185 → $225 → $260</code>, two <code>PartFixed</code>; in the test, the original ask of $38 was voluntarily reduced via <code>ASK FOR LOWER</code> to $33, then sale of $259, actual profit of $226 and Cash of $461;
- Nissho: <code>$675 → $730</code>, one <code>PartFixed</code>, sale of $693, profit of $618 and final Cash of $1079; without Wronkler haggle, the canonical result remains $1068–1077;
- leave/rejoin preserved Rusty at 1/3 with the next <code>Repair Wheels</code>, Wronkler at 1/2 with a working Engine and next <code>Repair Wheels</code>, Nissho at 0/1 with a single <code>Repair Engine</code>;
- repeated repair after Ready did not produce VFX or value; the standard post-tutorial Rusty became Ready only after Body → Wheels → Engine;
- <code>DataService</code> did not change: 48,069 bytes, Adler32 <code>1a65f860</code>. After the test, the QA harness was removed, <code>TycoonConfig.DATASTORE_NAME</code> was exactly restored to <code>SellCarsTycoon_v9_20260810_working2_stock6</code>, Studio was stopped in Edit and publish was not performed.

The following 10 authoritative Studio scripts were modified: <code>DealerCarConfig</code>, <code>DealerCar</code>, <code>ProgressionService</code>, <code>TradeService</code>, <code>GameplayEvents</code>, <code>FunnelAnalytics</code>, <code>DealerCarClient</code>, <code>ObjectiveClient</code>, <code>CustomerCardClient</code> and <code>CarVfxClient</code>. <code>CarVisuals</code>, <code>JunkLotService</code>, prices and save schema were not changed.

Known verification limits: MCP could not physically call native <code>ProximityPrompt.Triggered</code>, although live prompt paths, part order, hold/range and helper presence were verified. Repairs in the acceptance run were introduced via a temporary Studio-only listener of an existing unused <code>RepairPart</code>, which called the same authoritative <code>DealerCar.repairPart</code> with standard validations; landing used the real <code>VehicleSeat</code>, list assist and trade remotes. Therefore, human E-hold and feel/pace require a subsequent manual playtest. Both Studio MCP capture routes stalled, and the OS session did not see the Studio HWND, so the screenshot sequence was not obtained. <code>SHOW ME</code> is missing in the project; P2 reduction of overall hint levels was not implemented, but helper is never created for already fixed details. A precise analytics event for the first machine entry still does not exist.

### Current verification — 27 August 2026

The current Studio contract and change sets 2.44–2.50 are re-verified against the main sections of the Bible. The canonical tutorial contains one Rusty, 9 tutorial cues, manual order <code>Engine → Wheels</code>, mandatory FirstSalesman before listing and fixed buyer payout of $1,000; the player entry path for dealer stock is missing. Fresh wallet, rolling migration of old deals 2/3, staff-only W→P, Parts Sales Laptop, post-tutorial guidance, cross-zone <code>PadId</code> targets, customer markers and circular Sale Spot are reflected as current. The dated verification of weighted selector 25 August remains valid for post-tutorial arrivals, but is no longer a test of the old three-car tutorial. This docs-only audit did not run a new Play, did not modify Studio/DataStore, and did not perform a publish.

### Rush

<code>TutorialService</code> — deprecated placeholder from four lines. Fields <code>rushAvailable</code> and <code>rushCompleted</code> are still retained for compatibility, but there is no active Rush prompt, payout or progression service.
