## 4. Cars and Pricing [LIVE]

### Current dealer pool

In <code>DealerCarConfig</code> the **11 catalog cars** are described, and all **11** are dealer-eligible upon completing their progression gates. <code>ZentauriR</code> enters the owner-stock selector only after full service unlock; the current tutorial does not bypass this gate and contains only Rusty:

| Display name | Model/template | Class | Base seller cost | Base value | After 3 parts | Gate | Readout rarity |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Rusty | RustyCar | low | $25 | $90 | $220 | start | 1-in-2 |
| Wronkler | Wronkler | low | $35 | $130 | $260 | start | 1-in-6 |
| Chevrolux C10 | ChevroluxC10 | low | $80 | $220 | $350 | start | 1-in-12 |
| Rado Boxlet | RadoBoxlet | low | $75 | $215 | $345 | start | 1-in-10 |
| Nissho Slide SX | NisshoSlideSX | mid | $250 | $600 | $730 | OpenRepairBay | 1-in-20 |
| Bavora B3 | Bavora | mid | $650 | $1400 | $1530 | RB_CarLift | 1-in-25 |
| Hondo Civix EK | HondoCivixEK | mid | $500 | $1100 | $1230 | RB2_Foundation | 1-in-23 |
| Mercurion 190R | Mercurion190r | mid | $3900 | $8600 | $8730 | TA_PaintControl | 1-in-70 |
| Fierro Rossa | FierroRossa | high | $4200 | $9800 | $9930 | TA_DynoRollers | 1-in-80 |
| Porschka 911 | Porschka911 | high | $5100 | $11200 | $11330 | TA_BodyKitStation | 1-in-90 |
| Zentauri R | ZentauriR | hyper | $24000 | $52000 | $52130 | full service (both lifts + both wash stations + 3 stand) | catalog metadata 1-in-200 |

<code>ZentauriR</code> has <code>dealerEligible=true</code>, <code>spawnWeight=1</code> and full-service class share <code>0.5%</code>. Gate <code>isFullServiceOpen</code> requires exactly seven functional pads: <code>RB_CarLift</code>, <code>RB2_CarLift</code>, <code>CW_BrushMachine</code>, <code>CW2_BrushMachine</code>, <code>TA_DynoRollers</code>, <code>TA_BodyKitStation</code> and <code>TA_PaintControl</code>. Without any of them, the hyper share equals zero. Mid slots on <code>CW_BrushMachine</code> / <code>CW2_BrushMachine</code> are reserved; live mid-models for them do not exist yet.

### Personal Garage collection and economy [LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO — 2026-09-01]

Personal cars are a permanent player collection and are completely separated from dealer W/P stock. The legacy profile is determined server-side based on the existing DataStore payload without <code>personalGarage</code>; if the tutorial has already been completed and the starter car has not yet been selected, the client displays <code>WELCOME BACK — WE UPDATED THE GAME</code> only after closing any active modal, including offline earnings, and an additional 2-second pause. Until a selection is made, the physical door is closed, and the app <code>GARAGE</code> in Phone is hidden; Phone remains accessible via post-tutorial entry to selection, and <code>BUY CARS</code> temporarily takes the first position. After <code>starterChosen=true</code> the Phone desktop shows <code>GARAGE</code> first and <code>BUY CARS</code> second in one row. The Garage uses the approved Workshop home/garage icon <code>rbxassetid://77153690525766</code> from the web prototype <code>sell-cars-concept-3-home.png</code>; a separate left-rail Garage slot has been removed. Only the first selection of Rusty or Wronkler is free. After a successful selection, the second starter model immediately receives a standard price: Rusty $120,000 or Wronkler $180,000; forged <code>Buy</code> before starter selection is rejected by the server. Other market prices: Rado Boxlet $300,000; Chevrolux C10 $450,000; Nissho $750,000; Hondo Civix EK $1,100,000; Bavora $1,500,000; Mercurion 190R $3,000,000; Fierro Rossa $4,500,000; Porschka 911 $6,000,000; Zentauri R $12,000,000. Locked cards remain visible with progression hint; unlock is determined by <code>DealerCarConfig.isCarUnlocked</code>. Rado requires <code>RepairPad2</code>, Chevrolux requires <code>Parking2</code>, the rest follow catalog/Car Index gates.

Garage action <code>SPAWN CAR</code> does not create a new car and does not return it to the garage. The server takes the current <code>activeCars[player]</code> instance, verifies that the car is free and not in Customize, finds the ground in front of the player via raycast, and moves the same model through <code>PivotTo</code>, setting both linear and angular velocity to zero. A two-second cooldown is applied.

Garage view simultaneously displays two independent commands. <code>RETURN TO GARAGE</code> triggers a server action <code>Return</code> and physically restores the active car on the authored <code>CarSpawn</code> within the garage. <code>SPAWN CAR</code> triggers <code>Summon</code> and moves the current runtime instance to the player; one command does not replace the other.

The base sale of a personally purchased car returns 60% of its market price. The car is always repurchased in factory state; customization is removed upon sale. To the base sale, an additional 35% of the installed hardware cost is added: Neon $26,250, Nitro $70,000, maximum $96,250. Body color, tint level, and tint color licenses are cosmetic purchases and are not included in salvage. Any sale proceeds only through a server-authoritative two-phase contract <code>PrepareSell → ConfirmSell</code>: prepare returns a one-time token with TTL 15 seconds, confirm revalidates ownership/state, and replay or repeated taps have no effect. A free starter car has <code>sellPrice=0</code>/<code>baseSellPrice=0</code>/<code>salvage=0</code> and cannot be released until it is the only personal car. When another personal car exists, the UI clearly displays payment of $0 and the price of re-purchase; a successful release preserves <code>starterChosen=true</code>, clears <code>freeStarterCarId</code>, and does not trigger a new free selection. If the active car is sold, another owned car is designated active within the same critical transaction; rollback fully restores Cash, ownership, active car, starter identity, and customization.

Full personal rigs must exclude their own replacement body collision hull from collisions with each driving wheel. Rusty already had 4/4 wheel↔<code>_RustyCollision</code> <code>NoCollisionConstraint</code>. After body replacement in Wronkler and Nissho, these connections were missing: all four wheels physically intersected respectively <code>Wronkler_Body</code> and <code>Car_Body</code>, causing Wronkler to lift the body, shake the rear axle, and lose traction, while Nissho barely moved. The canonical <code>ServerStorage.CarModels</code> now contains 4/4 wheel↔body links on both models. Wronkler returned to authored <code>CylindricalConstraint -1…1</code> and standard damping 200/100; without internal collision, its occupied equilibrium naturally lies approximately <code>+0.07…+0.16</code>. Nissho retains superseding limits <code>0…1</code>, necessary for its lower replacement body.

<code>FierroRossa</code> uses the accepted occupied ride-height preset <code>FierroBalanced_3p98_3p72</code>: <code>frontSuspensionLength=3.98</code>, <code>rearSuspensionLength=3.72</code>, standard stiffness/damping <code>9000/1200</code> in front and <code>10000/1400</code> in the rear. Previously increased to <code>VisualScale=1.1</code> rig incorrectly preserved unscaled Porschka lengths <code>3.43/3.55</code>; when the driver seat was occupied, the front CylindricalConstraint consistently remained at or beyond the upper limit <code>0.495</code> with equilibrium <code>0.50…0.53</code>, causing the wheels to enter the arches and the front axle to lose working travel. After tuning, all four occupied equilibria lie approximately <code>0.19…0.24</code>, the body stands level, the clearance replacement collider increased by approximately <code>0.96 → 1.27</code> stud, all four driving wheels touch the surface, and the existing 8 wheel↔visual/body <code>NoCollisionConstraint</code> links are preserved.

The visual contract <code>FierroRossa</code> version <code>2026_09_01_v2</code> uses the body <code>rbxassetid://85132761275182</code> and glass <code>rbxassetid://107088779141046</code> from asset <code>105910747050643</code>. The body has a white tint-compatible Base Map and one <code>VehiclePaintTarget=true</code>; tint performs <code>VehiclePaint</code>. Body SurfaceAppearance uses only non-wheel maps <code>M_FierroRosso_*</code>: Color <code>122257192899641</code>, Normal OpenGL <code>78660320544631</code>, Roughness <code>97663132242125</code>, Metallic <code>79071305687267</code>; the wheel set <code>M_FierroRossoW_*</code> is not assigned. Final light/plate fit is determined by 12 root-relative transform deltas of the selected <code>Workspace.FierroRossa</code> and marked with <code>selected_workspace_v4</code>; previous uniform front/rear shifts <code>−0.25/+0.15</code> are only a base for manual fit. <code>FierroRossaDamageProfile</code> uses authored <code>Body1</code>, 451 control point and cracked-glass overlay; runtime mapping equals 1203 affected vertices. The service <code>DriverSeat</code> is always invisible and without shadow.

Customization is available only to the owner at a physical <code>CUSTOMIZE</code>-terminal inside their garage when the active car is parked. Body Paint requires <code>TA_PaintCanisters</code> and exists only on <code>FierroRossa</code>, <code>Bavora</code> and <code>ChevroluxC10</code>, for which authored <code>VehiclePaint.FACTORY_PALETTES</code> are defined; on all other vehicles, body controls are unavailable. On each supported vehicle, six additional licenses are always available for $25,000: Black, White, Dark Red, Emerald, NARDO GREY and Midnight Blue. The visible authored factory color costs $5,000; any owned color is then selected for free. Red/gray factory duplicates are not displayed: Fierro hides Rosso, Bavora — DeepBurgundy, Chevrolux — MainBrickRed and GraphiteGray. The server whitelist is built from the same model-specific palette, so hidden/cross-model IDs cannot be purchased via forged request. Selection and ownership are preserved per vehicle as a universal ID or <code>factory:&lt;paintId&gt;</code>. Rendering uses a shared <code>VehiclePaint</code>: exact <code>SurfaceAppearance.Color</code>/<code>BasePart.Color</code> changes without mutation of authored Material/Reflectance/clearcoat, and glass, wheels, lights, interior, dirt/oil and other excluded details are not painted.

Window Tint requires <code>TA_BodyKitStation</code> and separates two independent purchases. Levels: <code>0%</code> is free and owned by default, <code>50%</code> $25,000, <code>100%</code> $50,000. Colors: black is free and owned by default; red/yellow/cyan/magenta cost $10,000 each with the same display palette as Paint Atelier. Purchased level/color belongs to one vehicle and can be reselected for free. <code>VehicleWindowTint.applyTint</code> mixes the original glass color with the selected color on alpha 0/0.5/1 and linearly transitions the original transparency to 0. At 0% the authored Color/Transparency/Material is exactly restored; when alpha > 0 the <code>SmoothPlastic</code> is used. The legacy dealer API is not broken: <code>applyFinal(true)</code> still means 100% black tint from completed Body stage, <code>false</code> restores factory glass.

Neon requires <code>TA_Neon</code>: hardware $75,000 on a specific model, the first color is enabled; saved color change $10,000; On/Off is free. Palette: Cyan, Bright Red, Magenta, Lime, Orange. Nitro requires <code>TA_NOS</code> and costs $200,000 on model. Rusty uses max nitro speed 48, Wronkler 63; duration 3 s, recharge 6 s. Nitro input/UI appears only on personal car with <code>PersonalNitroInstalled=true</code>, and server runtime rejects boost without upgrade. Customize-camera starts with a light auto-orbit, but the first mouse/touch drag switches it to a constant manual 360° orbit; scroll changes radius. All price labels and body/tint catalogs receive <code>state.prices/bodyPalette/tintLevels/tintPalette</code> from the server; client preview uses an accurate <code>catalog[].model</code> of the active vehicle and locally applies paint/tint/neon until Apply.

All purchases are handled by <code>PersonalGarageService</code>: owner, active car, garage parking, terminal distance, pad unlock, palette, balance, and repeat request are checked server-side; Cash + customization go through <code>commitCritical</code> with deep rollback, after which visuals are applied. New server actions — <code>ApplyBodyColor</code>, <code>ApplyTintLevel</code>, <code>ApplyTintColor</code>; invalid ID and insufficient Cash do not change money, ownership, or selection. Another player cannot occupy the personal seat. During Customize seat/prompt, control is disabled and the owner is forcibly ejected; close/death/despawn restores control. An empty parked vehicle keeps headlight meshes optically off, but not matte gray: a single <code>VehicleHeadlightLens</code> applies <code>RGB(2,2,3) / SmoothPlastic / Reflectance 0.025</code>, and the road SpotLight is turned off. When the actual owner occupant is present, the selected lens color/Neon and road lighting are restored; dealer running-light path exactly restores authored Color/Material/Reflectance. The overall normalizing matcher takes <code>Headlight/FrontLight/Headlamp/RunningLight/DRL/Lights</code>, so Fierro <code>FrontLights</code> and names with underscores do not fall out of contract. All matching meshes receive OFF appearance, but a separate L/R set remains the source for at most two generated road emitters.

### MainTrack personal-car activity [LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO — 2026-08-30]

The activity is available only to the owner actually occupying a permanent <code>PersonalVehicle</code>; dealer/debug/traffic cars are ineligible. On entering the vehicle outside the track, the client briefly displays <code>TRACK READY / DRIVE TO MAIN TRACK</code>. When the personal car is within 90 studs of the authored route, the state changes to <code>guide</code>, and a horizontal owner-private Beam with the standard arrow texture <code>rbxassetid://88195218049022</code> leads from the chassis to <code>Workspace.MainTrack.FinishLineArch.FinishLine</code>. Cash checkpoints are not created until the first passage through the arch. Passing through it in the <code>guide</code> state reserves the run and starts exactly **one lap**.

The route is based on authored marker parts <code>Workspace.MainTrack.CashPickups.01…24</code>. The side of the intersection of the starting arch determines whether the route order is forward or backward, so the player continues moving forward regardless of the entry side; the first runtime position is forcibly set exactly 40 studs in front of the finish in the direction of movement. <code>CashPickup</code> remains a visual source model and is cloned at runtime to <code>ReplicatedStorage.TrackCashPickupTemplate</code>. The client stores the route, but simultaneously creates only three owner-private visuals from server-authoritative <code>activeIndices</code>; a rolling window advances along the nearest route progress, so missed points do not block the loop and become inactive. The server does not trust <code>Touched</code> or client messages and accepts only the active point whose X/Z coordinates fall within the oriented horizontal footprint <code>Model:GetBoundingBox()</code> of the player's personal car, expanded by 7 studs. The Y coordinate is intentionally ignored, and server-side <code>collected</code> set ensures a single payment per index. After passing the route gate, the exit from the arch must first be re-armed; the next passage through the finish completes the loop upon <code>collectedCount ≥ 12</code> and triggers a cooldown. When 11 or fewer points are collected, the server publishes <code>minimum_required</code>, returns the rolling window to the first uncollected segment of the route, and leaves the run active for the next lap. Exiting the vehicle pauses the active run; switching or despawning the personal car ends it; previously collected money is not reversed, and the cooldown, reserved at startup, prevents retrying the attempt.

Each of the at most three active points has a client-private vertical green <code>Beam</code> and a soft <code>PointLight</code>. The reward <code>BillboardGui</code> is drawn <code>AlwaysOnTop</code>, sized 160×52 px, with a large 28 px <code>+$value</code> and a contrasting outline, so the beacon does not cover the amount.

The reward is captured at the start and is identical for all 24 points of the current run.

<code>roundTo25(125 × clamp(sqrt(personalMarketPrice / 120000), 1, 10) × clamp(1 + log10(cashMultiplier), 1, 3))</code>.

Here <code>cashMultiplier</code> — the current business-income multiplier per built pads. Minimum — $125 per point; Rusty $120,000 at multiplier 1 yields $3,000 per full lap. This represents consumer value for personal car ownership and business growth, but is not yet a direct consumer readout parameter <code>topSpeedKph/handlingRating/accelerationRating</code>. Each point is accrued through <code>TycoonService</code> and saved; completion updates <code>personalGarage.trackCooldownUntil = os.time() + 600</code> via critical commit. Cooldown is also reserved by the critical commit until the first reward is issued, so leave/rejoin does not create a new attempt. <code>TrackRunStarted</code>, <code>TrackLapCompleted</code> and <code>TrackRunCompleted</code> enter gameplay analytics.

<code>TrackActivityGui</code> has <code>DisplayOrder=900</code>: OWNER DEBUG, Phone and garage/customize modals are drawn above it. On desktop card it has 300×46 px, anchored in the top-right and does not overlap the central cash/luck HUD. On touch card it has 220×42 px, short copy, and automatically hides passive <code>available/guide/cooldown</code> states after 3–4 seconds; the full run HUD remains compact. On touch tablet outside the vehicle, the card is positioned to the right at <code>Y=84</code>, i.e. below Objective; when any seat is occupied, the card changes anchor to <code>(0.5,0)</code> and is positioned at <code>(0.5,76)</code> between the steering wheel and the right vehicle cluster. This is a mandatory placement contract: the track HUD must not overlap <code>Exit</code>, <code>Nitro</code>, <code>Gas</code>, <code>Brake</code> or <code>HandBrake</code>. In <code>OWNER DEBUG → Money & profile</code> there is <code>RESET TRACK COOLDOWN</code>. The button has no client authority: <code>DebugService</code> rechecks the admin allowlist, records cooldown 0 via critical commit, and publishes the updated state.

### Progression-weighted arrivals

All stock sources — world seller, phone fence and Tow Truck — trigger one <code>DealerCarConfig.selectArrival(pads, luck)</code>. Compatibility <code>pickCar</code> accepts the old signature but ignores the previous <code>carIndex</code>; round-robin is no longer a live selector. First, the progression tier sets class shares, then server-authoritative Luck rebalances only the already unlocked classes, after which <code>spawnWeight</code> divides the class share among actually available models:

| Arrival tier | Functional gate | Low | Mid | High | Hyper |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| <code>starter</code> | before Repair Bay | 100% | 0% | 0% | 0% |
| <code>repair</code> | <code>OpenRepairBay</code> | 70% | 30% | 0% | 0% |
| <code>wash</code> | <code>CW_BrushMachine</code> | 45% | 55% | 0% | 0% |
| <code>tuning</code> | any of the three Tuning stands | 20% | 45% | 35% | 0% |
| <code>fullTuning</code> | all three Tuning stands | 10% | 35% | 55% | 0% |
| <code>fullService</code> | both Repair lifts, both Wash brushes and all three Tuning stands | 10% | 34.5% | 55% | 0.5% |

Seller UI displays the calculated value for the current profile <code>% ARRIVAL</code>, not the catalog <code>1 IN N</code>. <code>rarityOneInN</code> remains metadata/readout for the catalog; this is not a general selection formula. In deterministic test <code>25,000</code> samples per each of the six tiers matched the analytical shares; fullService Zentauri gave 0.492% sample against exact 0.5%, and the tier without <code>RB2_CarLift</code> had 0% hyper.

Car Index: the active client surface belongs to <code>StarterGui.SellCarsHUD.Client</code>; legacy <code>CarIndexClient</code> is preserved but disabled. After tutorial, the Phone slot is active for personal desktop and occupies the second visible rail slot immediately below Car Index; Home and separate Garage slots remain absent/hidden. Inside Phone <code>GARAGE</code> has <code>LayoutOrder=1</code>, and <code>BUY CARS</code> has <code>LayoutOrder=2</code>; Garage becomes visible after starter selection. Workshop Car Index uses a single-column catalog, expanded <code>ViewportFrame</code> with visible-body bounds and pseudo-orthographic camera FOV 18. The only source of its 11 strings is <code>DealerCarConfig.catalogCars()</code>: a separate hardcoded Workshop catalog and fixed total no longer exists. Strings are deterministically grouped LOW→MID→HIGH→HYPER, so Mercurion remains in MID; the counter shows the actually open vehicles from server-owned <code>CarIndexState</code> relative to current 11 catalog cars. LOW entries have gray accent and are open from the start; locked entries remain in the list as <code>UNKNOWN VEHICLE / LOCKED</code> without model, price, or stats. There is no CTA <code>VIEW THIS VEHICLE</code>: the open card is read-only and shows only class, Ask, Market Value and three progress-bar parameters from <code>DealerCarConfig</code> — <code>topSpeedKph</code>, <code>handlingRating</code>, <code>accelerationRating</code>. Server-side unlock and persisted <code>carIndexSeen</code> still belong to <code>CarIndexService</code>; Personal Garage market uses the same catalog/gates but separate ownership/prices. Opening Workshop <code>Car Index</code> or <code>Settings</code> requires snapshot-and-suppress <code>TopHUD</code>, <code>MobileSprintGui</code>, <code>OwnerDebugPanel</code> and Objective; closing restores the exact previous <code>Visible/Enabled</code> values. Left rail and compact <code>?</code> remain navigational edge elements.

Mobile HUD contract: <code>SellCarsHUD</code> maintains fullscreen presentation, but the interactive left rail computes a local offset from <code>GuiService:GetInsetArea(DeviceSafeInsets)</code> and the actual <code>Root.AbsolutePosition</code>, so Car Index does not fall under Dynamic Island. When short edge ≤600, the directional guide <code>NewPurchase</code> is limited to <code>88×88</code>. When viewport ≤760 in width or ≤430 in height, the central real <code>HUD.Powers</code>/<code>Buy Next</code> has <code>92×92</code> instead of desktop <code>170×170</code>. Objective uses responsive <code>UIScale</code> desktop/tablet/phone 1.0/0.7/0.5 and is fixed in the top-right corner across all profiles. This is a single thin action-row without a progress bar/counter; on phone its Y position is taken from live <code>CashPanel.AbsolutePosition</code>, and the runtime width is approximately 170 px. In collapsed state, the entire panel is hidden and only <code>+</code> remains; the visual 22×22 control has its own 44×44 touch target. <code>MobileSprintGui.SprintButton</code> uses Roblox <code>TouchControlsSheetV2</code>, a white circle and label <code>RUN</code>, which dynamically positions itself to the left of live <code>JumpButton</code> with a minimum gap of 12 px and has dimensions 58…76 px; when landing or during any active <code>MVPDetailsOpen</code>/<code>MVPDealModal</code>/<code>ActiveInspectionCard</code>/<code>SellCarsWorkshopModalOpen</code> it is hidden and resets held sprint. The top Cash sum uses <code>TextSize=58</code>. The actual <code>StarterGui.ScreenOrientation=LandscapeSensor</code>, so standard phone mode is only landscape; each mobile layout must idempotently reapply direction, sizes, padding, and canvas on every viewport change. The portrait branch is preserved as a defensive Studio/device fallback: TopHUD 280×60 is positioned in the top-right under safe area, Cash has 218×60 and 28 px amount, gear is 56×56; the left rail is positioned to the left under Roblox CoreGui and provides Car Index/Phone via a 64×64 touch zone. Settings displays a unified version <code>V3.05</code> muted text in the bottom-right.

Models located in Storage or concept gallery do not automatically become dealer stock. To add a vehicle, at least a config entry, a correct full model, and compatible visual/traffic assets are required.

### Repair value

| Part | Addition to value |
|---|---:|
| Body | +$40 |
| Wheels | +$35 |
| Engine | +$55 |
| **Total** | **+$130** |

Manual hold — 1.2 seconds per part. The mechanic processes a missing part approximately in <code>3 / repairMultiplier</code> seconds and yields if the player has already started manual repair.

### Formulas

<code>partsValue = сумма repaired parts</code>

<code>serviceMultiplier = произведение множителей всех completed owner-service stages</code>

<code>calculatedValue = (baseValue + partsValue) × serviceMultiplier</code>

<code>listingMarkup = max(0, valueMultiplier − 1)</code>

<code>listedPrice = floor(calculatedValue × (1 + listingMarkup))</code>

<code>ownedOperatingCost = round(10% × созданный этапом value)</code>

<code>finalProfit = finalOffer − purchaseCost − ownedOperatingCost</code>

| Owner stage | Cost multiplier | Persisted plan rule |
|---|---:|---|
| Repair Bay | ×1.50 | 100% only for <code>condition.oilLeak=true</code>; a vehicle without oil leak passes through Repair Bay |
| Car Wash | ×1.15 | 100% only for <code>condition.dirty=true</code>; a dirty car cannot be washed |
| Dyno tune | ×1.35 | 100%, if stage is built and available |
| Body kit | ×1.30 | 100%, if stage is built and available |
| Paint + finish | ×1.20 | 100%, if stage is built and available |

The plan is built once during the first successful planning pass of a specific <code>Ready tradeId</code>. RNG and upgrade-scaled optional chances no longer determine whether owned stock receives tuning: all built Dyno/Body/Paint are included in canonical order, Repair is determined solely by persisted <code>oilLeak</code>, and Wash is determined only by persisted <code>dirty</code>. Insufficient pipeline capacity delays planning; a previously saved plan is not reloaded during polling, normal rejoin, or subsequent purchases. Compatibility normalization marks excess Repair/Wash in the old saved plan as <code>condition_clear</code>/<code>condition_clean</code>, without issuing a completed multiplier or operating cost; such stages are not displayed on the selected route. The full vehicle chain, including wear and tear, yields <code>1.50 × 1.15 × 1.35 × 1.30 × 1.20 = 3.63285×</code> before listing markup.

The price remains frozen during listing. Any owned stage yields zero station payout: revenue cannot be earned servicing one's own vehicle. Instead, each JunkSpot part repair and each successfully completed Repair/Wash/Tuning stage incurs an operating cost equal to 10% of the value it creates. Available Cash is deducted immediately; in case of shortage, the remainder is held in <code>serviceCost − serviceCostPaid</code> and retained until the authoritative sale settlement, so a zero balance does not block physical pipeline progression. The profit card separately displays <code>BOUGHT / SERVICE / SOLD / PROFIT</code>, where profit always includes the full cost of operations. Street/customer jobs retain their previous immediate station payout. After Accept world billboard for the same vehicle, the middle row changes to green <code>SOLD</code> and the bottom row to actual gross <code>finalOffer</code>; the cash popup shows the actual sale payout after deduction of the debt.

One-time plan/empty decision and each successful owner stage proceed through <code>DataService.commitCritical</code> until physical detach or return of the vehicle. A temporary claim slot lease uses routine save and safely restores to queued state in the absence of a live slot.

Current buyer fallback:

- tutorial Rusty: fixed $1,000;
- normal sale: <code>floor(listedPrice × 0.92 × wealthMultiplier × (1 + rarityOfferBonus))</code>. Wealth multiplier equals <code>0.92 / 1.00 / 0.85 / 1.50 / 2.50</code> for <code>POOR / BUYER / FENCE / HIGH CLASS / SUPER RICH</code>; rarity bonus equals <code>0% / 3% / 8% / 15% / 25%</code> for <code>COMMON / UNCOMMON / RARE / EPIC / LEGENDARY</code>. The only tutorial deal Rusty uses a fixed $1,000 and does not receive wealth/rarity multiplier.

No script registers an external <code>TradeContract.setResolver</code>, so the fallback is the actual behavior.

Each open P-slot has an independent buyer lane. Claim stores a server timestamp; the global watchdog calls arm every 3 seconds for all players. A <code>Listed</code>-claim older than 30 seconds is released and re-planned only if there is no live buyer model and no pending TradeContract. <code>Offer</code>/<code>Sold</code> watchdog does not reset. Therefore, a lost delayed task/path cannot permanently leave the car in <code>Listed</code>, and P1–P6 continue selling in parallel.

Physical buyers display-car are created separately via <code>LotNpc.spawnBuyer</code> as R15, while driveway sellers and the rest of the authored townsfolk cast retain their original R6 rigs. The R15 buyer-template is lazily cached by <code>personId</code> and transfers actual <code>ShirtTemplate</code>, <code>PantsTemplate</code>, ShirtGraphic, BodyColors, face decal, and all head elements of both formats — modern <code>Accessory</code> and legacy <code>Hat/Accoutrement</code>. Two corrupted records from the original pack have a clear same-pack fallback: <code>BigMike</code> takes the full outfit <code>TowTruckTony</code>, <code>NewbieNora</code> has missing shirt <code>Marla</code>; original face, skin, and hair are preserved. After successful server <code>trySettleTrade</code>, but before boarding/drive-away, the buyer completely loses the one-shot R15 animation <code>customer_energized</code> (<code>112387112633214</code>, Action4, 5 seconds, non-looping), then the existing seat/drive contract replaces it with the drive pose.

### Negotiations

- Buy-side HAGGLE: once; on success reduces ask by approximately 12%; base chance 65%; a specific Townsfolk may override it.
- Sell-side ASK MORE: once; uses <code>person.askMore</code> or fallback +12%.
- In the current fallback, a positive ASK MORE increases the offer without realizing the risk of buyer departure.
- REFUSE to a buyer returns the car to Listed and triggers the next buyer.

### Luck, rarity and wealth: live consumer

Car selection uses progression class weights and per-template <code>spawnWeight</code>, then the persisted server-authoritative <code>Luck</code> 1–5 reweights only already opened classes. Class multipliers: low <code>1/√Luck</code>, mid <code>Luck^0.35</code>, high <code>Luck^1.15</code>, hyper <code>Luck^2</code>. Luck never bypasses pad/facility gate, so an early profile cannot obtain a closed hypercar solely due to luck. The field <code>rarityOneInN</code> remains catalog/readout metadata and does not override the computed <code>% ARRIVAL</code> of the current profile.

A new profile starts with <code>Luck=1</code>, HUD shows <code>LUCK · NORMAL</code>. Levels 2/3/4–5 are named <code>LUCKY / VERY LUCKY / LEGENDARY</code>. Base chances of buyer classes under Luck 1: HIGH CLASS 15% with payout multiplier <code>1.5×</code>, SUPER RICH 3% with <code>2.5×</code>. Luck increases them via formulas <code>min(15% × Luck, 65%)</code> and <code>min(3% × Luck^1.5, 25%)</code>; buyer budget eligibility is preserved.

Incoming stock may already have custom paint, tint/body state and neon. Base chance of at least one mod: common 0%, uncommon 5%, rare 22%, epic 48%, legendary 75%; each Luck above 1 adds 8 percentage points, with a total cap of 95%. Rare/uncommon receive at most one mod, epic up to two, legendary up to three. Each mod increases seller ask by 10%, and already completed tuning stages are preserved in the owner pipeline and are not re-executed. Queue, Tow Truck, phone/world seller presentation, and paid stock preservation transfer the exact variant. The current body kit is visual state/tint plus neon contract, not a body geometry swap.

> [OPEN DECISION] The source of Luck increase for a player has not yet been selected. Runtime, persistence, HUD, and all consumers are ready, but currently a normal profile remains on Luck 1; separately, it must be decided whether Luck is granted through purchase pad/building, achievement, rebirth/prestige, temporary boost, or a combination thereof.

### Stock sources

On fresh tutorial after Cash Register and before hiring FirstSalesman, there is only one deterministic world-seller deal with Rusty; the phone fence is not created during this period. For a migrated post-tutorial profile where FirstSalesman has not yet been purchased, a legacy mix is preserved:

- 75% — live world seller;
- 25% — phone fence;
- fence offers three options and refreshes approximately every 10 seconds;
- live seller gap is approximately 7–14 seconds, patience is about 60 seconds;
- seller ask is usually <code>floor(baseCost × uniform(0.90, 1.25))</code>.

The purchase of <code>FirstSalesman</code> is a hard boundary for sourcing channel: the existing fence session is cleared, the delayed refresh rechecks ownership, and new phone offers are no longer created or restored. After this, only the physical world-seller queue operates.

After purchasing <code>RB_TowTruckDriver</code>, a second physical source periodically adds exactly one progression/luck-weighted vehicle to the same FIFO through the same <code>selectArrival(pads, luck)</code>. The tow truck reserves a channel in advance and only returns when the queue is unloaded to a safe level; a regular seller pump cannot steal this slot. Delivery preserves the standard ask with a variant markup, exact seller, and the subsequent BUY/repair/listing flow — the delivered vehicle is not free. A successful towing service only awards the owner a station payout after an authoritative insertion of the exact cargo into the FIFO. The total queue capacity remains 3.

Phone-fence selection uses actions <code>pick1…pick3</code>; the server whitelist accepts them strictly based on <code>FENCE_OFFER_COUNT</code> and forwards them to the exact fence-session. This channel exists only until FirstSalesman is purchased.

## 5. Progression and all active purchase pads [LIVE]

In the fresh <code>TycoonConfig.PADS</code> there are **159 authored rows**, but the runtime pad is active only when:

<code>MVP_ALLOWED_PADS[id] == true</code> and <code>mergedInto == nil</code>.

The actual result is **153 active pads** with a total value of **$8,432,300**. Visible builds such as Repair Bay, both Car Washes, and Tuning Atelier are purchased in separate stages. Junk storage, Repair Bay warehouses, and the second bay remain optional parallel branches and do not block the main business ladder.

A locked pad is hidden, while an open pad defaults to preserving the canonical color and <code>$PRICE</code>/<code>FREE</code> independently of the current Cash. A blocked presentation is enabled **only after the physical presence of a local owner**, when the server rejects a purchase of this exact pad due to insufficient funds and sends through the existing <code>InsufficientFunds</code> a structured DTO <code>{ surface="purchasePad", padId, pad, needed, title, reason="funds" }</code>. Only this pad receives a gray top purchase-surface <code>RGB(115,119,124)</code>/<code>SmoothPlastic</code> and a standard line <code>Price = NEED $N MORE</code>; the black background does not get repainted. Changing Cash recalculates only already armed pads. When Cash ≥ cost, closure/purchase, owner ID change, or deletion removes the blocked state and precisely restores the authored font/color/scaling/wrapping/visibility, Color, and Material. <code>Buy Next</code> and station upgrade cannot color the world pad. The existing compact two-line insufficient-funds notification after touch is preserved. <code>PersonalGarageSecondBay</code> publishes the same <code>PurchasePadId/Cost/Open/Effect</code> contract, uses a standard 150×56 billboard, <code>GothamBlack</code> title, <code>FredokaOne</code> price, and server constant $2,500,000; its server-authoritative touch purchase remains unchanged and sends the same exact touch DTO. There are no permanent world effect labels above open pads. Only after a successful purchase does an authoritative <code>effectText</code> appear next to the pad in CashPopup style — <code>FredokaOne</code>, green <code>RGB(120,255,150)</code>, black outline, and no background/green UIStroke — rises to 5 studs over 1.1 seconds and dissolves in the last 0.45 seconds.

After manual sale of tutorial Rusty objective and world guide, it is recommended to proceed with <code>RepairPad2 → Parking2 → SecondMechanic → OpenRepairBay</code>. Here <code>RepairPad2</code> is the second working W-slot, and <code>Parking2</code> is the second sales <code>SALE SPOT</code>; these concepts are not merged into a single pad or identical UI copy. In early progression, separate Assistant, Buying Salesman, and Selling Salesman are not introduced; <code>UsedCarsSalesman</code> remains merged in <code>FirstSalesman</code>, and buyer auto-accept is enabled only after manual tutorial close.

Chapter: $7,630,000 and 40 separate stages. After <code>TA_BodyKitStation</code> at position 15, two independent available branches diverge: Body/Paint continuation and Admin, starting at <code>TA_AdminFoundation</code> on position 16. The Admin chain proceeds <code>Foundation → Walls → Furniture → ServiceAdvisor → OperationsManager</code>; it does not block the Body/Paint path. <code>TA_Compressor</code> remains a formal completion pad and follows Admin Furniture in editorial order. Simulation <code>nextMandatoryPad</code> provides 98 guided purchases totaling $986,700 before Compressor; after formal completion, there remain $7,394,550 active optional purchases. Of these, 7,000,000 are Service Advisor and Operations Manager, i.e. 91.7% of the price of Chapter 6. Therefore, the current term means "business operational," but not a full business purchase. The main branch <code>TA_WarehouseRack1 → TA_WarehouseRack2</code> opens after Atelier ceiling; this is not Paint storage. Each purchase has its own green pad/build reveal and multiplies Dyno, Body, and Paint cash simultaneously: together <code>1.25 × 1.30 = 1.625</code>. Production contract — exactly two top-level purchase models, each containing exactly two <code>Shelf</code>: a total of **four shelves** in owner-authored layout 2×2 for Paint Booth. On Tycoon1, the centers of the first purchase pair: <code>(93.900, 4.780, 795.400)</code> and <code>(102.250, 4.780, 795.400)</code>; the second pair: <code>(93.900, 4.780, 806.607)</code> and <code>(102.250, 4.780, 806.607)</code>. It is not possible to clone the entire <code>WH1_Row1Model</code>: it contains three shelf sections and creates extra shelves. For transfer between plots and in <code>TuningAtelierTemplate</code>, the authoritative anchor is the actual <code>PaintBooth_Primitive.Foundation.FloorBase.CFrame</code>, and geometry is transferred part-by-part; <code>PaintBooth_Primitive:GetPivot()</code> and nested <code>Shelf:GetPivot()</code> cannot be used, as their saved pivots from plot sections have become outdated. Both warehouse pads clone the canonical horizontal orientation of tuning pads: a thin local X-axis is aligned with world Y (<code>|RightVector.Y|≈1</code>), so the pad lies on the floor rather than standing perpendicular to it.

**[RISK] Economic drift in Chapter 6:** each fully assembled Tuning Atelier line still exceeds the full Repair Bay in station payout ($89,375 / $95,214 / $109,688 versus $80,450), but no longer in stage-capsule bonus: +$67,375 / +$70,214 / +$79,688 below full Repair +$79,850. The previous double invariant is no longer satisfied. In this change set, the balance was intentionally not altered; prior to release, an owner decision is required as to whether the bonus presentation should again grow monotonically.

The paint service uses the exact owner-imported <code>Workspace.Spray Can2</code> as the tool source: the runtime clone <code>ReplicatedStorage.TuningPaintAssets.SprayCan2</code> preserves MeshId <code>98782154703516</code>, color/normal/metalness/roughness maps <code>98295689217945/74486237813807/84304588958817/107809333433063</code>, and owner-authored dimensions ≈<code>0.806×1.390×0.806</code>. Only in <code>WorkerServicing</code>/<code>PlayerServicing</code> is the can welded to the painter's working hand: from <code>Nozzle</code> comes <code>P_Spray</code> texture <code>105910893298328</code> (<code>Rate=15, Lifetime=1, Speed=10, Drag=3, Front</code>), and four surface points on the dominant body/chassis provide <code>P_Splash</code> texture <code>12572542650</code> (<code>Rate=5</code> each, the same Lifetime/Speed/Drag/Front). Both emitter types receive the current <code>ServicePaintHue</code>; upon completion of the paint phase, the tool, four splash attachments, and sound are deleted. <code>ReplicatedStorage.Assets.Sounds</code> contains <code>Spray Can Shake</code> <code>131821009892723</code> (<code>0.5/1.0</code>) and looping <code>spray_can</code> <code>9119522706</code> (<code>0.6/0.5</code>): the spray loop is verified to load, but shake is currently blocked by Roblox asset permission for experience and will activate after Share Access without changing the code.

The physical kit <code>PaintBooth_Primitive.PaintCanisters</code> is synchronized with the correct side of the Paint Booth in Tycoon1 as the canonical local layout on Tycoon2…Tycoon8 and <code>TuningAtelierTemplate</code>. All four display cans are now scaled copies of the exact <code>Spray Can2</code> MeshId/SurfaceAppearance; legacy cylinders, caps, and separate hose parts have been removed. The four instances differ by Hue/tint variants: red, orange/yellow, cyan, and magenta. A separately imported owner-authored <code>Workspace.Spray Can2</code> remains in place and is not moved or destroyed.

The neon underglow uses **11 separate owner-authored references** from <code>Workspace.EditorOnlyQueueSlots.TuningAtelierNeonReference</code>; the position Left → Front → Right → Rear cannot be derived from the overall bounding box or copied between models. All 44 CFrame/Size are saved in <code>ReplicatedStorage.VehicleNeon</code> relative to the root <code>Chassis.CFrame</code>. The runtime <code>LayoutVersion=4</code> applies them through the exact root chassis, not through <code>Model:GetPivot()</code>, since the bounding-box pivot has an individual offset and is particularly noticeable in shifting the tubes <code>Fierro Rosso</code> inward into the chassis. Checking all 11 models resulted in a maximum reproduction error of <code>0.000001 stud</code>.

### Bundles, merged and hidden pads

In live, only one compatibility bundle remains: OpenRepairBay builds the foundation and compatibly delivers legacy entitlement RB_Foundation. All visible Repair Bay, Car Wash, and Tuning Atelier models are purchased via separate pads and receive their own reveal/build animation.

Hidden/merged:

- <code>UsedCarsSalesman</code> → FirstSalesman;
- forecourt lights → post-tutorial capacity; <code>Parking2…Parking6</code> are separate active purchases;
- empty <code>RB_CarRepairPlace</code> without a model remains disabled;
- Repair Bay 2, Warehouse 1/2, and Junk storage are enabled as optional branches.

Do not show raw authored **159 rows** as the current staircase: only 153 are active based on <code>TycoonConfig.isPadEnabled</code>. The local HTML pad editor stores a dated snapshot PlaceVersion 967 with 142/136 and $8,216,000; this is historical evidence, not current truth. When the bundle ID or pad ID changes, simultaneously verify the config, DataService migration, all eight plot copies, and build reveal.

| Station/queue | Unlock/automation | Street base payout | Street full payout | Key mechanic |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| Sell Car Parts / Parts Sales Laptop | FirstWorkbench / FirstMechanic | $28 | depends on level | anti-softlock, manual then auto; maximum 20 |
| Repair Bay | RB_CarLift / Mechanic / Checkout Clerk | $600 | $80,450 | customer repair, persistent checkout reminder, then auto checkout; Clerk +25% income/+20% speed |
| Tow Truck | RB_TowTruckDriver | $300 | $40,225 | job-driven towing income only after exact FIFO delivery; exactly 50% Repair payout at max pads; no upgrade prompt |
| Car Wash 1 | Checkout Clerk + CW_BrushMachine | $2400 | $4680 | fully auto after Repair checkout automation |
| Car Wash 2 | Checkout Clerk + CW2_BrushMachine | $2400 | $4680 | independent auto lane |
| Tuning Dyno | TA_DynoRollers / Specialist / Service Advisor | $22,000 | $89,375 | manual/auto work; Advisor auto-checkout; Operations Manager +25% |
| Tuning Body | TA_BodyKitStation / Specialist / Service Advisor | $25,000 | $95,214 | separate queue; shared admin automation/bonus |
| Tuning Paint | TA_PaintControl / Specialist / Service Advisor | $30,000 | $109,688 | separate queue; shared admin automation/bonus |

After Checkout Clerk, the full Repair Bay pays <code>$80,450</code> at a base payout of <code>$600</code>; the stage-capsule bonus equals <code>+$79,850</code>. Tow — is a separate station-zone, but Repair cash pads enter it through <code>bonusZones</code>, and the Clerk gives the same +25% income; therefore, the full Tow pays exactly half — <code>$40,225</code> at a base of 300, bonus <code>+$39,925</code>. Full Tuning lines after Operations Manager still pay <code>$89,375 / $95,214 / $109,688</code>, but their stage-capsule bonuses <code>+$67,375 / +$70,214 / +$79,688</code> are now lower than Repair <code>+$79,850</code>; this is clearly fixed [RISK], not a hidden invariant claim. Car Wash remains unchanged.

### Quick Fix [SUPERSEDED 2026-08-27]

- level 1: $14 approximately in 5 seconds;
- payout: <code>14 + 8 × (level − 1)</code>;
- max level 10;
- upgrade price: <code>floor(220 × 1.35^(level − 1))</code>;
- CarJack accelerates the cycle;
- FirstMechanic automates;
- serves as a guaranteed anti-softlock income;
- when current Cash is insufficient even for the guaranteed minimum purchase of a basic car, a client-only hint highlights the built <code>SimpleWorkbench</code> and suggests performing a Quick Fix; the hint automatically disappears after Cash is restored.

### Selling Car Parts [LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO — 2026-08-27]

The visible <code>Quick Fix Job</code> is completely replaced, but the existing server-authoritative <code>MoneyStation</code> is retained as technical foundation for the anti-softlock loop. The purchased object is called <code>PARTS SALES LAPTOP</code>, and the station action is <code>SELL CAR PARTS</code>: this copy simultaneously explains the physical laptop and the player's action. On the current workbench for all Tycoon1–8, an exact clone <code>ChipTuningLaptopStation.Props1.ChipTuning.laptop</code> from Tuning Atelier is installed. The player manually performs <code>SELL PARTS</code>, and after purchasing FirstMechanic, the employee works at the station with base animation <code>idle_working_computer</code> (<code>116122076689674</code>). Laptop is anchored, massless, and does not participate in collision/touch/query. In 2.50 the Billboard is raised from <code>StudsOffsetWorldSpace.Y=4</code> to <code>7</code>, so that the upgrade/progress UI does not cover the player or mechanic silhouette at the compact workbench.

Live payout equals exactly double the previous curve: <code>28 + 16 × (level − 1)</code>, that is, $28 at level 1, $172 at level 10 and $332 at level 20. Maximum equals 20. Upgrade price remains <code>floor(220 × 1.35^(level − 1))</code>, cycle duration is about five seconds, CarJack continues to accelerate it. Visible copy: <code>PARTS SALES LAPTOP</code>, <code>SELL CAR PARTS</code>, <code>SELL PARTS</code>; the line <code>QUICK FIX</code> in the active station and billboard labels are removed. 'Auction sale' is purely fiction and does not create inventory, bidding, or player marketplace. Play confirmed <code>basePayout=28</code>, <code>payoutPerLevel=16</code>, <code>maxLevel=20</code> and eight safe laptop instances.

### Repair Bay

Bay Mechanic automates lift/work and uses repairEngine animation; a manual HoodServicePrompt triggers the player's repair animation. During HoodServicing, the vehicle receives a loop VFX, and upon completion — a separate burst. While the vehicle is waiting for checkout, a constant <code>CHECK OUT CAR</code> remains above the checkout point. The Checkout Clerk automatically completes this stage. Raise/service/checkout and payout require server-authored JobId/occupancy, the existing TrafficCars template, full ServiceGeometry, and server-confirmed vehicle arrival. An empty lift is not serviced and does not pay. Inbound and reverse/outbound ground paths are projected once onto the whitelist of real Streets, ParkingSlab/ParkingBase, FrontApproachRamp, and RampDriveSurface. Then <code>TycoonServiceClient</code> raycasts each wheel, fits the overall pitch/roll to the contact plane, and minimally corrects Y; movable lift pads and station ramps are considered based on actual collidable surfaces, but the lift height remains a separate service phase. The support size is calculated as the spin-invariant cylinder radius, not the rotating AABB of the wheels.

Both Repair slots use a full three-point exit: P1–P4 backward with nose turn, P5–P7 forward on the eastbound road. In Bay 1, the previous P4→P5 turn almost in place has been removed; Bay 2 also received a more even steering seam. All <code>112</code> P1–P7 markers (2 slots × 8 plots × 7) are synchronized in <code>Workspace.ServiceRouteMarkers.Tycoon1…Tycoon8</code>; code fallback arrays <code>REPAIR_BAY1_EXIT_LOCAL</code> / <code>REPAIR_BAY2_EXIT_LOCAL</code> match them. After P7, the client enters RoadGraph in the direction of exit (node 218 east, first arc 218 → 140), not the nearest node 217 west of P7. The owner waiting for the next pipeline stage stays at the end of reverse P4 until handoff. During actual <code>WAITING FOR TRAFFIC</code>, the controller freezes position, yaw, and wheel spin: the vehicle does not rotate in place until <code>trafficBlocks</code> confirms a real obstruction.

Final road seams for Used Cars are a separate authored contract in <code>Workspace.ServiceRouteMarkers.Tycoon1…Tycoon8.LinkOverrides</code>. <code>RepairToUsedCarsApproach</code> preserves the transfer from Tycoon1 <code>RB→UC 14</code> to <code>(556.300, 683.000)</code>, and <code>AtelierToUsedCarsApproach</code> — the transfer from <code>TA→UC 50</code> to <code>(556.400, 683.000)</code>; the remaining seven plots receive the same plot-local transform. These points replace the previous last RoadGraph sample at X=528, rather than being added after it. The finalized owner vehicle after Repair only reaches the authored <code>RB→UC</code> seam and there it transfers to a detached parking journey; the traffic-only tunnel tail that exits the map and takes the customer car away from the map is not added to the owner run. <code>RoadTraffic.displayParkingPlan</code> initiates the final Used Cars bridge from the approach, then proceeds through the gate and central passage to reserved P. The reverse direction has a different meaning: <code>UsedLotEntranceReference</code> and debug labels <code>UC→TA Entrance</code>/<code>RB→UC Entrance</code>/<code>TA→UC Entrance</code> are only input destination points and are never inserted as the first waypoint of the helper that picks up the vehicle from Junk Spot or Used Cars; the collection route begins at the vehicle's actual position and selects the nearest valid RoadGraph join. For the origin stage <code>junk</code>, before this join there exists a separate 34-stud reverse phase along <code>-LookVector</code> W-vehicle, so the proxy first physically releases the Junk Spot/fence and only then turns forward onto the road.

Surface-fit preserves actual elevations and slopes, but does not accumulate pitch/roll between frames: <code>surfaceAlignedFrame</code> each call starts with a horizontal yaw from the current frame and builds a slope once per frame using four whitelisted wheel probes. Per-frame slope clamp remains at 18°. During actual vehicle body intersection, older-dispatch arbitration uses a dynamic threshold <code>clamp((widthA+widthB)/2+0.5, 5.5, 10.5)</code>; adjacent lanes remain independent, and same-lane gap serves <code>SERVICE_TRAFFIC_GAP</code>. A 45-second play profile of 8 models/2318 samples achieved the maximum surface angle <code>10.58°</code> on the Dyno ramp and did not detect a simultaneously blocked pair within the vehicle body threshold.

The Checkout Clerk has an authored desk pose <code>CheckoutDesk_v4_ComputerSitBlend</code>: the root is attached to <code>ChairSeat</code>, and the direction is taken from <code>Keyboard</code> with a fallback to <code>AdminDeskTop</code>. For the AnimationConstraint, only <code>HumanoidRootPart</code> is anchored; all other parts of R15 are freed from <code>Anchored</code>, disconnected from collision/query/touch, and remain massless. <code>StaffAnimationController</code> simultaneously plays a looping base animation <code>idle_working_computer</code> (<code>116122076689674</code>, Action) and an independent lower-body overlay <code>sit_legs</code> (<code>99092341273470</code>, Action2); public APIs are <code>PlayBaseAnimation</code>, <code>PlayOverlay</code>, <code>StopOverlay</code>, and <code>StopAll</code>. The head and <code>BodyColors.HeadColor3</code> use the same <code>#D8AA5E</code> as the mechanics. In <code>CO_DeskModel</code> a four-part <code>CheckoutComputer</code> has been added; the internal countertop, its legs, and the Cash Register are moved toward the chair by 1.35 studs, resulting in a final horizontal center-to-center gap of approximately 3.005 studs. The geometry and authored content for Tycoon1…Tycoon8 and <code>ServerStorage.RepairBay_New_Template</code> are implemented, and the entire desk-folder is still built on pad <code>CO_ComputerDesk</code>.

After deepening Tycoon1 Repair Bay in 1.47, its authored Bay 2 exit was reconnected to the road; version 1.93 replaced this Tycoon1-only contract with a smooth P1–P7 route shared across eight plots for both Repair slots. P7 remains on the eastbound side of the city road, the Y-coordinate of each segment is projected onto the actual pavement.

The physical owner queue before the Repair Bay shows two positions for each actually open repairing slot. Before purchase <code>RB2_Foundation</code>, only Q1/Q2 of the first box exist; Q3/Q4 of the second box are not replicated or rendered, even if additional vehicles are present in the persisted backlog. After opening Repair Bay 2, these positions appear automatically, and the backlog remains server-authoritative and is never lost. Critical W → processing commit immediately transfers the exact car to the persisted queue, but the client no longer teleports the helper to the anchor Repair Bay. The next clone starts at JunkSpot W and travels along the local front-road route to QueueFront/QueueRear; status <code>DRIVING TO REPAIR BAY</code>, then <code>WAITING FOR REPAIR BAY</code>. The server does not start the owner Repair job until the time of this journey has expired (<code>OWNER_REPAIR_DRIVE_IN_MIN…MAX</code>, speed <code>OWNER_QUEUE_DRIVE_SPEED</code> = 22, without speed-pad). The service run after arrival consists only of a short driveway outer→apron→lift; catch-up no longer blocks Raising/Hood. Otherwise, speed pads would have compressed the work to <code>MIN_PHASE_TIME</code> even on approach, making the vehicle appear instantly repaired. The route through <code>RepairBayEntranceReference</code> inside Used Cars is forbidden: it would cut the vehicle through dealership fences and back.

In all owner queue/service proxy instances, an exact authored appearance of a reserved <code>ServiceHelper</code>, <code>ServiceHelper2</code> or <code>ServiceHelper3</code> is used: shirt, pants, BodyColors, face, and accessories are synchronized via <code>ServiceEscortId</code>, and the asynchronous friend-avatar loader is not allowed to overwrite them. As long as at least one locally visible queue/service proxy uses a specific assistant, only their idle copy is hidden by a reference-counted mechanism; after the final task is completed, it is restored. This is the exact same employee in visual contract, not a generic black placeholder standing next to a static original.

### Tow Truck

<code>RB_TowTruckDriver</code> starts a server-only cycle <code>Boarding → Departing → Offsite → ReturningWithCar → Delivering → ReturningHome → Cooldown</code>. <code>ROUTING_V2_TOW=true</code>; Tycoon1 stores owner-authored forward P1–P5 <code>(430.890,3.998,752.492) → (430.782,2.999,739.712) → (430.647,2.999,723.767) → (430.489,3.247,705.105) → (434.154,3.123,690.504)</code>, which are transferred to Tycoon2…8 through the plot-local W1 frame. Catmull-Rom/densify limits the step to ≤3 studs and interpolates entry/exit without sharp seams. The authored truck's parking orientation is already directed toward the driveway, so departure proceeds along P1–P5 in front: on the apron, speed does not exceed 18; after exiting onto the road — normal <code>TOW_DRIVE_SPEED</code>. Return uses a dense long RoadGraph path, and late-Q correction remains curved/dense; final snap is only allowed when error ≤0.2 stud and ≤5°. After the delivery truck passes the apron, it stands in setup position before the original location and briefly parks backwards through <code>towHomePlan</code>.

Tow income is a separate job-driven station-zone <code>tow</code>, because two hosts in the same zone <code>repair</code> would conflict in <code>StationService.hosts[record]</code>. Host is a decorative <code>Buildings.RepairBay.Building.RB1_TowTruckModel.TowTruck.Car_Body</code>; the existing <code>MoneyStationClient</code> displays above it <code>TOWING SERVICE</code>, payout, and a synchronized Repair stage capsule. Base payout equals $300; level-up is absent, and Repair cash pads through <code>bonusZones={"tow"}</code> provide the same multiplier. Therefore, Tow payout for any identical set of cash pads is half of Repair payout; on a fully developed profile, this is $40,225 versus $80,450, and bonuses +$39,925 versus +$79,850.

Cargo remains without a driver only as long as it is physically hanging on the hook. After unloading <code>IncomingSellerQueue</code> joins the exact previously saved seller with verified <code>PersonId</code>; rejoin/promotion guard blocks mismatch until FIFO mutation. Therefore, Tow-delivered owner offer no longer appears as an empty vehicle or with a different seller.

Irreversible boundary payout — successful <code>IncomingSellerQueue.enqueueTowDelivery</code>. Only after this does <code>TowTruckService</code> trigger <code>StationService.completeServiceJob(record, "tow:" .. queueId, "tow")</code>; exact <code>queueId</code> serves as an idempotency key, so abort/offsite retry does not pay. UI/host unavailability does not roll back already delivered cargo: service refresh is performed once and the error is logged separately.

### Car Wash

The Checkout Clerk opens the Car Wash as a business, and the BrushMachine activates a specific lane. This gate is checked both in pad progression and server service requirements, including old saves. For Street/StationCash, the full station multiplier is: <code>1.2 × 1.25 × 1.3 = 1.95</code>, payout $4680. The Owner/CarValue uses a separate stage multiplier ×1.15 and does not receive a station payout. The two lanes are independent. Side brushes move and rotate in the local axes of the model, so building rotation does not break the animation.

Materials and local lighting for both lanes are governed by the contract <code>LightingContractVersion=CarWashBalancedLight_v2</code>. Red-blue <code>L_brush</code>/<code>R_brush</code>/<code>Top_Brush</code> use a matte <code>Fabric</code> with a neutral tint <code>RGB(140,140,142)</code>, while floor <code>LeftGuide</code>/<code>RightGuide</code> are <code>SmoothPlastic</code> color <code>RGB(220,174,20)</code>; these elements are not light sources. Three ceiling <code>DownLight</code> on the lane have <code>Brightness=1.5</code>, <code>Range=30</code> and <code>Angle=100°</code>, and their panels remain <code>SmoothPlastic</code>. Range once again covers approximately 22.5-stud distance from ceiling to floor, so the space is illuminated, but bright textures do not cross the global Bloom threshold and remain readable without a glowing halo. The contract is synchronized across both lanes Tycoon1…Tycoon8 and in <code>ServerStorage.CarWash_UPD_AuthoredSource</code>/<code>CarWash_UPD_RuntimeTemplate</code>; global <code>Lighting</code> and <code>BloomEffect</code> have not changed.

Both service doors in Car Wash repeat the interactive contract <code>WorkingSideDoor</code> from Tuning Atelier. <code>PersonnelDoor</code> of the first line and <code>CarWash2_PersonnelDoor</code> of the second line automatically open when any player approaches within 8 studs and close after exiting the radius: <code>DoorPanel</code>, <code>DoorWindow</code> and <code>DoorHandle</code> shift by 0.35 seconds to 4.75 studs **from the handle**, while the threshold and blue edges remain stationary. The movable assembly is authored at 0.55 studs in front of the outer wall surface, so when opening it slides along the facade rather than inside the wall; the side is determined separately based on the position of DriveSurface, since the second line is mirrored. Next to the handle outside there is a green Neon <code>DoorKeypad</code> of <code>#55FF46</code> color; the invisible <code>SideDoorTrigger</code> does not participate in collision, touch, or query. The only <code>ServerScriptService.Systems.World.AutomaticDoorController</code> serves the tag <code>ManagedAutomaticDoor</code> via a common polling scheduler and recognizes details by suffixes, so one contract supports both standard and <code>CarWash2_</code>-prefixed names. Eightfold model-local controllers and their template copies have been removed; tags and immutable motion attributes are inherited by runtime clones.

The visual frame of the service door has a separate versioned layout <code>CarWashSideDoorFrame_v1</code>. It includes extended and slightly protruding <code>DoorBlueEdgeFront/Back</code>, the upper outer overhang <code>DoorBlueEdgeTop</code> and keypad, shifted toward the rear pillar. These elements are static authored geometry: the proximity controller moves only panel/window/handle. The reference edit geometry is synchronized across both lines of all eight plots; for the second line, the normal coordinate is mirrored to the opposite outer side. The top edge and layout are also preserved in both first-line ServerStorage templates, so the plot assigned to a player at Play visually matches the Editor.

Default exit routes are defined in <code>ServiceConfig.exitRouteLocal</code> as vehicle-route control points relative to DriveSurface. The first line uses local P1–P4 <code>(-0.355, 2.033, -33.476)</code> → <code>(-0.742, 2.066, -87.824)</code> → <code>(84.071, 2.066, -87.750)</code> → <code>(83.873, 2.003, 29.758)</code>; the second — <code>(0.545, 2.066, -38.296)</code> → <code>(0.874, 2.066, -88.336)</code> → <code>(45.071, 2.066, -87.750)</code> → <code>(44.873, 2.003, 29.758)</code>. Starting from Bible 2.19 Studio runtime override exists separately for each <code>Workspace.ServiceRouteMarkers.Tycoon1…Tycoon8</code>: <code>CarWash1/CarWash2.P1-P2</code> define exit starts, <code>Common.P3-P4</code> — the common remainder, and <code>RepairBay1/2</code> and <code>TuningDyno/Body/Paint</code> have <code>P1…P7</code>. Each folder contains 111 descendants and is a precise world-space transform of the Tycoon1 canon relative to plot-local <code>PlayerSpawn</code>; <code>ServiceConfig.exitRouteLocal</code> remains a compatibility fallback, but is no longer the standard route for Tycoon2–8. After smoothing inbound/outbound routes, they are once projected onto whitelist Streets, ApproachRamp, DriveSurface, ExitRamp, and ExitWays, so Y follows the actual road and ramp profile, and then car-specific clearance is added. The next P5 is calculated from the closest side of RoadGraph separately from the inbound route. Owner inbound Repair Bay → Car Wash does not use the closest RoadGraph node on the plot (it is east of the bay, of Used Cars): wrong-way join and southern curb offset caused a vehicle to drive sideways off the asphalt. Instead, <code>localRoadBetween</code> holds the frontage of the plot (almost the X-axis at close Z) and does not fallback through a tycoon street node. Catch-up does not reset the remaining inbound onto the pad while the vehicle is further than 12 studs from the wash. <code>CarWash_ExitWayAll</code> is also preserved in both ServerStorage templates.

The canonical all-plot contract, introduced in PlaceVersion <code>1329</code>, is built from <code>Tycoon1.Buildings</code> through a stable <code>Buildings.JunkSpot.JunkSpot.PlayerSpawn</code>, rather than through a missing root Folder pivot or offset legacy <code>Locations.Spawn</code>. The roots <code>Tycoon2…Tycoon8</code>, their identity/owner attributes, <code>Locations</code>, mailbox/VFX remain owned; only Buildings are replaced, after which an empty runtime-owned <code>DealerCars</code> is created. Physical <code>PlayerMailbox</code> and their mechanics are preserved, but the temporary presentation contract hides both authored notification billboards on each plot — <code>Envelope</code> and <code>Dot/TextLabel</code>; all 16 <code>BillboardGui.Enabled=false</code>, and eight mailbox roots are marked <code>MailboxIconSuppressed=true</code> for explicit reversibility. The geometry baseline PV1329 had 6,547 Buildings descendants / 2,010 BaseParts, 208 placements / 149 unique <code>PadId</code> and 26 placements / 16 unique <code>UpgradeId</code>; after RB2/helper expansion, current 159 <code>TycoonConfig</code> definitions and configured model groups are resolved across eight plots, and baseline counts are not used as current pad recount. In the restored visual, <code>CarWash_ParkingApproachBase</code> is included, both Repair parking bases, <code>TuningAtelier.Building.ParkingBase</code> and full authored compressor/jack/rack/NPC subtrees. All 450 nested Models with physical geometry transfer their own <code>WorldPivot</code>; both brush machines are located 2.420 studs from their DriveSurface on each plot. <code>TycoonServiceClient.washGeometry</code> additionally ignores any future stale pivot beyond 40 studs from lane centre, mirrored guard in <code>ServiceRouteDebugBuilder</code>.

Foundation reveal contract: <code>CarWash_ParkingApproachBase</code> is part of <code>CW_Foundation.models</code>, and the only <code>TuningAtelier.Building.ParkingBase</code> is part of <code>TA_Foundation.models</code>. These drive/parking slabs must not remain unmanaged descendants: <code>PadService.applyOwnerVisibility</code> shows unmanaged geometry immediately after owner assignment, regardless of progression. Until purchase on all eight plots, both elements must have <code>Built=false</code>, <code>Transparency=1</code> and disabled collision/touch/query; after purchase, they are revealed and participate in the overall build animation together with ConcreteBase/ServiceFloors.

Starting from version 1.93, the wash whitelist additionally includes <code>CarWash_ParkingApproachBase</code>, and the overall four-wheel surface fit keeps tires on slopes and seams. For CarWash2, without authored QueueFront/QueueRear, the queue is calculated from <code>entry + axis × (6/-7)</code> and raycast-projected onto the parking base; fallback to Repair Bay is prohibited.

### Tuning

Dyno, Body, and Paint — three independent physical queues, each accepting a Street/customer job and the corresponding Owner stage. Until the specialist performs Street/customer work, it is done manually; after that, it is automated. The specialist first physically approaches the vehicle with a profile animation, and only after a successful approach does the full work timer begin; the player can interrupt an awaiting or already started job. Until the purchase of the general <code>TA_ServiceAdvisor</code> checkout, it is performed directly at the ready vehicle; after purchase, the advisor automatically completes Street jobs for all three queues. <code>TA_OperationsManager</code> adds ×1.25 to the server-calculated Street tuning payout; Owner/CarValue stages still do not receive station cash. Owner checkout bypass is described below.

The paint specialist uses hand-paint mist VFX, and during the process, the body interpolates to a random hue; the selected <code>paintHue</code> is saved in the exact owner car record. Any numeric repaint receives <code>RepaintGlossy=true</code>: the base paintable body becomes <code>SmoothPlastic/Reflectance 0.38</code>, and the repaired MeshPart/UnionOperation gains a thin welded clearcoat shell <code>Reflectance 0.42 / Transparency 0.68 / Scale 1.006</code> without collision, touch, query, or shadow. The factory removes the coat and returns the exact authored Material/Reflectance. PBR <code>RoughnessMap</code> runtime code is not modified: access to the texture slot requires a Plugin capability. Window tint at level <code>0&lt;alpha&lt;0.999</code> uses <code>SmoothPlastic</code> and interpolates to <code>Reflectance 0.18</code>; full tinting forcibly obtains optical black <code>RGB(2,2,3)</code>, <code>Transparency=0</code>, and <code>Reflectance=0.025</code> to preserve a narrow glossy highlight without a bright sky mirror. At level 0% the authored color/transparency/material/reflectance is returned exactly. <code>RustyCar</code> and <code>Wronkler</code> have <code>RepaintEligible=false</code>: the Street paint queue does not select them, and the owner pipeline removes incompatible Paint-stage. The tuning staff head accessory has a versioned layout contract; authored caps/headphones are positioned relative to the Head, and when <code>LayoutVersion</code> changes, they are guaranteed to be reassembled instead of retaining a stale weld layout.

The Body stage sets neon underglow and full-black window tint. On server creation of the Body job, it selects one <code>ServiceNeonColorId</code> from the approved palette Cyan/Red/Magenta/Lime/Orange; Blue and Violet are excluded. The client displays the installation in four sequential quarters of the server-authoritative work timer: <code>Left → Front → Right → Rear</code>; simultaneously, <code>ReplicatedStorage.VehicleWindowTint</code> interpolates cabin glass to fully opaque <code>RGB(0,0,0)</code> SmoothPlastic. At <code>AwaitCheckout</code> or <code>Departing</code>, both results are forcibly fully applied. For the owner pipeline, the selected neon ID is transferred into the exact processing car as <code>neonColorId</code>, and the tint is retrieved from persisted <code>pipeline.completed.tuningBody</code>, so no separate save flag is required and it is restored on every DealerCar spawn/rejoin. Street cars retain both visible results until physical departure, but do not create an owner save. All 11 catalog TrafficCars use one cabin <code>Glass/glass*</code> MeshPart and pass through a common classifier; runtime Fierro Rosso audit confirmed one black opaque window mesh.

Two floor inserts <code>AtelierProps.BodyKitStation.LiftAccent</code> on each of the eight plots and in <code>ServerStorage.TuningAtelierTemplate</code> are dark cyan-steel Metal <code>RGB(25,65,90)</code>, not Neon. A total of 18 details are synchronized; they retain lift geometry but no longer appear as a standalone source of cyan light.

All five purchasable roles in Tuning Atelier — Dyno, Body, Paint, Service Advisor, and Operations Manager — are authored as R15 on Tycoon1…Tycoon8 and in <code>ServerStorage.TuningAtelierTemplate</code>, totaling 45 rigs. The chest <code>RoleAccent</code> is removed. The dynamic R15 head, <code>FaceControls</code>, retains its own head texture and skin color, and the overlaid classic R6 <code>face</code> decal <code>18151722</code> is removed. Service Advisor and Operations Manager sit in their own <code>ChairSeat</code> at a standard root height <code>seatTop + 1.10</code>, look at the keyboard, and use the same dual-track computer/sit blend as the Checkout Clerk. Their monitor/screen/stand/keyboard is oriented toward the chair on all eight plots and in the template; the staff remain mobile R15s and use profile-specific idle/walk/work tracks.

The Admin Operations office has one large panoramic window on the left and right side walls in Tycoon1…Tycoon8 and in <code>ServerStorage.TuningAtelierTemplate</code>. To preserve the purchase/reveal contract, previous monolithic parts are replaced with models bearing the same names <code>AdminLeftWall</code>/<code>AdminRightWall</code>; each model consists of exactly 10 parts and still belongs to <code>TA_AdminWalls</code>. The opening of each window is <code>18.79 × 9.8 studs</code>: a blue-tinted <code>PanoramicGlass</code> (<code>Glass</code>, <code>Transparency=0.36</code>, <code>Reflectance=0.05</code>) surrounded by a dark metallic frame with a central post, and the original concrete is preserved as the lower parapet, the upper lintel, and two side supports. The glass remains <code>CanCollide=true</code>, so the visually open wall prevents exiting the office; frame parts do not participate in collision. Furniture, staff, the front counter, and individual <code>AdminLeftAccent</code>/<code>AdminRightAccent</code> have not changed.

At the center of the free space between the two workstations in Admin Operations is an enlarged automatic door <code>AdminRearDoor</code> with contract <code>AdminRearDoor_v3_TightFitDualFaceWindow</code>. The center of the passage is calculated from the bounding boxes of the two work areas and has a local offset <code>X=-3.145</code> relative to the back wall center. The previous monolithic <code>AdminBackWall</code> is replaced with a model composed of the left/right concrete sections and the upper lintel, so the physical opening <code>8.4 × 10.8 studs</code> is real, not overlaid on a collision wall. The door panel <code>8.28 × 10.64 studs</code> is extended by <code>0.55 stud</code> beyond the outer facade edge; the total closed gap is only <code>0.12 stud</code> in width and <code>0.16 stud</code> in height. The glass insert <code>DoorWindow</code> has dimensions <code>4.5 × 2.5 studs</code>, thickness <code>0.44 stud</code>, and protrudes on both sides of the panel, so it is visible from inside the office and from the outside. When approaching within a radius of 10 studs, the door shifts by <code>8.15 studs</code> toward the left handle strictly along the outer plane. <code>DoorWindow</code> and <code>DoorHandle</code> move with the panel, while the red frame, keypad, threshold, and outer step remain stationary. The door closes after the player exits the radius and does not enter the wall in any orientation of the plot. The named model retains <code>TA_AdminWalls</code> purchase/reveal contract; <code>AdminRearDoor_v3</code> is synchronized on Tycoon1…Tycoon8 and in <code>ServerStorage.TuningAtelierTemplate</code>.

A tuning specialist may have an explicit <code>ServiceMarkers/&lt;slot&gt;/WorkPath/P1…Pn</code>. <code>NPCService</code> sorts points by numeric suffix; a standard specialist walks the route once to the start of the work timer, while Body uses a special four-phase contract. Its <code>WorkPath</code> on all eight plots and in <code>ServerStorage.TuningAtelierTemplate</code> contains <code>01_Left</code>, <code>02_Front</code>, <code>03_Right</code>, <code>04_Rear</code>: the specialist begins repairs on the Left, then at 25/50/75% of the time physically crosses to the next side, and after each visit continues the Junk Spot animation <code>repairEngine</code>. <code>ServiceBodyWorkerPhase</code> replicates the current side. Without <code>WorkPath</code> a compatibility fallback is retained for a single <code>WorkPoint</code>. For Paint on all eight plots, a separate four-point route around Stop is defined: both longitudinal sides of the vehicle and two safe transverse crossings remain within the internal walls of the paint booth; <code>WorkerHome</code> and final <code>WorkPoint</code> are also inside the box. The Body Specialist Tycoon1 has an owner-authored starting model pivot <code>CFrame(141.176, 4.448, 777.601)</code> with LookVector <code>(-0.492262, 0, -0.870447)</code>; Tycoon2…8 and template use the same local transform relative to their Body Stop. This pivot is saved in <code>_PIVOT0</code>, and <code>WorkerHome</code> is synchronized with the Stop model position. The Dyno Specialist Tycoon1 similarly has an owner-authored home pivot <code>CFrame(151.255, 4.448, 770.480)</code>, LookVector <code>(0.762069, 0, -0.647496)</code>, and a separate work model pivot <code>CFrame(154.302, 4.558, 771.003)</code>, LookVector <code>(-0.182453, 0, 0.983215)</code>. Home is stored in the rig <code>_PIVOT0</code>, and WorkPoint is stored as a foot target with attribute exact <code>AuthoredRigPivot</code> and R15 runtime Y-compensation; after standard <code>BayStaff.place</code> the actual model matches the second pivot. Both Dyno transforms are locally translated to Tycoon2…8 and template. Dyno work uses the same Junk Spot animation <code>repairEngine</code>. Saved <code>_PIVOT0</code> of other Repair/Tuning staff are re-fixed to current authored positions, so reveal and return home no longer send NPCs to coordinates before building relocation.

Each tuning slot has its own <code>InboundRoadJoin</code> and <code>OutboundRoadJoin</code> with contract <code>TuningLaneJoin_v1</code>. When moving from the Used Cars Dyno side, the Dyno enters through the UC→TA point 42, Body through point 46, and Paint through the final Atelier point. When exiting into the opposite lane, the Dyno flows into the TA→UC point 6, Body through point 2, and Paint through the start TA→UC. The markers are synchronized with the canonical geometry of Tycoon1 on Tycoon2…Tycoon8 and <code>ServerStorage.TuningAtelierTemplate</code>; <code>TycoonServiceClient</code> uses them in live owner routes, and <code>ServiceRouteDebugBuilder</code> shows the same joins.

The tuning inbound/outbound and parking queue height uses the same four-wheel surface fit as Repair/Car Wash. The contact set includes ParkingBase, ApproachRamp, ServiceFloors, Streets, and profiled Dyno/Paint surfaces; actual collidable station ramps (such as BodyKitStation Ramp) are detected by tyre-raycast, so the vehicle receives real pitch instead of a front axle drop or entire body lift during transitions P4–P7.

The Dyno queue has a versioned layout <code>TuningDynoQueue_v2</code>: <code>QueueFront</code> is aligned along the Entry→Stop axis exactly 6 studs before <code>Entry</code>, <code>QueueRear</code> is located at 19 studs; the distance between centers is 13 studs. The contract was authored for Tycoon1…Tycoon8 and in <code>ServerStorage.TuningAtelierTemplate</code>. This ensures the front-waiting vehicle is directly adjacent to the Dyno door and prevents a stale marker from remaining on the far frontage line.
