Tycoon1 is the canonical reference for the current geometry and positioning of the Tuning Atelier: the entire folder — building, pads, service/staff markers — has been relocated relative to the fixed JunkSpot plot anchor on Tycoon2…Tycoon8 and <code>ServerStorage.TuningAtelierTemplate</code>. Old primitive AC/roof vents have been replaced with imported <code>DETAIL_acunit</code> and <code>DETAIL_boxvent</code>; pivots have been normalized to the bottom center of the mesh. AC occupies the third roof-equipment slot next to two vents and sits precisely on the FlatRoofHeavy. Source details are stored in <code>ServerStorage.ImportedDetailSources</code>.

### Dealer stock pipeline

<code>OwnerCarPipeline</code> builds once for an exact <code>tradeId</code> plan in order <code>Repair → dirty-only Wash → Dyno → Body → Paint</code>, skipping only unconstructed/unavailable stages. Purchased <code>ServiceHelper</code>, <code>ServiceHelper2</code> and <code>ServiceHelper3</code> form a pool of up to three universal logistics escorts; First/Second Mechanic remain staff members at JunkSpot W1/W2. For each queued/servicing record, the server reserves a specific free <code>escortId</code>, stores it in the pipeline, and cannot simultaneously assign this helper to another vehicle. In the service client, a local copy of the exact helper appearance is used, while the corresponding canonical staff rig remains on JunkSpot and is hidden only during its active visual traversal. The service slot replicates <code>ServiceSource</code>, <code>ServiceRewardMode</code>, pipeline/stage/next stage, exact W/trade identity, specific <code>ServiceEscortId</code> and condition.

Moving service proxies reside in client-local <code>Workspace.TycoonServices</code>, i.e., they are siblings of tycoons. <code>WorldHoverClient</code> scans both the owned tycoon and this separate folder. In idle <code>CustomerServiceBadge</code> it collapses into a blue circle 24×24, while <code>OwnerServiceBadge</code> collapses into green. On desktop, the full status expands on actual mouse hover and remains visible for five seconds after cursor exit; on mobile, hover is fully disabled, and only the selected card's exact <code>TouchTapInWorld</code> expands and closes the previous one. Runtime status refresh cannot voluntarily revert text to collapsed state. Owner <code>DealerValueBillboard</code> on JunkSpot/Used Cars also uses a compact amber marker 24×24 and reveals the detail card according to the same hover/tap/proximity rules; <code>QueueStatus</code> incoming queues remains outside this contract. The repair presenter can add a new <code>GuiObject</code> inside the dealer billboard after registration, so the hover controller on each state update accepts late direct children, hides them in collapsed state, and uses optional <code>WorldHoverExpandedWidth/Height</code> for expanded canvas. An invisible <code>CarInspectionHitbox</code> is built from live <code>Root</code> and <code>Width/Length/RoofY</code> attributes, welded to the moving vehicle and simultaneously serves as the precise hover/tap target; a stale world-space bounding box is prohibited.

The owner station queue has been fixed after diagnosing CAR-005. Non-Repair entries <code>Station_CarWash*/Station_Tuning*</code> now replicate <code>ServiceEscortId</code>, so the waiting proxy always receives a copy of the Service Helper. <code>QueuePosition</code> is an activation flag: upon actual trade/slot change, it is first cleared, then identity/stage/escort attributes are published, and only then is the position enabled in the final step; stable sync ticks do not restart. The client additionally prohibits station fallback without <code>QueueSlotId</code>, repeats render after a brief active-proxy handoff, and updates <code>drive.stage</code>/badge/destination on the existing model. The inter-stage Repair/Wash/Tuning → Tuning route passes through a profiled <code>InboundRoadJoin → RoadApproach → QueueFront</code>, so it no longer cuts into Used Cars and does not enter the Repair Queue. The parked proxy always displays the target stage, for example <code>WAITING FOR PAINT ATELIER</code>; <code>WAITING FOR TRAFFIC</code> is allowed only during actual <code>trafficBlocks(...)=true</code> while in motion. In verified Bavora <code>MovementDebugBlocked=false</code>, therefore the previous traffic signature was merely an UI semantic bug.

Sequential windows of the Tuning Atelier do not use a global <code>RoadGraph</code> and do not perform an intermediate departure to Used Cars. <code>tuningTransferPlan</code> is constructed from live <code>Stop/Exit/RoadExit/Entry</code> source and target boxes: the car departs from Dyno, smoothly turns along the common apron, and enters Body from the front; then, using the same local contract, it exits from Body and enters Paint. If Body is occupied, the Dyno car waits on a derived hold for approximately 75% of the cross-traffic span and 7 studs inward into the apron; if Paint is occupied, the Body car waits earlier — for 40% of the span and 2 studs inward. On Tycoon1 this provides ground points around <code>(137.79, 0.19, 732.00)</code> and <code>(116.99, 0.19, 727.00)</code>, but world coordinates are not hardcoded: all eight rotated plots compute identical local offsets from their own geometry. During waiting, the old slot remains authoritative, the exact proxy is not destroyed, and the next controller continues the same forward curve after the target window is freed.

Completion of the owner stage is serialized at the level of the exact service slot. <code>DataService.commitCritical</code> may yield, but <code>NPCService.clear</code> maintains a re-entry guard for the entire duration of release+teardown and does not remove <code>ServiceFromLot</code> until the end; the second Heartbeat cannot simultaneously leave the previous Repair job and start a new Tuning job for the same <code>tradeId</code>. The client retains the exact proxy Instance even after departure begins, if <code>ServicePipelineNextStage</code> exists, and passes it to the next queue/run controller; a new template clone is only valid as a fallback. Each owner slot/queue additionally publishes accumulated <code>ServiceSavedFactoryPaintId</code>, <code>ServiceSavedPaintHue</code>, <code>ServiceSavedNeonColorId</code>, and <code>ServiceBodyCompleted</code>; therefore, fallback/rejoin restores the selected factory variant, repaint, underglow, and full-black tint. <code>factoryPaintId</code> is acquired with the exact W model until critical processing commit; legacy processing records receive a stable trade-derived fallback without a new random roll. Any late condition refresh first saves <code>PaintHue</code>, restores the exact factory base, and then re-applies the already-set cosmetics, so Offer/Sold refresh and service proxy rebuild do not change the color. The owner badge is replaced idempotently upon controller change and does not accumulate.

The height of Tuning inbound/outbound is now determined by raycast projection against a whitelist of real drive surfaces, rather than saved pivot-Y P-markers. <code>tuningGeometry</code> passes <code>ParkingBase</code>, <code>ApproachRamp</code>, <code>ServiceFloors</code>, Streets, and profile Dyno/Paint floor surfaces to the general <code>conformServicePathToSurfaces</code>. X/Z and LookVector authored P1–P7 remain the source of shape and orientation, but after smoothing, each point receives actual Y coverage before adding car-specific ground clearance. On all three Tycoon1 lines, raycast provides P4/P5/P6 <code>Y=0.600</code>, P7 <code>Y=0.250</code>, then asphalt <code>Y=0.125</code>; previous negative Body/Paint marker-Y no longer sinks wheels below ground. Real Dyno owner-departure demonstrated smooth P4/P5, a smooth P6/P7, and a maximum frame Y-step of <code>0.146 stud</code>.

All client-rendered service/owner queue proxy objects are purely visual and must have zero <code>CanCollide=true</code> descendants. The old anchored <code>QueueCollision</code> has been removed: real-time audit recorded up to 160.5 studs/s, although the shell followed the body with an offset ≈1.18 studs. Hover/click provides only <code>CarInspectionHitbox</code> (<code>CanCollide=false</code>, <code>CanTouch=false</code>, <code>CanQuery=true</code>), so interactivity does not require physical collision with Character.

After a successful critical begin exact owner car, it atomically moves from W to persisted <code>processingCars</code>. Thus, the freed JunkSpot W immediately becomes available to accept the next purchased car. In case of a failed begin commit, a deep rollback occurs to the original W and the proxy is not launched. All subsequent claim/finish/abort operations are performed based on exact <code>tradeId</code> processing-record, not on the current content of W.

The owner car automatically proceeds past customer checkout after actual work completion: Repair completes the stage after lowering, Tuning — after work, Wash — after the full timed pass. After the final stage, the server selects and reserves an exact free P, performs a critical transfer <code>processingCars → Listed</code>, but no longer immediately displays the car on the parking surface. An authoritative display model is created invisibly, placed in handoff-frame <code>UsedLotEntranceReference</code>, receives a temporary driver with exact appearance <code>ServiceHelper</code>, and is only then published in the world for the physical final drive. If all P slots are occupied, the exact processing-record remains in <code>AwaitingDisplay</code>, does not block W, and retries transfer upon P release or after rejoin. Return to W and seating of FirstSalesman for this branch does not occur.

The final Used Cars planner is shared between owner helper and FirstSalesman: <code>RoadTraffic.displayParkingPlan</code> leads from the gate via the central drive to the computed setup before exact P, then performs a short 14-stud reverse strictly in authored <code>LotSlots.saleCFrame</code>. Therefore, P1…P6 use the same human maneuver "approach → display → reverse back", while distant P points no longer receive the long diagonal reverse from entrance. The route shape is derived from <code>UsedLotEntranceReference</code> and the final CFrame of each slot, and works identically on eight rotated tycoon plots; separate authored <code>VehicleRoutes</code> for P1…P6 are not live dependencies. The final <code>PivotTo</code> remains only as a seam for precise alignment after physical movement.

Before Used Cars, a physical queue of up to two vehicles may still appear: the number of visible <code>Q1/Q2</code> is limited by the number of active helpers; the helper sits in the vehicle and periodically selects one of seven replicas indicating the need to purchase additional parking/display spaces. At the moment of successful critical transfer, the client queue/service proxy completes its segment at the gate, and the server-owned display model takes on the same visual role and continues its journey to exact P; the previous seam "proxy disappears → vehicle appears at P" is prohibited.

The standard W → FirstSalesman → P transport is protected by a lease/watchdog mechanism. <code>LotAutomation</code> stores <code>startedAt</code> exact listing job; only jobs older than 75 seconds are considered softlocked, after which the watchdog invalidates the old StaffDirector epoch by exact <code>tradeId</code>, silently restores ListingTransport to the original W, and re-plans the pickup. A normal intermediate <code>Ready + no autopilot</code> is not orphaned and does not trigger recovery. The old coroutine cannot clear or move a newly assigned job; technical recovery does not open a player seat/prompt.

A free P-slot is reserved by itself <code>ListingTransport.sync</code> even before the FirstSalesman journey starts. Therefore, <code>LotAutomation</code> has no right to repeatedly request <code>DealerCar.availableDisplayIndex()</code>: its own exact reservation legally produces this result <code>nil</code> and previously created a self-block <code>Ready W → reserved P → automation refuses</code>. The only authority for session/reservation validation during automated pickup is <code>ListingTransport.beginAutopilot</code>; the actually filled dealership safely and idempotently rejects it.

<code>ListingTransport</code> stores waiting and salesman-controlled session buckets by exact car/model identity. Player-controlled lane is removed: even with multiple Ready W cars, only FirstSalesman sequentially accepts them into transport authority, and seats/prompts remain blocked during waiting, approach, driving, cancel, and recovery. Validation, commit, and cleanup always search for exact <code>tradeId</code>/model, so one stuck job cannot clear another.

The full dealer rig may store authored <code>Chassis.DrivePromptAttachment.DrivePrompt</code> as part of the template, but gameplay boundary always keeps it <code>Enabled=false</code>; <code>GetInToListPrompt</code> is also not created or enabled. If no free P is available, a separate <code>SaleSpotBlockedPrompt</code> appears on each ready W vehicle with text <code>SELL PREVIOUS CAR FIRST</code>; it does not seat the player and, upon click, explains that the player must first sell the previous car.

Street queue is still limited to 30 seconds: a overdue street vehicle leaves without payout. Owner occupancy wait no longer aborts in listing: an occupied bay holds the helper in line with status <code>WAITING FOR …</code> only until the next stage is actually occupied. If Car Wash is free, Repair Bay immediately hands off the exact car to wash (handoff with the same heading), without 9-second Departing and without teleporting to pad. Reverse leaves the nose in the bay; on exit, the vehicle turns left/right along the lane, not making an instant reverse on 180°. Manual active states <code>AwaitLift/AwaitHoodService/AwaitCheckout/AwaitWork</code> remain active for 90 seconds; only this stall applies owner penalty <code>×0.75</code> and direct listing without station cash. Abort/final transfer undergo critical commit before world state is released. Owner inbound between stages proceeds via local road (U-exit of wash, apron of bay, front-road), not directly through the plot or into a random tunnel.

The start of physical work in Repair Bay, Car Wash, and Tuning Atelier is arrival-driven, not timer-driven. A new job is published with <code>ServiceArrivalConfirmed=false</code>; the local renderer drives the exact proxy to the final authored inbound marker and sends <code>ServiceVisualArrived(slotId, jobId, tradeId)</code>. The server accepts acknowledgment only from the owner of the corresponding tycoon and only upon full identity match, after which it sets <code>ServiceCarAtBay=true</code>/<code>ServiceArrivalConfirmed=true</code>. Only then can Repair raise the lift, Tuning triggers a specialist, and Wash begins the through-bay phase. Timers set visual movement speed and work duration, but do not prove arrival and cannot remove a vehicle still en route. For Wash phase, the clock is reset at the moment of actual entry, so a long inter-stage handoff does not fast-forward the wash.

World badge is also computed from the actual proxy zone: <code>EN_ROUTE</code>, <code>AT_BAY</code> or <code>DEPARTING</code>. Therefore, server state and public display remain consistent: <code>DRIVING TO …</code> persists until arrival, <code>SPECIALIST APPROACHING</code> remains while the specialist is approaching, active <code>PAINTING/BEING WASHED/REPAIR IN PROGRESS</code> is set only for a vehicle in the bay. Studio-only <code>VehicleStatusAudit</code> is enabled via attribute <code>Workspace.VehicleStatusAuditEnabled</code> and checks every second for ID, position, zone, distance, server state, actual/expected text. In regular gameplay it is disabled; <code>VehicleMovementAuditEnabled</code> is also false by default.

Owner DEBUG panel contains a local toggle <code>SHOW CAR IDs</code>. While enabled, a lightweight cyan billboard <code>CAR-###</code> is created above each visible traffic/dealer/service vehicle; disabling immediately removes billboards and stops workspace scanning. The ID is recorded locally in <code>MovementDebugId</code> so that opt-in <code>VehicleMovementAudit</code> can use the same number in its tracing. The toggle is off by default.

Waiting for the next owner-service station is published only if all actually built compatible lanes are occupied. Unbuilt Car Wash/Tuning slots are not considered free capacity. On a held slot, the server sets <code>ServiceWaitReason=AllBuiltSlotsOccupied</code>, <code>ServiceWaitOccupied</code> and <code>ServiceWaitCapacity</code>, thus allowing the reason for WAITING to be verified without guessing. A vehicle paused in waiting retains the actual heading from the last exit-control and does not reorient on-site.

The queue includes customer vehicles, but is stage-compatible and starvation-safe. For each physical slot:

<code>if ownerQueued and (not streetEligible or consecutiveOwnerStarts &lt; 2) then Owner else Street</code>

The owner branch takes the oldest compatible <code>queuedAt</code> FIFO; street eligibility occurs only after its own <code>nextAllowed</code> and general road-service stagger. After two consecutive owner starts, the next ready street vehicle is guaranteed to receive this slot; a street start resets the counter. If one source is not ready, the other does not wait. Persisted <code>processingCars</code> has capacity 4 independent of W1/W2, so service no longer blocks acceptance of the next JunkSpot stock.

Condition is stored separately from JunkSpot parts. For each new vehicle, dirty and oil leak are determined independently via arrival rolls: the chance of dirty fluctuates between 35–80%, and the chance of oil leak is 30–70%. The resulting booleans are saved together with the exact car record and are not retransmitted during respawn/rejoin. Therefore, clean vehicles do not require Car Wash, vehicles without leaks do not show false issues, and actual dirt/oil conditions are preserved until the appropriate service. Repair removes oil leak, Car Wash removes body/wheel dirt. The owner pipeline sets <code>washing</code> in the plan only if <code>condition.dirty == true</code>; clean stock does not wait for Car Wash and does not hold a wash in the route. The general <code>CarConditionVisuals</code> applies these conditions to full DealerCar (including Nissho full rig) and to client-local TrafficCars proxy. Dirt shells live in <code>_CarConditionVisuals</code> and are not included in junk-repair recolor / <code>applyPaintHue</code>: the name <code>DirtShell</code> is no longer painted as body <code>shell</code>, so a red Nissho with dirty=true remains visually dirty after Body/Wheels/Engine.

Paint uses a single replicated contract <code>ReplicatedStorage.VehiclePaint</code> for progressive booth preview and for persisted DealerCar after pipeline. Paintable is defined as body or chassis, or a safe exterior part inside Body; wheel/tire, glass/window, light/lamp, turn/indicator/signal, brake/stop/tail, interior/seat/dashboard, exhaust, plate, dirt/oil are always excluded even within the Body folder. For textured bodies with <code>SurfaceAppearance.ColorMap</code> the <code>SurfaceAppearance.Color</code> changes, not just <code>MeshPart.Color</code>; this is necessary for Nissho <code>Body.Car_body</code>. The booth interpolates the original tint during WorkerServicing and preserves the same hue when transitioning to Used Cars.

General station formula:

<code>payout = floor((basePayout + payoutPerLevel × (level − 1)) × cashMultiplier)</code>

For Dyno/Body/Paint, after purchasing Operations Manager, a separate general step is applied: <code>payout = floor(payout × 1.25 + 0.5)</code>. This multiplier is stored once in <code>TycoonConfig.TUNING_OPERATIONS_MANAGER_MULTIPLIER</code> and used by payout authority, station capsule, and staff inspection.

<code>fillTime = max(0.15, baseFillTime / speedMultiplier)</code>

Cash and speed multipliers from the same zone are multiplied. For job phases, floor time applies; typical constraints: <code>MIN_PHASE_TIME=0.6</code>, <code>MIN_GAP=3</code>.

### Staff

- FirstMechanic — W1 and Selling Car Parts handled by Parts Sales Laptop; idle state uses computer animation on the laptop.
- SecondMechanic — W2.
- ServiceHelper — a separate JunkSpot logistics NPC for owner pipeline and final Dealership handoff; a free helper can also take the exact W→P listing fallback if not occupied by servicing.
- FirstSalesman — automatically purchases incoming **world-seller** deal, listing, and auto-accepts buyer. Its purchase fully disables phone-fence sourcing and clears active/pending fence; new vehicles arrive only physically. The driver exits immediately after vehicle placement, even if FirstSalesman is busy. During this period, the player can manually close the deal; the staff displays a green deal-cash popup and one of four gratitude messages for the substitution.
- Tow Truck Driver — purchased R15-employee Repair Bay. Seats in a separate runtime Tow Truck, drives outside the plot, returns exact cargo in incoming FIFO and returns to original location. The form uses <code>130837197855668</code>/<code>121089657153202</code>; canonical rig is present in all eight plots and template contains standard BodyColors and <code>BedHair</code>.
- Bay Mechanic — Repair Bay work.
- Dyno/Body/Paint Specialist — only own tuning queue.
- Service Advisor — general auto-checkout Dyno/Body/Paint and +20% Atelier service speed.
- Operations Manager — general ×1.25 Street tuning cash bonus.

FirstSalesman uses <code>salesmanCanBuy</code> in authoritative callback. Ask ratio, maximum Cash fraction and <code>salesmanCashReserve</code> are checked before automatic purchase; in Play after this fix, regular and tow-delivered transactions continue to go through the same server BUY path.

### Staff arbitration and incoming capacity [LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO — 2026-08-27]

FirstMechanic retains two responsibilities; <code>Quick Fix</code> in the card and state mapping is replaced with <code>Selling Car Parts</code>. His free home is moved to the laptop and uses <code>116122076689674</code>. SecondMechanic in free state uses a verified standing idle <code>507766388</code>; a separate animation-watchdog is not added. Runtime animation audit confirmed both active tracks.

Listing adds ServiceHelper as fallback job without creating a new profession. Priority: already assigned service-route → final handoff to the same helper → standard FirstSalesman W→P listing → free ServiceHelper fallback. Lease is stored as exact <code>HelperListingTrade_ServiceHelper*</code>; <code>TradeService</code> checks the processing pipeline, <code>ServiceSlots</code>, <code>ServiceParkingJourneys</code> and active <code>StaffJob</code>, and <code>NPCService.availableHelperId</code> symmetrically excludes the listing-leased helper from service. Both FirstSalesman and fallback go through one <code>StaffDirector.listJob</code> with the same exact <code>tradeId</code>, W, P and <code>ListingTransport</code> guards. Runtime audit gave <code>HELPER_DUPLICATE_CLAIMS=0</code>.

Incoming cadence is calculated server-side from real <code>min(workingCapacity, displayCapacity)</code>, purchased by First/SecondMechanic and ServiceHelper1–3. Authored seller gap is divided by bounded throughput <code>1…2.25</code>; ambient traffic is not included in the formula. Tutorial arrivals remain scripted, and existing working/display/queue limits retain hard capacity. Final Play ran longer than normal seller gap without nil-player or queue errors.

---

## 7. World and physical structure [LIVE]

### Scale

- In Workspace there are eight plots: <code>Tycoon1…Tycoon8</code>.
- This is a hard limit on the number of concurrent owners served.
- Each plot contains approximately 4900 descendants; the entire Workspace has about 57 thousand descendants.
- If all eight plots are occupied, a new player loads their profile and leaderstats, but the plot does not get assigned; currently there is only a server warning, with no queue or kick user experience.

### Collision build-state [LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO — 2026-08-27]

All unpurchased/hidden objects on Tycoon1–8 receive <code>CanCollide=false</code>, <code>CanTouch=false</code> and <code>CanQuery=false</code> via a common <code>PadService</code> build-state applicator. Before being hidden, each part retains its own authored collision/touch/query flags, and upon purchase, these are restored exactly as they were, not a global <code>true</code>. <code>DescendantAdded</code> guard re-normalizes accessories and late-appearing parts under any ancestor with <code>Built=false</code>. The final runtime descendant-audit resulted in <code>UNBUILT_INTERACTIVE_PARTS=0</code>; a manually added collidable late part was automatically converted to non-collide/non-touch/non-query (<code>LATE_DESCENDANT_GUARD=true</code>).

Owner-visibility applicator <code>PadService</code> also preserves authored enabled-state in <code>_OE0</code> and disables <code>LayerCollector</code>, <code>ProximityPrompt</code>, lights, emitters and beams for unowned tycoon/hidden build subtree. Fresh Play confirmed Tycoon2…Tycoon8: <code>P1</code> NumberGui, <code>CUSTOMIZE</code> ScreenGui and CustomizePrompt are disabled 7/7; for owned Tycoon1 the same surfaces have been restored and are visible.

### Canonical zones of each plot

| Zone | DataModel path inside TycoonN |
|---|---|
| Junk Lot | <code>Buildings.JunkSpot.JunkSpot</code> |
| Used Cars | <code>Buildings.JunkSpot.JunkSpot_UsedCars</code> |
| Repair Bay | <code>Buildings.RepairBay</code> (Model, road-aligned WorldPivot) |
| Car Wash 1 | <code>Buildings.CarWash</code> |
| Car Wash 2 | <code>Buildings.CarWash.CarWash.CarWash2</code> |
| Tuning Atelier | <code>Buildings.TuningAtelier</code> |
| Personal Garage | <code>Buildings.PersonalGarage</code> |
| Player spawn | <code>Buildings.JunkSpot.PlayerSpawn</code> |
| Spawn fallback | <code>Locations.Spawn</code> |
| Dealer runtime stock | <code>DealerCars</code> |

Road build members inside each Car Wash: <code>CarWash.ExitWays.CarWash_ExitWay1</code> and <code>CarWash.ExitWays.CarWash_ExitWayAll</code> belong to <code>CW_ExitRamp</code>; <code>CarWash.CarWash2.ExitWays.CarWash_ExitWay2</code> belongs to <code>CW2_ExitRamp</code>. These details are synchronized across Tycoon1…Tycoon8; the first-lane Way1/WayAll are also preserved in <code>CarWash_UPD_RuntimeTemplate</code> and <code>CarWash_UPD_AuthoredSource.Building</code>.

On join <code>TycoonService</code> synchronously loads the profile, creates <code>leaderstats.Cash</code>, assigns the first available plot, sets <code>OwnerUserId</code> and moves the character.

Each of the eight Personal Garage has <code>CarSpawn</code>, <code>RuntimeCars</code>, an internal <code>CustomizeTerminal</code> and a separate concrete driveway oriented according to <code>CarSpawn.LookVector</code>. Tycoon1 is the scale/layout source for seven copies. Intersecting bushes, grass/agave, rocks and cactus are removed only within the garage/driveway envelope; affected props are pre-copied to <code>ServerStorage.PersonalGarageEnvironmentBackup/TycoonN</code>. Ground, Terrain, road, sidewalk and fence are not cleanup targets.

Garage door uses transparent PNG <code>PERSONAL RIDES / AVAILABLE AFTER TUTORIAL</code>, asset <code>rbxassetid://72401628756356</code>. The neutral pre-profile state of an unowned/released garage — open: a returning player with a completed tutorial does not see a closed door while their profile is loading and their plot is being assigned. After loading <code>syncWorld</code>, the system synchronously applies the authoritative tutorial state; only a tutorial-incomplete owner receives a closed door. Delayed 0.25/1/3 s passes remain defensive reinforcement and are not considered the moment of first door opening or respawn of an active car. Door lookup is recursive, therefore nested <code>GarageDoor</code> is also supported.

### Map

The world contains <code>MainTrack</code>, roads, desert/Southwest roadside environment, fences, VFX, and traffic. <code>MainTrack</code> is now simultaneously world/road geometry, weather surface, and owner-only personal-car activity: start/finish defines <code>FinishLineArch.FinishLine</code>, and <code>CashPickups.01…24</code> define route positions, which the server orders by entry side. The only authored <code>CashPickup</code> serves as a visual template; a constant server-visible scatter of money in the world is not created, and each player sees only their own rolling window maximum of three active points.

Traffic uses a common road ride height and allows per-template <code>TrafficHeightOffset</code>. For <code>FierroRossa</code> a <code>+0.5</code> stud is defined so that the wheel's bottom point aligns with the rest of the traffic templates and the wheels do not dip halfway below the road.

### Performance

Do not add constant full scans of Workspace or all eight tycoon folders in hot loops. For new systems, use precise folders, tags, cached references, and bounded queues. Be especially cautious when modifying <code>NPCService</code>, traffic, and central/shared vehicle runtime.

The original desktop ScriptProfiler capture for legacy <code>TrafficClient</code> took <code>40,952.290 ms</code> for <code>319.085 s</code>, i.e., an calculated average of approximately <code>2.14 ms/frame</code> at 60 FPS; the capture did not include p95. After the central traffic rollout, built-in self-telemetry on desktop/default viewport checked High <code>14/30 Hz</code> with p95 <code>1.47 ms</code> and EMA <code>1.23 ms</code> in an early warmed-up snapshot; a subsequent transient <code>13/14</code> snapshot yielded p95 <code>1.16 ms</code> and EMA <code>0.92 ms</code>. On emulated Samsung Galaxy A06 landscape <code>705×338</code> Low <code>6/15 Hz</code>, p95 <code>0.86 ms</code> and EMA <code>0.99 ms</code> were observed. This is Studio self-telemetry, not a new ScriptProfiler capture on a physical PC/mobile; the final equal-scenario conclusion of a ≥50% reduction remains a verification boundary until a repeat render-active equal-scene ScriptProfiler run is performed.

The final combined soak 19 August lasted <code>1,257.133 s</code> (20 min 57 s) in Average Laptop profile. It confirmed server/Heartbeat lifecycle, bounded population and cleanup without game-script errors, but Studio lost the active render viewport: the external observer saw zero <code>PreRender</code>, and traffic telemetry stalled on an early snapshot. Therefore, this run is not a valid sustained-render, 60/30 FPS or final ScriptProfiler acceptance. 18 warnings were recorded <code>NPCService server-confirmed stalled visual arrival</code>; their association with the stopped client render is likely, but remains an inference. Client memory in monitoring remained approximately in the range <code>3,876.7–3,977.7 MB</code> and ended around <code>3,902 MB</code> at the original screenshot baseline <code>3,953 MB</code>: the endpoint was below baseline, but a short-term peak was approximately 25 MB higher, so the strict criterion "never above baseline" is not asserted.

---

## 8. Project Architecture [LIVE]

### DataModel of the top level

~~~text
game
├─ ReplicatedStorage
│  ├─ TycoonConfig / DealerCarConfig / ServiceConfig / TownsfolkConfig
│  ├─ WeatherConfig / RadioConfig / RexVoiceConfig / PersonalGarageCustomizationConfig
│  ├─ RoadGraph / RoadGraphData
│  ├─ NPCAvatars / NPCSeatFit / NumberFormat / OfferReadout
│  ├─ VehicleLighting / VehiclePaint / VehicleWindowTint / CarExitHandler / WeatherPresets / NPCTemplates
│  ├─ BuildAnimConfig / PropSpinConfig / JuiceAnimations
│  ├─ UI/*
│  ├─ Shared/{Config,Traffic,Vehicle}
│  ├─ TycoonRemotes/*
│  ├─ TrafficCars/*
│  ├─ Car
│  └─ Templates/NPCS/*
├─ ServerScriptService
│  ├─ TycoonBootstrap
│  ├─ TycoonSystem/*
│  └─ Systems
│     ├─ NPC/{NpcAnimationController,NPCService}
│     ├─ Vehicle/{VehicleServerController,CarSpawning,Runtime}
│     └─ World/{AutomaticDoorController,Collision,WeatherService,RadioService,RoadGripService,HideEditorOnlyQueueSlots}
├─ ServerStorage
│  ├─ CarModels/*
│  ├─ CarRigFitter / Lighting/*
│  ├─ RepairBay_New_Template
│  ├─ TuningAtelierTemplate
│  ├─ CarWash_UPD_AuthoredSource
│  ├─ CarWash_UPD_RuntimeTemplate
│  └─ dated archives/backups
├─ StarterPlayer
│  └─ StarterPlayerScripts
│     ├─ Controllers/Traffic/TrafficClient
│     ├─ Controllers/Vehicle/VehicleController
│     └─ legacy domain entrypoints/*
├─ StarterGui
│  ├─ SellCarsHUD (exactly one ScreenGui; EditorPreview inside)
│  ├─ HUD / Phone / DealerCarUI / CustomerUI
│  ├─ RexRedlineSubtitleGui / SellCarsRadioGui
│  └─ SpeedLines / SuperTexture
└─ Workspace
   ├─ Tycoon1 ... Tycoon8
   ├─ MainTrack / Streets
   ├─ Environment / VFX / Fences
   └─ EditorOnlyQueueSlots
~~~

The project does not have a detected framework/package manager, <code>PackageLink</code> or numeric <code>require(assetId)</code>. Before migration, a large number of Lua containers did not equate to as many unique systems: chassis and NPC animation scripts were repeatedly copied into models. The current topology is centralized and has 179 containers with 179 different Source.

### Script topology and centralized runtime

Live Edit audit <code>Sell Cars Dev.</code> PlaceVersion <code>1262</code> before migration established a baseline: <strong>1,803 LuaSourceContainer / 189 unique Source / 1,614 exact duplicate copies</strong>. The goal of migration was to have no more than <strong>250</strong> LuaSourceContainer throughout the place and zero model-local runtime scripts inside NPCs, staff, and full vehicles; this has been achieved. During migration, <code>ServerStorage.__CentralizationBackup_PV1262</code> temporarily stored 189 disabled unique-source copies and chunked manifest instances of original 1,803 sources. After final exact path/class/Source guards, Play smoke and Edit recount checkpoint were removed. The current rollback boundary is the saved PlaceVersion <code>1262</code>; ChangeHistory waypoints are available only while the current Studio session is active. Draft is not published.

The confirmed NPC phase uses <code>ServerScriptService.Systems.NPC.NpcAnimationController</code> as the sole owner of server-controlled animation tracks. Its public API — <code>playBase</code>, <code>playOverlay</code>, <code>stopOverlay</code>, <code>stopAll</code>; the base/overlay state is stored in a weak map by rig. <code>TycoonSystem.JunkLot.LotNpc</code> enables semantic animation and differences between R6/R15, while <code>TycoonSystem.StaffAnimationController</code> remains a compatibility facade for existing callers. The rollout flag is located in <code>ReplicatedStorage.Shared.Config.RuntimeFlags.UseCentralNpcAnimation</code> and must remain <code>true</code> in the current draft. Five exact-source groups have been removed from guarded models: 48 townsfolk <code>Animate</code>, 48 <code>Health</code>, 48 <code>Sound</code>, 48 staff <code>Animate</code> and 9 tow-driver <code>Animate</code> — a total of 201. Gameplay-consumers Health/Sound were not found; <code>TakeDamage</code>/<code>BreakJoints</code> are absent, so replacement was not created. After the Edit audit: <strong> 1,604 authored containers / 186 unique Source / 1,418 exact duplicate copies </strong>, <code>Animate=Health=Sound=0</code> outside runtime Player Character and zero direct model-local scripts on NPC/staff.

Play test 19 August 2026 on desktop Studio confirmed 15 real R15 central rigs with 1–2 active tracks, computer base+legs overlay, queue drivers and post-cleanup temporary R6 clone with <code>NpcAnimationOwner=Central</code>, one playing track and zero model-local scripts; the clone was destroyed. At the same time, the existing Luau ambiguous-cast blocker in <code>TycoonSystem.DealerVehicleJourneyService</code> was removed, after which <code>NPCService</code> loaded normally. The existing client compiler limit <code>Out of local registers</code> in <code>TycoonServiceClient</code> was eliminated by placing a separate function boundary around the service-slot runtime tail; the exact final Source was compiled through live <code>loadstring</code>, and a re-run Play loaded the client without this error.

The confirmed vehicle phase uses <code>ReplicatedStorage.Shared.Vehicle.RuntimePackage</code> as the only authored chassis package, <code>VehicleProfiles</code> for model-specific differences, <code>ServerScriptService.Systems.Vehicle.VehicleServerController</code> as the server registry and <code>StarterPlayerScripts.Controllers.Vehicle.VehicleController</code> as the only client entrypoint. A full runtime vehicle is detected only by combining tag <code>ManagedVehicle</code>, <code>VehicleRuntimeActive=true</code>, ancestry in <code>Workspace</code> and actual <code>DriverSeat</code>/<code>VehicleSeat</code>; lightweight <code>ReplicatedStorage.TrafficCars</code> is not included in this runtime. <code>TemplateId</code>/<code>VehicleProfileId</code> define the profile. Each attach creates a separate worker outside the Model and links it to the vehicle via <code>ObjectValue Car</code>; cleanup destroys the worker and its per-car mutable state. The general package in ReplicatedStorage is always inert: executable entrypoints are marked <code>CentralRuntimeEntryPoint</code> and are only included in bound workers. This is the current verified bridge contract; one general passive-FX scheduler has not yet been extracted from the package and remains known as a performance boundary after traffic phase.

Spawn boundaries <code>CarSpawning.spawnCar</code>, <code>TycoonSystem.JunkLot.CarVisuals.spawn</code> and <code>TycoonSystem.DebugService.spawnDebugCar</code> set tag/profile/active contract only for full rigs. <code>IncomingSellerQueue.sanitize</code> transitions the vehicle to <code>VehicleRuntimeActive=false</code> before parenting/freeze, and <code>restoreDriveRig</code> re-enters the runtime; legacy enable/disable embedded scripts have been removed. Profiles retain Bavora/Fierro/Mercurion/Porschka light followers, Zentauri brake follower, <code>DriverAvatarScale=0.92</code> for Fierro/Mercurion/Porschka/Zentauri, plate variants and mandatory check <code>DealerNitroDisabled</code>. Physical <code>Remotes</code>, <code>Animations</code>, <code>Inputs</code>, <code>Lights</code>, <code>Plates</code> and constraints remain with the rig; GUI templates have been moved to the central package.

Mobile vehicle input must receive an exact bound car through <code>require(RuntimePackage.Context)</code> within <code>Client.Input.Touch</code>. A touch controller is selected based on <code>UserInputService.PreferredInput == Touch</code> with a stable <code>DeviceClass=mobile</code> fallback, and form-factor reporting does not degrade due to a synthetic mouse event after spawn. <code>VehicleTouchGui</code> creates direct <code>InputBegan</code> and independent <code>MouseButton1Down/Up</code>/<code>Activated</code> paths for Wheel/Gas/Brake/Exit; the global hit-test remains only as a simulator fallback. Nitro visibility must be called with a bound car and a safe function fallback. If <code>car</code> is missing, the GUI appears in time, but the module crashes during wiring of all controls — this is an emergency regression signature: the vehicle does not move, steer, or exit on touch simultaneously.

Guarded manifest - deletion by exact path/class/Source destroyed <strong>1,446</strong> embedded LuaSourceContainer from 29 full-rig roots: 49 in <code>ReplicatedStorage.Car</code>, 647 in 13 <code>ServerStorage.CarModels</code>, 201 in four archived test-drive rigs and 549 in 11 editor neon references. All 29 <code>Scripts</code> folders were removed; model-local vehicle scripts = 0. After NPC+vehicle phase live Edit audit, excluding rollback checkpoint: <strong>213 authored / 180 unique Source / 33 duplicate copies in three groups</strong>. The subsequent centralization automatic doors guarded-deletion removed the last 36 duplicate instances. Final post-checkpoint Edit recount: <strong>179 LuaSourceContainer / 179 unique Source / 0 exact duplicate instances and 0 duplicate groups</strong>, classes <code>33 LocalScript + 25 Script + 121 ModuleScript</code>; all 179 Sources are compiled. Legacy <code>Animate/Health/Sound</code> and three embedded door-controller names are zero. Individual counts: 170 Humanoid rigs — 0 model-local scripts; known full-vehicle roots — 0; descendants of all 36 <code>ManagedAutomaticDoor</code> — 0.

Desktop Play on 19 August 2026 tested all 13 full vehicle profiles — 11 catalog cars plus the utility <code>NormalCar</code>/<code>TowTruck</code>: server/client attachment, shared light state, optional components, four driver-scale profiles, and plate rendering on 11 rigs with plate geometry; NisshoSlideSX and ZentauriR have no plate-named geometry and are recorded as asset exceptions. An actual post-delete generic spawn confirmed zero model-local scripts, enter/exit, ownership, Controls/Speedometer, Scriptable→Custom camera restoration, chassis movement, recovery below −80 Y, and <code>DealerNitroDisabled</code>. Disabling runtime cleared server/client workers for 14/14 test vehicles; temporary Play clones were removed when Play stopped. No new vehicle errors occurred. The orphan emit <code>OwnerServiceExpense</code> was removed from <code>DealerCar</code> after a live consumer audit: canonical <code>ServiceCompleted</code> is already emitted by <code>NPCService</code>, while the 17th event had neither a consumer nor an analytics/progression mapping. Source compiled, and repeated Play sessions produced no <code>Unknown gameplay event</code>; exact low-cash owner-stage settlement remains part of final gameplay acceptance.

Default plate presentation uses existing plate-named BaseParts as the sole physical geometry. <code>RuntimePackage.Server.Plates</code> adds only <code>SurfaceGui</code>-ink: the <code>PlateStyles.Default</code> background transparency is 1, the font is <code>GothamBold</code>, and a light white stroke has transparency 0.75. The template TextLabel occupies <code>X=0.23…0.96</code> and <code>Y=0.10…0.90</code>; the left 23%-section is not used for text and is reserved for future Roblox logo. It is not possible to return a full-size white Frame over the authored mesh.

Traffic runtime now consists of <code>ReplicatedStorage.Shared.Traffic.RouteCache</code> and a single <code>StarterPlayerScripts.Controllers.Traffic.TrafficClient</code>; rollout flag <code>UseOptimizedTrafficClient=true</code>, legacy owner has been removed. RouteCache lazily builds immutable cumulative distances, sorted junction gates, and corner-speed profile; each vehicle has its own segment/gate cursors. Heartbeat performs fixed simulation High/Medium/Low = <code>30/20/15 Hz</code> with caps <code>14/10/6</code>, <code>carsById</code>, 96-stud spatial buckets, sticky junction ownership, and a maximum of three catch-up steps. PreRender only interpolates; near chassis is updated every frame, medium up to 30 Hz, far up to 10 Hz. Authored body parts, dynamic wheels, and the single anchored <code>HumanoidRootPart</code> of each constraint-driver are collected into one <code>Workspace:BulkMoveTo</code>; fallback <code>Model:PivotTo</code> remains for future incompatible template. Adaptive tier takes into account both EMA and rolling p95 of own cost: it decreases after 3 s over-budget and increases after 10 s when both metrics are below 80% of budget. After population change, the controller waits 3 s for a stable count and resets the p95-window before downgrade decision to prevent immediate Low↔Medium oscillation from route-build/spawn spikes.

For <code>Bavora</code>, the same spawn boundary triggers <code>VehiclePaint.applyFactory</code> with a sequential per-template variant key, so the first three spawns receive three different factory colors and the cycle repeats. Its authored <code>TrafficHeightOffset=0.30</code> is applied before the first <code>PivotTo</code>; at <code>WheelRadius=1.05</code> verified tyre clearance on Asphalt equals approximately <code>0.045 stud</code>.

Owner/customer service cars have a separate client interest-management contract calculated for server cap 8 players. A player's tycoon simulation is always active independently of distance, because its pipeline and arrival acknowledgement must continue to function. A foreign tycoon is activated when distance ≤800 studs and deactivated only after exiting beyond 950 studs; the range 800–950 is hysteresis. Upon deactivation, local proxy/controllers are destroyed, and upon return they are restored from current server attributes and server time. The server publishes <code>ServiceVisualOrigin</code>, so the decision does not depend on stream-in <code>OwnerIndicator</code>. Decorative traffic is unaffected by this rule: it already has its own caps, fixed-rate simulation, and adaptive LOD. This is a live mitigation based on the number of client-side simulations, not proof of no lag: full eight-client performance acceptance after expansion to three helpers in the log has not been verified.

Desktop Play confirmed one runtime owner, 14-car High population, movement, pooling/respawn after <code>CharacterRemoving</code>, unlock-filter, independent route cursors, nested drivers, and zero model-local scripts. Relative body/driver poses after batch render were preserved with max positional drift <code>0.000126</code> stud and max body look-dot error <code>4.77e-7</code>; all 13 TrafficCars have authored <code>Root</code> PrimaryPart, four wheels, and only anchored visual parts. Device Simulator after mobile smoke has been returned to <code>default</code>. Acceptance budgets remain p95 ≤<code>1.7 ms</code> desktop and ≤<code>3.3 ms</code> mobile; a comparable final ≥50% profiler capture has not yet been declared.

Automatic doors belong to one <code>ServerScriptService.Systems.World.AutomaticDoorController</code> under flag <code>UseCentralAutomaticDoors=true</code>. Tag <code>ManagedAutomaticDoor</code> is set on 36 parent Folder: 32 live doors and four ServerStorage templates. Attribute <code>AutomaticDoorMotionMode</code> equals <code>HorizontalAwayFromHandle</code> for 27 doors and <code>ModelLocalNegativeX</code> for nine; existing distance/radius remain <code>4.75/8</code>, <code>8.15/10</code>, or <code>3.75/8</code>. One 0.15-second scheduler manages Tween 0.35 s, tag/ancestry lifecycle, and template-clone attach/cleanup. Guarded-removed 18 <code>CarWashAutomaticDoorController</code>, nine <code>AdminRearDoorController</code>, and nine <code>TuningAtelierDoorController</code>. Play verified 32 active/36 observed, four open/close formulas, exact radius boundary, <code>Built=false</code>, and attach/detach/re-attach of all four scriptless template clones.

After central runtime stabilization, a limited Explorer reorg was performed without changing Source: client entrypoints remain in <code>Controllers/Traffic</code> and <code>Controllers/Vehicle</code>; <code>CarSpawning</code> has been moved to <code>Systems.Vehicle</code>, <code>NPCService</code> — to <code>Systems.NPC</code>, and <code>Collision</code>, <code>WeatherService</code>, <code>RadioService</code>, <code>RoadGripService</code>, and <code>HideEditorOnlyQueueSlots</code> — to <code>Systems.World</code>. <code>TycoonBootstrap</code> remains the root bootstrap. <code>TycoonSystem</code> and other legacy client entrypoints were intentionally not moved: live grep found 13 absolute TycoonSystem references and associated platform <code>PlayerModule</code>/<code>PlayerScriptsLoader</code> packages; a large-scale reorg requires a separate rollout and is not included in duplicate-removal acceptance.

The final fresh Play after checkpoint removal confirmed five enabled flags on client and server: <code>UseOptimizedTrafficClient</code>, <code>UseCentralNpcAnimation</code>, <code>UseCentralVehicleClient</code>, <code>UseCentralVehicleServer</code>, <code>UseCentralAutomaticDoors</code>. The client created exactly one traffic owner with 14-car High population; the server saw 32 active / 36 observed automatic doors, and new routes <code>Controllers.Traffic</code>, <code>Controllers.Vehicle</code>, <code>Systems.NPC.NPCService</code>, and <code>Systems.Vehicle.CarSpawning</code> loaded without errors. Play was stopped, and the repeated Edit recount matched 179/179/0, the internal checkpoint is absent.

### Bootstrap

<code>ServerScriptService.TycoonBootstrap</code> triggers <code>init()</code> strictly in that order:

1. GameplayEvents
2. DataService
3. FunnelAnalytics
4. TycoonService
5. ProgressionService
6. PadService
7. StationService
8. SalesmanService
9. StaffInspectionService
10. JunkLotService
11. DebugService
12. AdminChatService

Self-starting Scripts <code>Systems.NPC.NPCService</code>, <code>Systems.Vehicle.VehicleServerController</code>/<code>CarSpawning</code> and <code>Systems.World.AutomaticDoorController</code>/<code>WeatherService</code>/<code>RadioService</code>/<code>RoadGripService</code>/<code>Collision</code>/<code>HideEditorOnlyQueueSlots</code> start independently by Roblox. <code>ServerScriptService.TycoonSystem.RexVoiceService</code> is also self-starting <code>Script</code>, but it is nested in <code>TycoonSystem</code> and is not a require-able ModuleScript. There is no shared ordering guarantee between these Scripts; new dependencies must use explicit readiness waiting or idempotent initialization.

### Player lifecycle and restore boundary

~~~mermaid
flowchart LR
    A["DataService.load"] --> B["leaderstats.Cash"]
    B --> C["claim first free Tycoon1…8"]
    C --> D["OwnerUserId + character teleport"]
    D --> E["tycoonClaimed"]
    E --> F["JunkLot setup"]
    F --> G["free-pad visibility + staff"]
    G --> H["restore incoming, W1/W2, P1…P6"]
    H --> I["sync transport/automation + publish state"]
    I --> J["Gameplay session"]
    J --> K["leave: teardown/release + flush"]
~~~

On join, the profile is loaded synchronously before claim. <code>JunkLotService</code> then restores the incoming queue, working/display stock, staff, and automation and publishes the state. On leave, <code>TycoonService</code> releases the plot, and <code>DataService.release</code> first flushes the profile, then clears the cache/locks. On shutdown, all active profiles are flushed within a bounded deadline. If no free plot is available, the lifecycle terminates after profile/leaderstats and a warning is issued.

### Server module map

| Path after <code>ServerScriptService</code> | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| <code>TycoonSystem.DataService</code> | profile, sanitize/migrations, cache, save, critical transactions |
| <code>TycoonSystem.PersonalGarageService</code> | permanent collection, server-authoritative market/sale/active car, physical garage terminal, Body Paint/Window Tint/Neon/Nitro purchases, critical rollback and visual reapply |
| <code>TycoonSystem.OwnerCarPipeline</code> | persisted plan, stage order/chances, leases, completed services, condition and value of product in dealer stock |
| <code>TycoonSystem.TycoonService</code> | eight plots, ownership, Cash API, character placement |
| <code>TycoonSystem.PadService</code> | locked/affordability visibility, recommended pad, touch/buy, exact shortfall, effect payload, reveal/build |
| <code>TycoonSystem.ProgressionService</code> | server-authoritative chapter/objective DTO |
| <code>TycoonSystem.StationService</code> | Parts Sales Laptop and job-driven station ledgers/payout, including idempotent Tow delivery |
| <code>TycoonSystem.GameplayEvents</code> | server-only gameplay event bus |
| <code>TycoonSystem.FunnelAnalytics</code> | AnalyticsService custom events |
| <code>TycoonSystem.TradeContract</code> | server-only boundary for buyer offer result |
| <code>TycoonSystem.SalesmanService</code> | staff appearance/friend avatars |
| <code>TycoonSystem.StaffInspectionService</code> | protected world inspection staff |
| <code>TycoonSystem.BuildAnimator</code> | prime/play/reset building constructions |
| <code>TycoonSystem.BayStaff</code> | movement of Repair Bay staff |
| <code>TycoonSystem.DebugService</code> | owner-only debug API |
| <code>TycoonSystem.AdminAccess</code> | explicit immutable UserId allowlist for economy-changing admin paths |
| <code>TycoonSystem.AdminChatService</code> | admin-only <code>/addmoney</code>/<code>/releasecar</code>, feedback remote and owner-pipeline release event |
| <code>TycoonSystem.RexVoiceService</code> | self-starting Script: 9 tutorial cues, contextual rejoin/session dedupe and normal pad reactions after tutorial |
| <code>TycoonSystem.JunkLot.JunkLotService</code> | orchestration, restore, remote validation |
| <code>...JunkLot.DealerCar</code> | authoritative W/P/<code>processingCars</code> state, one-Rusty tutorial/fixed value, Engine→Wheels order, atomic owner-pipeline handoff, physical gate→P display arrival, safe player egress, listing gate and value breakdown |
| <code>...JunkLot.TradeService</code> | seller/fence buy, listing, buyer offer, sale |
| <code>...JunkLot.ListingTransport</code> | physical W → P transfer |
| <code>...JunkLot.IncomingSellerQueue</code> | FIFO presentation queue, capacity 3; weighted normal arrival and atomic tow delivery, exclusive tow reservation, exact cargo/seller handoff |
| <code>...JunkLot.TowTruckService</code> | server-only cycle R15 driver/truck: authored P1–P5 forward depart → fetch → dense long-road return → exact seller/FIFO delivery + payout → reverse home/cooldown |
| <code>...JunkLot.LotAutomation</code> | mechanic/salesman automation |
| <code>...JunkLot.StaffDirector</code> | staff movement/actions |
| <code>...JunkLot.LotSlots</code> | W/P/queue/road anchors |
| <code>...JunkLot.RoadTraffic</code> | arrival/departure paths, general Used Cars P1…P6 approach/reverse planner, Tow P1–P5 interpolation and depart/home plans |
| <code>...JunkLot.CarVisuals</code> | models, condition, persisted paint/neon/window tint, prompts, wheels, value/<code>SOLD</code> billboard |
| <code>...JunkLot.LotNpc</code> | NPC spawn/dress/walk/seat/animation |
| <code>...JunkLot.Remotes</code> | creation of runtime RemoteEvents |
| <code>Systems.NPC.NPCService</code> | general Repair/Wash/Tuning phases, stage-aware Owner/Street FIFO, fair dispatch and reward split |

Particularly closely related and significant: <code>TradeService</code> around 2433 lines, <code>NPCService</code> around 1665, <code>DealerCar</code> around 1445, <code>StaffDirector</code> around 1383, <code>ListingTransport</code> around 1176. Do not change their contracts in isolation.

### Shared config/modules

| Module | What constitutes its responsibility zone |
|---|---|
| <code>TycoonConfig</code> | pads, zones, stations, chapters, bundles, multipliers, MVP gates |
| <code>DealerCarConfig</code> | 11-car dealer pool, the only Rusty tutorial transaction, progression arrival tiers/weights, W/P capacities, timings, and automation constants |
| <code>ServiceConfig</code> | service slots, phases, gaps, durations, owner capacity, <code>OWNER_BURST_LIMIT</code>, <code>QUEUE_WAIT_TIMEOUT</code>, <code>OWNER_QUEUE_DRIVE_SPEED</code>, <code>OWNER_REPAIR_DRIVE_IN_MIN/MAX</code>, <code>ACTIVE_SERVICE_STALL_TIMEOUT</code>, and <code>OWNER_TIMEOUT_VALUE_MULTIPLIER</code>; <code>LOT_JOB_CHANCE</code> — legacy/unused |
| <code>CarConditionVisuals</code> | shared body/wheel dirt shells and oil-leak VFX for full and TrafficCars models |
| <code>PersonalGarageCustomizationConfig</code> | whitelisted body/tint catalogs, exact colors, tint alpha and server-authoritative prices for permanent personal cars |
| <code>VehiclePaint</code> | common body classifier for dealer Paint and Personal Garage; idempotent hue from captured authored color, <code>SurfaceAppearance.Color</code> support and factory restore |
| <code>VehicleWindowTint</code> | common cabin <code>Glass/window/windshield</code> classifier: legacy Body Kit progress/final and explicit personal alpha+color; preserves original Color/Transparency/Material and supports exact factory restore |
| <code>ReplicatedStorage.UI.OwnedCarIndicator</code> | a single client-only inspection card and world-model click routing for dealer stock and Street/customer service cars. Owned proxy keyed by authoritative <code>tradeId</code>; customer proxy keyed by <code>customer:ServiceJobId</code> and stores service kind/state/problems/expected station payout. Service world badges maintain the current second line by phase: ARRIVING / SERVICE IN PROGRESS / WASHED / REPAIRED - LEAVING and similar, not a static station name; their presentation collapses <code>WorldHoverClient</code>. Customer card displays service payout instead of sale value. Click target stores an exact payload snapshot and accepts desktop mouse release or mobile <code>TouchTapInWorld</code> directly on the vehicle body via raycast that passes through ramps, lifts, wipers until reaching the car; local ClickDetector instances are removed, so camera-drag release does not open the card. The card is strictly non-modal: close is not selectable, open/close clears <code>GuiService.SelectedObject</code>, so WASD remains always available to the character controller |
| <code>TownsfolkConfig</code> | NPC identities/classes/behaviour and ask-more data |
| <code>RoadGraph / RoadGraphData</code> | roads: 312 nodes, 352 edges, entrances, tunnels |
| <code>NPCAvatars / NPCSeatFit</code> | avatar pool and boarding |
| <code>WeatherConfig / RadioConfig / RexVoiceConfig</code> | environment/audio/VO |
| <code>NumberFormat / OfferReadout</code> | presentation helpers |
| <code>BuildAnimConfig / PropSpinConfig / JuiceAnimations</code> | construction/visual motion |
| <code>ReplicatedStorage.UI.*</code> | UIRegistry, UIButton, UIVisibility, UIMotion, NewPurchase, tween/effects |

Always look not only for the field in config, but also for the actual places where it is read. Several economic fields are currently dormant.

### Independent server services

- <code>Systems.NPC.NPCService</code> — authority for service jobs; client only visualizes attributes/state.
- <code>Systems.World.WeatherService</code> — weather state and VFX.
- <code>Systems.World.RadioService</code> — server-wide station timelines and per-car controls.
- <code>Systems.World.RoadGripService</code> — friction of drivable rigs in rain.
- <code>Systems.World.Collision</code> — collision groups Car/Character.
- <code>Systems.Vehicle.CarSpawning</code> — kiosk/tagged source → clone <code>ReplicatedStorage.Car</code>.
- <code>Systems.World.HideEditorOnlyQueueSlots</code> — removes editor-only markers at runtime.

---

## 9. Client, UI and presentation [LIVE]

All active controllers are located in <code>StarterPlayer.StarterPlayerScripts</code>. There is no main business logic inside ScreenGui.

<code>WorldHoverClient</code> reduces constant world-UI noise only on the local owner's plot. Authored <code>MachineTag</code> for purchased equipment is hidden by default. Desktop uses mouse hover; mobile client, determined by startup touch capability and server-echoed <code>DeviceClass=mobile</code>, never reads synthetic <code>Mouse.Target</code> and only reveals the card after a precise <code>TouchTapInWorld</code>. Mobile tap selects exactly one card for five seconds and discards the previous one, so camera swipe cannot accumulate Tire Mount/Car Jack/Wheel Balancer/Air Compressor simultaneously. Moving service cars use a constant compact marker: green for <code>OwnerServiceBadge</code>, blue for <code>CustomerServiceBadge</code>; owner dealer cars on Junk Spot/Used Cars use amber <code>DealerValueBillboard</code> marker. Incoming <code>QueueStatus</code> remains standard. For repair-state expanded dealer card, there is a unified canvas 310×146, and compact marker 24×24 hides both server labels and the client-added progress-root.

### Core gameplay clients

| LocalScript | Role |
|---|---|
| <code>DealerCarClient</code> | seller/fence UI, buy/offer actions, cache <code>CarState</code> and the card of their own vehicle's state; player-driven listing is not a live path |
| <code>ObjectiveClient</code> | objective, guide, target |
| <code>HudClient</code> | Cash, next purchase, BuyPad |
| <code>CustomerCardClient</code> | seller/buyer detail card |
| <code>ListingNoticeClient</code> | notices, guide, park assist |
| <code>MoneyStationClient</code> | station billboard/click, Parts Sales Laptop upgrade and stage-income progress capsule |
| <code>TycoonServiceClient</code> | service slots/queues visualisation and registration of owner-car proxy by <code>tradeId</code> |
| <code>PadHologramClient</code> | preview of the next build; the only useful economic effect is added as a large yellow line in the existing authored pad billboard, original bold title/price are preserved, category/status and separate world-card are prohibited |
| <code>BuildAnimationClient</code> | local animation <code>PadPurchased</code>, grey unaffordable upper surface and only post-purchase rising/fading effect receipt in CashPopup-style FredokaOne/green/black outline without background; persistent effect labels are removed |
| <code>RepairAnimationClient</code> | player repair animation |
| <code>CarVfxClient</code> | repair/sale VFX |
| <code>FundsFeedbackClient</code> | insufficient-funds feedback |
| <code>StaffInspectionClient</code> | staff card/highlight |
| <code>SellCarsHUD.Client</code> | Workshop HUD shell, Car Index, Settings, top Cash presentation, left rail and visual integration of legacy <code>HUD.Powers</code> |
| <code>OfflineEarningsClient</code> | Workshop-styled welcome-back acknowledgement over already credited server Cash |
| <code>OwnerDebugPanelClient</code> | F8 owner/debug panel |
| <code>PersonalGarageClient</code> | Phone Garage/market, active/sale/return/summon and owner-only physical garage surface |
| <code>PersonalGarageCustomizeClient</code> | right-side local preview/camera and Apply UI for Body Paint, Window Tint, Neon and Nitro; prices/ownership/catalogs are read from server state |

Each successful construction purchase — touch of a physical pad or HUD <code>Buy Next</code> — must trigger the same Junk Spot build-VFX: a short local <code>BuildPurchaseSpark</code> with <code>Trail</code> flies along an arc from the exact purchased pad to the center of the actually opened structure, simultaneously with the departure animation of the pad. The authoritative route is <code>PadService.purchase → PadPurchased { padId, pad, target, title, effectText }</code>: <code>target</code> server computes only after a successful financial transaction as the center from the outermost model roots of <code>definition.models</code>, and <code>effectText</code> is taken from explicit config or truthful kind/bonus fallback. The indirect client watcher attribute of <code>Built</code> remains only as a compatibility fallback; per-pad debounce prevents double spark in case of different replication order. The contract applies to all active definitions and all eight plots, not just Junk Spot.

Fresh isolated pad QA on <code>CashRegister</code> with Cash=$50 confirmed the grey upper surface <code>RGB(115,119,124)</code>, unchanged black base and exact shortfall; the pad remained interactive. Starting from 2.35 the constant effect label is removed: after purchase, the local popup uses the same effect text and does not create a second server transaction.

Money-station UI retains one authored cyan pill container <code>ReplicatedStorage.Templates.MoneyGUI.Gui.Upgrade</code>, but uses two modes. On Junk Spot <code>QUICK FIX JOB</code> remains the same clickable Upgrade: level, price, next payout increase, affordability and <code>MAX</code> work through <code>StationUpgrade</code>. Starting from <code>REPAIR STATION</code> the same container is enlarged and transformed into a non-interactive stage-progress element: a dark pill serves as track, and cyan fill has exact width <code>clamp(StagePadsPurchased / StagePadsTotal, 0, 1)</code>. On top of it, the current accumulated income increase <code>+$…</code> is prominently displayed, on top left — <code>purchased / total pads</code>, on top right — <code>MAX</code> only upon full completion. All lower amounts, including plus, dollar sign and digits, use a unified monetary <code>FredokaOne</code> without nested RichText font override. The server-side <code>StationService</code> publishes on host attributes <code>StageTitle</code>, <code>StagePadsPurchased</code>, <code>StagePadsTotal</code> and <code>StageIncomeBonus</code>; the last one equals the actual server payout increase relative to <code>basePayout</code> and is never the purchase cost. For Tuning it includes profile income pads, shared racks through <code>bonusZones</code> and purchased Operations Manager <code>×1.25</code>, so the capsule matches the actually issued amount. <code>StationService</code> refreshes on every pad purchase, so count, fill and income update without a new remote.

### Ambient and platform clients

- <code>UIBootstrap</code>, <code>OwnerIndicatorClient</code>;
- <code>TrafficClient</code>;
- <code>MusicController</code>, <code>RexVoiceClient</code>;
- <code>EnvironmentClient</code>, <code>WetRoadClient</code>;
- <code>VehicleHeadlightClient</code>, <code>VehicleEntryLogo</code>;
- <code>PropSpinClient</code>;
- <code>SprintController</code>;
- <code>MVPDeviceReporter</code>.

### StarterGui surfaces

In <code>StarterGui</code> there is exactly one root <code>SellCarsHUD</code>, and its class is <code>ScreenGui</code>. The previous sibling <code>Folder</code> with the same name has been removed; <code>EditorPreview</code> remains a child of the single HUD. The other surfaces: <code>HUD</code> (including legacy <code>CarIndexButton</code> / <code>CarIndex</code> / <code>CarUnlocked</code> and active <code>Powers</code>), <code>Phone</code>, <code>DealerCarUI</code>, <code>CustomerUI</code>, <code>RexRedlineSubtitleGui</code>, <code>SellCarsRadioGui</code>, <code>SpeedLines</code>, <code>SuperTexture</code>.

### Workshop UI contract [LIVE draft, HUD 2.7.1]

**2.49 override:** the description of the expanding <code>Bottom purchase control</code> below is a snapshot HUD 2.7.1 and no longer describes the active lower-right control. Runtime <code>HUD.NextPurchase</code> is always hidden, <code>HUD.Powers</code> has been profiled into a compact button <code>?</code>, and all old power/fan children are hidden and marked unavailable.

<code>StarterGui.SellCarsHUD</code> — active <code>ScreenGui</code> with <code>DisplayOrder=300</code>, <code>IgnoreGuiInset=true</code>, <code>ZIndexBehavior=Global</code> and version attribute <code>WorkshopHUDVersion=2.7.1</code>. The visual language is unified: black/deep panels <code>#07080A/#0D0E11/#1C1D21</code>, metal <code>#43464D</code>, warm white <code>#FFF8E2</code>, hazard yellow <code>#F8CC18</code>, action red <code>#FF3C2F</code>, cash green <code>#48FF35</code>; pure gradients without diagonal stripes are used. Desktop composition is authored on canvas 1920×1080 and scaled via <code>UIScale</code>; full-screen input blockers are located at the viewport root, not inside the fitted canvas, to prevent letterbox areas from remaining clickable.

In Edit DataModel <code>SellCarsHUD.EditorPreview</code> the current top/left/bottom Workshop shell is statically displayed instead of the misleading legacy overlay. Authored <code>HUD.Balance</code>, <code>HUD.Powers</code> and <code>CustomerUI.DealPanel</code> are hidden in Edit. This is not a second runtime HUD: upon startup <code>SellCarsHUD.Client</code> removes all sibling preview objects before building the actual interactive tree; then it manages the visibility of Powers and deal cards through their existing controllers.

- **Top HUD.** At the center there is a separate gear/menu icon without a plate and a single cash panel with a yellow lower inset. Cash uses a bold <code>FredokaOne</code>; the legacy <code>HUD.Balance</code> is hidden, so there should be no second counter.
- **Money typography.** <code>FredokaOne Regular Normal</code> is the single client presentation font for any money amount: Cash/Balance, pad prices, ask/value/offer, payouts/rewards, offline earnings, Robux price and world BillboardGui/SurfaceGui. <code>MoneyTypographyClient</code> applies a contract to existing and late-created <code>TextLabel</code>/<code>TextButton</code>/<code>TextBox</code> in <code>PlayerGui</code> and <code>workspace</code>; the amount is recognized by the symbol <code>$</code>, semantic name, or explicit attribute <code>MoneyTypography=true</code>. A point opt-out is only permitted via attribute <code>MoneyTypography=false</code>. Color, size, outline, alignment, and all other typographic hierarchy remain unchanged.
- **Left rail / Phone apps.** The visible center axis runs near the left edge; icon buttons have no plates. <code>CAR INDEX</code> is always active, after the tutorial <code>PHONE</code> becomes the second visible slot immediately below it; there is no separate Garage icon on the rail, Home is hidden. In Phone desktop, the first tile is <code>GARAGE</code> with an approved Workshop garage asset <code>77153690525766</code>, the second is <code>BUY CARS</code>. Garage opens the currently parked ride, Buy Cars opens the personal market. Label tiles on the rail are enabled via setting <code>ShowIconLabels</code> and are disabled by default.
- **Car Index.** Opening creates a light blur and a small FOV zoom, blocks world input, and shows the Workshop window without a black dimmer. Closing is via X, backdrop, Escape/ButtonB. The list updates the selected vehicle, class accent, Ask/Value, and three stat bars; there is no vehicle-select CTA.
- **Settings.** Uses the same modal focus/input contract. Currently contains <code>ICON LABELS</code> and a session-local music toggle. Music is managed by <code>MusicController</code> via <code>SellCarsMusicEnabled</code>/<code>SellCarsMusicDucked</code> and a global <code>SoundService.SellCarsMusicMix</code>; radio/world/ambient timelines are not stopped but locally ducked by group volume.
- **Bottom purchase control.** This is a visual skin of the real <code>HUD.Powers.Toggle</code>/<code>BuyNext</code>, not <code>HUD.NextPurchase</code>. Therefore, the text, price, sequence of all available purchase pads, and <code>TycoonRemotes.BuyPad</code> remain under the old server-authoritative logic. Legacy circles are hidden; Down and Buy use new image assets. When the control is expanded, Down rotates counterclockwise by 180° and stays up while the fan is open; when closed, it returns to down. Before <code>TutorialComplete=true</code> the control is hidden, then Back/bubble scale appears. In HUD 2.7.1, seating any seat triggers a scale-down to 0, exit triggers bubble scale back; <code>SeatPart</code>, <code>Humanoid.Seated</code> and a backup check occur every 0.1 seconds. The last seat-aware patch has been compiled and tested in Edit, but its physical runtime transition to Used Cars after version 2.7.1 still needs manual confirmation.
- **Objective/quest.** Data and world guide still belong to <code>ObjectiveClient</code>. The card now has a Workshop black/metal/red/yellow presentation. Modal suppression is handled by a separate <code>MVPObjectiveSuppressedByModal</code> and should not conflict with notice suppression.

### Guidance, objective and deal presentation [LIVE IN OPEN STUDIO — 2026-08-27]

- <code>ObjectiveClient</code> is the sole owner of <code>PlayerGui.ObjectiveWorldTarget</code>, and <code>PadPathGuideClient</code> is the only renderer ground Beam. The fallback to a separate pad target has been removed. Runtime toggle shows exactly one target/Beam; when OFF, the Beam remains only as a disabled object and is not rendered.
- The large yellow Down-button in the bottom-right corner is replaced with a compact button <code>?</code>. During tutorial <code>SellCarsGuidanceEnabled=true</code> it is enforced and cannot be disabled. After the tutorial session starts with OFF; <code>?</code> toggles the Beam/target and switches stroke yellow↔green. Persistence in DataStore is not used. <code>HUD.NextPurchase.Visible=false</code> has been confirmed in Play.
- The objective-card does not show <code>CHAPTER N</code>. On the phone top it matches the CashPanel with an error ≤1 px; collapse makes the card/background/strokes/title/progress/reward invisible and leaves only the 44×44 input target with a compact <code>+</code>. Guidance does not reset on collapse. The MCP pointer injector did not trigger a real <code>Activated</code>, so interactive click acceptance is marked BLOCKED, despite source/runtime property proof.
- Purchase objective passes a unique canonical <code>PadId</code> (<code>CW_Ceiling</code>, <code>CW_SideDoor</code> and so on), and the client approves it via a physical purchase pad attribute. Duplicate names <code>CeilingBuyPad</code>/<code>SideDoorBuyPad</code> can no longer redirect the Beam to Repair Bay, Tuning Atelier, or to another side of the plot. Cross-plot verification of all Car Wash/Car Wash 2 objectives: <code>208/208 PASS</code> on Tycoon1–8.
- Owner-local customer service UI in collapsed state retains a compact blue locator; the wide private status expands only according to previous hover/proximity rules. Green owner-service points are not returned.
- All active Rex cues are shortened to a maximum of two short sentences/lines, without long hyphens <code>—</code>; one cue explains one action. The canonical handoff string: <code>Manny will park the car. Close the first sale yourself.</code> Listing automation is enabled for FirstSalesman before the tutorial ends, and buyer auto-accept requires <code>tutorial.completed=true</code>, so Manny physically performs Rusty W→P, but the first sale is closed by the player.
- Purchase card uses two main values: <code>BUY FOR $X</code> and green <code>EST. VALUE AFTER REPAIRS $Y</code>. The active client source contains both strings and does not contain <code>CURRENT VALUE</code>.
- Client reminder <code>SELLER IN LINE</code> / <code>N SELLERS IN LINE</code> is completely removed without replacement-toast. Server queue, telemetry, physical NPCs, and deal cards are preserved; runtime UI audit provided 0 seller alerts.
- Sale Spot uses local contextual **perfectly circular** parking marker without a vehicle silhouette, text, or arrows. Texture <code>rbxassetid://136313547846467</code> contains a lime-green ring with yellow segments on a transparent background; SurfaceGui slowly rotates and pulses, Neon host provides glow, and upward ParticleEmitter emits rare sparks. The old primitive target is hidden, and the marker does not alter the physical parking geometry. In 2.50 owner client, the marker is cross-checked with replicated server-authoritative <code>P#ListingTarget</code>: the ring is restored if the join-time RemoteEvent arrives before the start of LocalScript or the target is reached later, and is cleared after the armor disappears. Runtime on real P2/P3 confirmed the presence of the marker and rotation change of approximately <code>145.8° → 152.2°</code> over 0.8 s.

### Deal, phone, inspection and offline surfaces [LIVE draft]

- <code>DealerCarClient</code> restructures the world seller/purchase dialog in Workshop shell, preserving original seller/fence remotes and action whitelist. <code>DealerCarUI</code> uses <code>ZIndexBehavior=Global</code>: dynamic <code>WorkshopBackdrop</code> must remain on <code>ZIndex=2</code>, authored portrait/name/subtitle/message/chips — on <code>ZIndex=3</code>, and BUY, ASK FOR LOWER PRICE/HAGGLE, and NO THANKS — on <code>ZIndex=4+</code>. Otherwise, the backdrop covers the portrait and entire deal UI, leaving only the <code>VIEW CONTACT INFO</code> button and actions visible. <code>DealInputBlocker</code> with <code>Modal=true</code>/<code>InputSink=All</code> closes the entire active Phone/WorldSeller surface: clicking through the dialog cannot open a vehicle, employee, or other world prompt.
- <code>Phone</code> currently retains white chat presentation, but uses <code>ZIndexBehavior=Sibling</code>: the white Screen no longer covers messages/buttons. Body physically ends above the footer, long messages scroll to the last element, and action buttons must not be overlapped by text. An open phone deal publishes <code>MVPDealModal=Phone</code>.
- <code>OwnedCarIndicator</code> — Workshop right-side read-only card without dimming. Preview uses FOV 26 and large front three-quarter framing. Owned value or customer service payout is in a separate outlined block; customer layout has separate narrow/short/wide breakpoints. World raycast must abort when <code>SellCarsWorkshopModalOpen=true</code> or any <code>MVPDealModal</code> is present; touch route is triggered only from <code>TouchTapInWorld</code>, never from raw <code>InputEnded(Touch)</code>.
- <code>StaffInspectionClient</code> builds the Workshop right-side card as a standard <code>Frame</code>, not as a subtree-tinting <code>CanvasGroup</code>; blank clicks inside are absorbed <code>InputSink.All</code>. The card retains server-fed name/role/metric/responsibilities/zones, world highlight, and reserves a strip above the objective-card at a limited height.
- <code>OfflineEarningsClient</code> builds a separate Workshop modal with a rounded inner mask: child full-width layers do not extend into square corners from UICorner. It exists only when server attributes <code>OfflineAutomationUnlocked=true</code> and <code>OfflineEarningsEligible=true</code> are present, i.e., after tutorial + FirstMechanic + persisted time anchor. Opening synchronously applies a uniform Lighting blur <code>0→16</code>, reduces camera FOV by 7°, lifts the card by 18 px, and expands its Back-scale. Roblox post-processing does not blur ScreenGui/BillboardGui, so modal-focus separately snapshots and temporarily disables all gameplay ScreenGui except <code>SellCarsHUD</code>, all world <code>BillboardGui/SurfaceGui</code> and <code>ProximityPromptService</code>; inside SellCarsHUD, only <code>TopHUD</code> with settings/cash remains visible, and Roblox CoreGui is entirely outside PlayerGui. The full-screen InputSink and its visual backdrop are completely transparent: there is no separate bottom tint/veil, so no horizontal seam appears under TopHUD. Clicks outside the Card and <code>COLLECT</code> trigger one idempotent close. Closing or emergency destroy symmetrically restores original Enabled/Visible, prompts, FOV, and removes the blur; <code>COLLECT</code> — warm-white text, payment has already been processed server-side, the button only confirms. Offline modal waits for release of <code>MVPDealModal</code> and <code>SellCarsWorkshopModalOpen</code>.

Critical presentation invariant: <code>UIGradient</code>, placed directly in <code>TextButton</code>, changes not only the background but also the glyph color. Therefore, a red background X or colored background COLLECT/CTA cannot be gradiented directly on the TextButton if the text must remain purely white; the gradient must reside on a separate background child or be absent.

When the UI name of an object changes, verify controller lookup, UIRegistry, and all fallback paths. Do not move financial logic into LocalScript.

Player-owned dealer cars do not use a separate Sims-style diamond or other world indicator.
